# Project status — 2026-10-25

## Implemented

- Rust workspace with neopets-data, neopets-sim, neopets-app, and a local Git repository (no remote).
- Read-only ISO9660 indexing, serial/version and executable-header checks, bounded file/range reads.
- Explicit ordered mod-file resolution with disc fallback and path containment checks.
- Engine-independent simulation boundary. Only a scaffold tick counter; no original gameplay.
- Bevy 0.19.1 status window with 3D/UI features enabled for later viewers.
- **Range-read hardening (groundwork, no spec):** `Disc::stream_range` feeds any file range to a callback in bounded chunks with one reused buffer; whole-tree caps on indexing (65,536 files, 64 MiB of directory data); I/O errors after indexing (e.g. image truncated) report offset and length. `read`/`read_range` share one bounds check.
- **Fingerprint plumbing:** `neopets_data::fingerprint` streams SHA-256 over any disc file (`sha2` 0.10 crate), compares against an expected digest, and parses 64-digit hex. The inspector takes `--sha256 <disc file>` (repeatable). SPEC-0001's boot-program digest is now enforced by the archive index.
- **Fixed-timestep loop (groundwork, no spec):** `neopets_sim::FixedStep` converts host-supplied elapsed nanoseconds into whole ticks with an exact integer remainder (no drift at 60 Hz), caps catch-up per call and counts dropped ticks, and gives an interpolation alpha. `Simulation::run_for` runs the due ticks with one input. The app samples WASD/arrows/left stick into `PlayerInput` (movement only, buttons unmapped), steps the sim, then draws a diagnostic HUD. The 60 Hz rate and 8-tick catch-up cap are placeholders pending R-0007.
- **M2 archive access (SPEC-0001, SPEC-0002):** `neopets_data::archive::ArchiveIndex::from_disc` requires the boot program's SHA-256 to match SPEC-0001, maps the index table's virtual address to a file offset through the ELF program headers (`elf.rs`, public ELF format), reads the 1434 entries, and checks invariants 1–4 (starts at 0, contiguous, covers GAMEFILE.DAT exactly, 76 empty). Resources are read by number with range checks; empty placeholders read as no bytes. `neopets_data::resource` classifies families by first bytes and walks IDM chunk chains (stop on type 0, buffer end, or an implausible header; one-chunk limit for texture packs and 544–575). The inspector gains `--resource <n>`, `--resources` (survey of every resource), and `--coverage` (writes private/asset-coverage.txt; every decoded count is 0 until decoders land).
- **M3 textures (SPEC-0003, partly):** `gsmem.rs` emulates 4 MiB of GS local memory with PSMCT32, PSMT8 and PSMT4 addressing (public GS documentation); `texture.rs` parses type-4 chunks, uploads the pixel block at the segment's base (so front-end absolute addresses work), and decodes indexed textures through 256-colour (CSM1 order) or 16-colour palettes with GS alpha (A × 2, capped). `material.rs` parses shader lists (type 2), reads each group's pass-0 TEX0 from its state block, and resolves texture and palette inside texture chunk k, as SPEC-0003 specifies. Not implemented from SPEC-0003: flipbooks, UV modes, CPU vertex animators, blend/test/filter state, billboards, residency, the pass-1 texture context, nested shader lists in model-set wrappers.
- **Resource browser and texture viewer:** picks a resource number (type digits + Enter, `[`/`]` ±1, PgUp/PgDn ±100, or `--resource N` at start), clamped to the index, and shows its sectors, size, family, and chunk-type chain. Its decoded textures appear on the 3D quad, sized to their aspect ratio; `,` and `.` cycle them. Textures under a shader list are shown once per material pairing; front-end pictures (no shader list) use their single palette. Tone mapping is off so stored colours show unchanged.
- **M4 models (SPEC-0004, partly):** `mesh.rs` walks node trees (group, visibility, distance-cull and leaf nodes; link, bounds and cycle checks) and decodes one-packet leaves: the packet header (one or two GIF tags), the VIF stream (STMASK, UNPACK V3-32/V2-32/V4-8/masked S-8, MSCNT, tag records), layouts A (skinned), B (plain) and C (lit), two-pass UV halves, and the strip rule. `model.rs` finds trees in standalone type-13/12 chunks, type-8 container children, type-3 level trees and type-18 model-set wrappers, and builds indexed triangle lists with each leaf's material: texture decoded at the TEX0 sampling size, CLAMP wrap modes, and vertex colours scaled per the pass-0 program (16 = 1.0 for lit programs, 128 = 1.0 for unlit). Held-item models 544–575 use resource 7's shader list. Not implemented: pass 1 of two-pass materials, blend/alpha-test state (alpha is masked at 0.5), distance and visibility culling, type-29 placed objects and type-20 instances (M6), type-33 shadow blocks, attach points, the 13 CPU-animated water leaves (packet kind 1).
- **World scenes (SPEC-0004 placement layouts; SPEC-0006 loader table):** `world.rs` collects a world pack's level display trees (type 3, world space), placed objects (type 29: inline type-12 tree and column-major matrix at +0xC0) and instanced meshes (type 20: inline tree and 0x50-byte instance matrices), skipping references that do not resolve. `model::build_world` draws them all with the pack's shader list, baking each matrix into the vertices exactly as stored. Story-layer gating (SPEC-0046 Culling) is applied with the new-game world layer mask 0: type-4 nodes with nonzero layer flags are hidden with their subtrees (this removes Meridell's storm vortex). The sky dome is chosen by the area slot from the boot program's area table (`area.rs`, SPEC-0006 tables A and B behind the fingerprint) and its role-1 layer is drawn at the world origin; the role-10 layer needs an atmosphere zone (type 41) and is not drawn. Not implemented: story flags and SetWorldDisplayMask (the mask stays 0), type-3 display-tree slots and visibility selectors, distance culling, foliage (type 16), ground patches of type-5 model sets, characters, dome UV scroll.
- **M5 animation (SPEC-0005, partly):** `lzo.rs` is an LZO1X decoder written from the public format, with bounds checks and exact-size output. `anim.rs` parses skeleton definitions (types 22–24 and the headerless family headers), the flat type-18 bone table, and clip entries: mode-2 channel streams (constant and animated channels, sample scale 0x3A800EAE), mode-0 frames (÷ 4096), and compressed entries expanded to the plain layout; it samples key pairs (sign flip, componentwise blend, no normalising), combines samples into the pose shown last (replace at full weight, else the φ mix), composes main, sub-part and face families (mask and parent rules, skin bone order) and builds skin matrices W × translate(−rest). `neopets_sim::clip::PlaybackRecord` implements clip start (speed R × 0.5 ÷ S, fade-in), the end rule (one-shot hold; loop wrap keeping the overshoot), key pairs, fractions, φ and progress. The app plays a hero outfit (504–519) with its hero's three families (headers, banks and action tables from SPEC-0005's table), stepping the records once per sim tick and skinning layout-A parts on the CPU each frame; `-` and `=` change the action id (lookup misses give clip 0). Not implemented: root motion (travel), the second main layer, mirrored clips, per-bone translation scale, event bits, secondary-motion chains (Tor's tail, Roberta's head chains), region characters' type-36 animation tables, attach points, crossfade between differently-masked clips beyond the spec rule.
- **Timing (SPEC-0007):** the sim tick is the game's fixed 1/60 s step; catch-up is capped at 4 ticks per frame, and `--original-pacing` caps it at 1 so slow frames slow the game as on the PS2.
- **Ground and spawn (SPEC-0018, SPEC-0006):** `ground.rs` reads the level grid (type 19) and the pack's own type-5 height patches, samples a patch bilinearly per SPEC-0018, and runs the ground probe's accept rule (highest height at or below the ceiling, else the last rejected height, else the miss value; 0.0 with no cell). It reads entry record 0 of the type-11 chunk as the world spawn and applies the spawn height rule (probe from y + 8). Not implemented: inline model sets of placed objects (patch ids other than 0), the runtime candidate gates, the outline-record list test, owner placement. The grid's cell order is taken as x-major by measurement (R-0009).
- **Hero locomotion (SPEC-0033, SPEC-0040; M7 groundwork):** `neopets_sim::hero` implements the stick speed value, the speed tiers (walk mode and R1 run mode with the 30-pass restart wait, tier factors as the play rate), the moving facing turn (0.95 floor), the zeroed step while the stick points behind, and travel by the previous tick's root step through the facing basis, projected onto the ground plane while the gap is below 1.0. Physics (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A items 5 to 7, `neopets_sim::motion`): the record tick probes 2.5 above the feet for the gap and the ground normal (SPEC-0018's truncated half-sum normal, `Ground::probe_normal`), applies the root travel, the ground constraint, gravity with the gain G, friction 0.6 on the ground, the contacts and the integrator. Air (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 9A and 50): the walk-off check, the Cross jump chooser (states 7 to 10 by stick magnitude, normal y at least 0.82), the launch, air phases 0 to 7 and 10 with their gains, the air steering, the clip tails 76 to 78 and 70 to 67, and the soft, moving and hard landings. Tests reproduce the spec's standing-jump heights, the state-10 drift distances (2.85 and 14.0), the walk-off drops and the walk-off phase timings. `HeroAnim` follows the rate every tick and returns the main layer's root step (doubled while a finished one-shot is held). `neopets-app --play` puts Tor (outfit 504) at pack 909's spawn with a follow camera: WASD or the left stick move, Shift or the right trigger is R1, Space or the south button is Cross (jump), the follow camera turns with the right stick (J/L and I/K on the keyboard) and R is R3. `--autowalk` holds the stick forward for unattended checks; `--autojump` also presses Cross every 120 ticks; `--play-trace <ticks>` runs play mode without a window and prints position, state, air phase, gap and vertical velocity per tick. Box walls: `ground::rectangles` reads the kind-4 and kind-7 rectangle lists (SPEC-0028 6.1) and `neopets_sim::contact` pushes the feet out of every rectangle that passes the box-push gate (class bit 0 or 3, mask 0, positive B extent) with the hero radius 1.175 (SPEC-0040 body size; the 1.25 radius belongs to the camera's eye pass), using SPEC-0018's grown-box test, six face depths, bottom bias, top gate and push distances. Frame-record walls (before the boxes, SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A branch 0): `Ground::frames_at` reads the 0x70-byte frame records named by the cell's entry items (SPEC-0018) with the field layout of SPEC-0033 Behavior 37 (census in 909: every start point lies on its face and run planes with top-plane value H, so a record is a wall of depth H hanging below its top edge), and `WallEdge::push` applies the two-sided ordinary-wall correction r − |a| of SPEC-0018's Petpet pass, gated by a near-vertical face, a run-plane value in (0, L), and feet less than 3.5 below the wall's bottom. Unverified (R-0011): the hero routine's own gates; the 0.25 step height that keeps low edges from blocking is this build's choice; the model-set entry mask and inline (placed-object) model sets are not applied; the ledge-drop and edge acceptance tests and their state hand-offs are not run. Not implemented: cylinders; the corner and preferred-face rules; reverse walk and the backward jump, strafe jumps, air attacks and the plunge, grabs, ledges and every other hero state; the landing event windows; moving platforms and the alternate friction branch; the floor-contact flag; status factors, the eased stationary turn, the displayed-height lag. The stick is applied relative to the screen; the spec's sign is asked about in R-0010.
- **Follow camera (SPEC-0027 mode 0; M7):** `neopets_sim::camera::FollowCamera` runs first in each play tick from the hero's previous pose: the profile chosen by the ground kind (+0x38) of the patch under the hero (`Ground::probe_hit`; kept when there is none), the right-stick stick value and spin input (converter A, D = B⁴ with the 1.3 switch), the target distance (0.65 sin² e), the focus 3.5 above the feet, the spin integrator, the chase and sticky manual elevation with the recentring gate T, the eye on its sphere, the critically damped spring (k 0.085), ground clearance 2.0 above the eye's ground, the eye wall push (frame records other than type 6 and the boxes at radius 1.25) the line-of-sight timer (recentre on the 61st blocked tick) and the R3 recentre (behind, else in front, shrunk by 0.25 while the line is blocked or the candidate is buried). Line tests use `Ground::segment_clear`, SPEC-0018's segment query over the endpoint cells' frame records with type 6 skipped under the restriction flag; the +1.5 endpoint shift is not applied because the spec leaves its coordinate open. The load attach places the eye 10 behind the hero through the pull-in setter. The Bevy view looks from the eye at the focus with SPEC-0027's projection (50.2° vertical, near 1.0); the hero's stick is relative to the camera's rows. Tests reproduce the spec's polar vector, target distances, settle curve, spin rates, chase and manual steps, manual raise and the pull-in vectors. Not implemented: the script override profile, camera-locked states, hold and lean terms, the spring's contact-normal exception, the failure flag and stuck timer, the camera visibility test, side-swing events, scripted plays, shakes and the fade ramp; the eye uses the characters' two-sided wall correction instead of its single-margin plane test (Medium).
- **Overworld streaming (SPEC-0006 World buffers; M7):** `neopets_data::travel` reads the area-pair table, the no-swap list, the block-pair list and the nine family entry names of the host table (SPEC-0030) from the boot program at run time, classifies each pack by the Init entry its module exports (host records 397, 398 and 399 are the Transition, Exterior and Interior Init names: the `--areas` census gives exactly SPEC-0006's 36, 87 and 50 modules, plus the 15 moduleless transitions the spec lists and 3 moduleless exteriors), and reads the tag-0x0B neighbour list (145 tables, position 0 unused and positions 1..m used in all, 528 world ids, as the spec counts). `Ground::cell_value` gives the exit value of the cell under the hero. Play mode starts as the new game does (909 in E current, 691 in T), runs the pending step each tick (neighbour by cell value, tables B and A, area pairs, `neopets_sim::travel::stream_buffer`), loads a streamed neighbour into the other buffer between ticks, and when the current world has no ground under the hero searches the other buffer (when the neighbour-world test passes; the 92-slot swap set and the 15 moduleless transitions are the spec's stated rule members) and swaps worlds on a hit. `sync_worlds` draws the current world, the other world when the neighbour-world test passes, and the current world's sky dome. `--trace-to swap` walks a searched route to the nearest reachable swap point: from the 909 spawn the hero walks onto 691's ground and 691 becomes current with no height jump. Not implemented: doors and teleport landings, interiors, replacement loads, movie returns, kill memory, zone and sound changes on a swap, and the loader's buffer-state checks (a request is read once to learn the pack's kind, which the original knows from its code rule). Stream loads run on a loader thread with its own disc handle (`Disc::reopen`): it reads the pack, classifies it and builds its scene only when the buffer rule will stream it; the window keeps running meanwhile.
- **Doors (SPEC-0006 Doors, Door prompt and confirm, Hero placement, Interior entry steps; SPEC-0028 section 13):** `ground::teleport_planes` reads the teleport plane list (kind-7 rectangles with kind-mask bit 1 and a class other than 500 and 501, in disc order) and `ground::entry_record` the entry records; `Neighbours::arrivals` reads the tag-0x0E arrival list. `BootTravel::imports` matches a module's import names by function name (SPEC-0030 section 2): the `--areas` census finds 169 teleport planes in 102 packs, and the packs whose module does not import RegisterTeleportCallback hold exactly 45 doors, the spec's count of default doors. Those doors work in play: a touched door posts prompt 13 (Enter, while E is current) or 26 (Exit); the chooser shows it on the next tick and E (Square) or the west button confirms; the door then applies the default rule: E to a transition lands on its door (teleport landing, flat and upright forms, reverse flag from the door normal and the hero's forward), E to a non-paired pack enters it as an interior at entry record 0 snapped from 6 above (T released and remembered, E kept), E to a paired exterior replaces E and arrives at the arrival door, T lands on E's door, and I leaves to E's door (upright, probed) and reloads the remembered T. Loads go through the loader thread. Script doors (59 packs import RegisterTeleportCallback) run their level's handler when one is written (below); others do nothing yet. Unverified: the hero's touch test (R-0012); fades, the camera's return to normal and the five idle loader frames are not reproduced. `--trace-to door` stands the hero before door 0, walks in, presses Square on the prompt and goes on through door 0 of each world it reaches twice more.
- **Level behaviour (SPEC-0030 section 7; per-level specs):** `neopets_sim::level` holds the state the level handlers read and write (story flags, the type-and-slot blackboard, held items), an action vocabulary (StartEnterInterior, StartLeaveInterior, MoveHero to an entry record, LevelMsg), and handlers written from the per-level specs: 909's teleport callbacks 0 and 1 (enter slots 72 and 73, prompt 13) and its activate-world routine (round-up flag reset; blackboard value 10, 30, 40 or 50 for slot 68 places the hero at locator 2, 1, 3 or 4, or 28 at stage 6 without item 36; SPEC-0045), 951's teleport callback (line 126 with state 119 set and 122 clear, otherwise message 10 and leave to slot 5; SPEC-0041) and 955's (message 40 and leave to slot 5; SPEC-0032). The `--areas` census confirms 909's entry records 1 to 4 are the spec's locators 1 to 4. Play mode runs a script door's handler on a Square press edge with the confirm flag set and never clears that flag (SPEC-0006 stale confirm), and runs the activate-world routine whenever a world becomes current. Story flags start clear except 707, which a New Game at start index 0 sets (SPEC-0049; the start-point chooser's stage rows are not applied). LevelMsg shows its line in the play HUD for four seconds: `neopets_data::dialogue` reads the pack's IDMX line table (type 1000; the `--areas` census resolves all 7,547 records of the 131 tables, as SPEC-0022 counts) and strips control codes, a page stop becoming " / ". The dialogue box, its reveal, paging, emotes, speaker overrides and the wait for the box to close are not implemented. `--flag N` sets a story flag at the start for checks. Every other level's handlers, and these levels' triggers, talk, threads, slots, objects, cutscenes and Update, are not written yet.
- **Region characters (SPEC-0006 Character regions, kind-3 placements; SPEC-0005 families; SPEC-0045 909 activation thread):** `neopets_data::region` walks a region's type-36 records (kind-3 placements with name, position, facing, skeleton, table and model-set indices, type, variant and summon byte; kind-2 animation tables) and sorts its chunks into model sets, skeletons (22 to 24) and clip sets (25 to 27). `archive_view::region_character` builds a character from its model set (with the region's materials), the bone table inside that set, and its families: the region's own k-th skeleton and clip set, or a global main set for -7 (header 492 and body 526), -6 (Tor) or -5 (Roberta), each with its kind-2 action table. 909's activation now also returns its slot loads by story stage, and play mode loads those regions on a second loader thread: with no story flags set, region 910 (five Flower Monsters and three unnamed type-109 characters), 914 (Lucy), 920 (Patricia) and 926 (Hubert); the inspector shows Lucy, Patricia and Hubert at the positions SPEC-0045 lists. Characters stand at their placements and play action 0, drawn while their world is drawn. A world's embedded type-32 region is loaded when it becomes current (909's holds no placements; 893's gives Douglas the Drackonack Hunter); characters of worlds that are no longer resident are released. Characters settle on the ground probed 2.5 above their placement (the fall itself is not simulated). Not implemented: streaming by distance and the active cap, AI, turning to face a talker, colour words, effect lists, kill memory and summons. `--look x,z` turns the hero toward a point at the start.
- **Talk (SPEC-0022 Conversations, Proximity talk frame, Line rotation patterns, Interaction range; SPEC-0045 909 proximity routines):** `travel::entity_table` reads the tag-0x12 entity table and characters get their entity id by the reverse rule (region byte and kind-3 ordinal): in 909 entities 31 to 36 are regions 914 to 919 (Lucy), 37 to 42 are 920 to 925 (Patricia) and 43 to 50 are 926 to 933 (Hubert). `neopets_sim::level` has the talk engine (first-match flag cascade, rotation patterns with a per-routine branch marker and counter that reset when the pack loads again) and 909's routines A, B and C with their item and flag side effects, Tor's lines of the x/y pairs; tests reproduce SPEC-0045's routine-A and routine-B vectors. Play mode picks the nearest registered character of the current world within 20 and the facing cone (cosine 0.5); within 5.8 it shows the Talk label, and Square runs the routine: the line shows with the talked character as speaker in a box that stays open until the next Square press, holding the hero in place. Not implemented: the talker and lock-on lists' leader hold and reorder, the talking hero state and turning, Roberta's lines, the real dialogue box (reveal, pages, emotes), and every other level's routines.
- **Townsfolk routine (SPEC-0056; SPEC-0022 talk facing):** `neopets_sim::hero::steer` is the steering blend v = w t + (1 − w) c with the anti-parallel side nudge (test: SPEC-0056's 90-degree turn sequence at w 0.3 and the w 0.1 tick-23 value). In conversation the hero turns toward its partner with weight 0.1 and the partner toward the hero with 0.3. `neopets_sim::npc::Townsfolk` runs the class-6 tick for class-6 characters: the greeting entry pre-step (hero within 6.0 horizontally and in the front half; repeats each tick), mode from the behavior code (placement +0x84), mode 0 (idle 0; code 0 turns back to the placement facing with weight 0.05), the poses 10, 11 and 12 (ids 65, 97, 98) and the greeting 13 (idle for 180 ticks, then id 30 to the end of its clip; exits beyond 12.0 or behind −0.1). The conversation freezes the talked character's routine. Not implemented: wander (5) and patrol (6) stand idle (they need the navigation mesh, its route table and the random stream), the villager pair, script walks, the monster and companion rows that give other characters their class, and enemy AI.
- **Random generator and monster table (SPEC-0008 Random numbers; SPEC-0029 section 4):** `neopets_sim::rng::Rng` is MT19937 with the 1998 69069 seeding and the Range, Index and FRange rules with their no-draw shortcuts; tests reproduce every SPEC-0008 vector (first outputs for seeds 1, 4357 and 860282, the 624/625 boundary, the wrapper sequences and the no-draw checks). Play mode holds one stream, seeded from the local clock (`--seed N` fixes it for traces). `neopets_data::monster::MonsterTable` reads resources 530 and 531 (radius, height, hit points, default class with the companion fallback, hostile, weight, can fly); the `--areas` census gives 259 rows and, over all 3,036 placements, 666 of class 6 with every main -7 placement among them, as SPEC-0029 and SPEC-0056 count. Characters carry their row; the townsfolk routine runs for class 6. Region 910's three unnamed characters are class 7 (the Whinnies) and its Flower Monsters class 17.
- **Navigation and the Whinny round-up (SPEC-0018 Navigation meshes and queries; SPEC-0056 Navigation step, Route step, Route table, Wall confinement, Blocked flag, Travel by root motion; SPEC-0029 Behaviors 2b and 5; SPEC-0049 item 6):** `neopets_data::navmesh` reads type-21 meshes (census: 998 meshes, 848 of kind 1 and 150 of kind 2, 156,675 vertices, 68 one-way links, every route table at least 2T²/8 bytes; pack 722 mesh 2's neighbours and route rows equal SPEC-0056's vector). `neopets_sim::nav` has the inside test, the hinted and no-hint locates, the full locate, the nearest triangle, the route-table read and the route step's crossing point with its clearance clamp and behind test. `neopets_sim::walker` has the navigation step (off-mesh recovery, settled and fresh-block arrival, same or new triangle, route part), the goal latch, the navmesh confinement (boundary-edge reach and push, predicted test, off-mesh restore, blocked flag and timer) and travel by the playing clip's root step with ground projection and the motion-record physics. `neopets_sim::whinny` has the four modes in priority order (penned, lead, flee with its direct-away and safe-point sub-steps, wander), the pen rules and the group count that sets flag 107 + p. 909's Init sets the pen (inner mesh 0, entrance mesh 5, pen point locator 8). Class-7 characters run it each tick on their walker. With `--trace-to herd` the hero keeps behind the nearest loose Whinny: all three are led in and penned, and flags 107, 108, 109 and 110 are set in turn. This build's choices, labelled: a random point on a mesh is a uniformly drawn triangle's centroid; flee sub-step B's safe point is the far-centroid pick at 10 from the hero; sub-step A's path test is read as clear, the blocked latch alone choosing sub-step B (R-0073; superseded the earlier on-mesh reading, see the Whinny pen bullet). The navmesh is taken once at start and set to the entrance mesh on entering lead and to the inner mesh on becoming penned (R-0013 answer). Not implemented: the steering look-ahead's obstacles, the batched goal locate, the occupancy quirk, the group sound, cell list B and the character push for NPCs.
- **Tor's attack step (SPEC-0036 Attack input, Charge completion, Combo windows, Attack router):** `neopets_sim::attack::TorAttack` runs state 13: the hold (request 22, then 13 once 22 has played), release below 50 ticks to the first swing (14) and at 50 or more to the power step (0x1B with no Mote), the combo windows (20 to 33 into the second swing 15, 17 to 30 into the finisher 16, a late press closes the chain), the chained variant 17 with its restart and at most 3 repeats, and the end at progress 0.99 back to the stance. Tests reproduce SPEC-0036's combo, chained and power vectors. In play F, the left mouse button, Circle or R2 attack from the ground states; the step holds the hero's stick. Not implemented: the gesture starts (sub-states 7 to 9), the power-ready check, sounds and Roberta.
- **Hero swing hits and damage (SPEC-0036 Hit detection, Combat reach test, Damage; SPEC-0005 hurt capsules and attach points; SPEC-0021 item table and weapon reach; SPEC-0029 row defence and disappear timer):** `neopets_data::item` reads resource 529 (census: 208 records with the spec's type counts; weapons 98 to 105 and 111 to 118 with stat A, sword 98 at 4). `BoneTable` now reads the hurt capsules (+0x30, +0x40, +0x44) and the nine pose entries (attach points). `neopets_sim::combat` has flat-ended cylinders, the static overlap and segment tests (an approximation: the finite-cylinder test the specs cite is not described; R-0014) and SPEC-0036's swept test (all ten vectors pass; the normal's order is fixed by the c = 0 vector, also in R-0014). While the swing's window bit 0x1 is on, the weapon cylinder (radius 1, length W + 2, from attach point 0 + 0.25 along its Z axis) is tested against every bone capsule of each character of the current world within 65.0, statically and then swept from the previous tick's axis, with a hit list cleared on every sub-state transition. A hit deals SPEC-0036's melee damage without Mote or petpet factors (stat A × the sub-state factor − the row's defence, truncated); townsfolk ignore hits; at 0 hit points a character disappears after its row's death timer. Tor starts unarmed (A = 0), so swings deal nothing until a weapon is equipped; `--give N` equips one for checks and `--start x,z` places the hero. Not implemented: blocking, the hittable predicate's status tests, hit reactions, hit cues and effects, Motes, magic and the posing lag of one pass.
- **Common enemies, class 17, and hits on the hero (SPEC-0038 Behaviors 1 to 8, 13, 18, 23, 34, 36, 37, 40, 44 and 48.6; SPEC-0029 Behavior 10.2 weapon volumes; SPEC-0036 Start values, Taking a hit, Enemy attack damage to a hero, Knockback on the hero, Health and death; SPEC-0033 Behavior 9B):** `neopets_data::monster` reads the class-17 row values (`Tuning`: approach routine, notice band and chances, view angle, awareness, agro bands, the four attack slots with trigger band, arc, chance, delays, charge, damage, knockback, flags and loop words, and the five weapon volumes); the census finds the spec's 109 class-17 rows (approach 1: 38, 3: 37, 4: 34) and 36 packs whose embedded region places ground melee enemies. Placements now carry the emerge distance (+0xC0) and selector word (+0xC8). The boot words give Tor's health 150 and the band factors F1/F2 equal to the spec's table. `neopets_sim::enemy` has the pre-step (awareness countdown, alert flag, the per-state switch), the notice check with the band factors, the setter with its entry work (states 1, 2/21, 8, 10, 19, 22, 23 and the default case), the idle picker, state 8's emerge and hide (Behavior 40), the agro machine's set-up, rush, keep-distance and strafe sub-states with their gait sets, the search after lost sight, the hit reactions (kinds 0, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7), the normal attack choice (slots 4 to 1, strict band, arc, chance with backbiter and anger rules, charge rule), the distance draws, the attack sequencer's melee, charge and loop states with the end path, the agro tail with the leash, the chase stop, the take-hit hook (doubling on entering agro, the reaction kind, facing the attacker, alert, anger) and the dead state (clip 58 once, the disappear countdown from progress 0.95, the 1/180 sink, release). Tests reproduce the spec's band-factor, notice-band, emerge, take-hit doubling, strafe-circle and corpse-sink vectors. In play, ground class-17 enemies get a walker (navigation and root-step travel) and the AI; their strike test is weapon N's cylinder on attach frame N−1 while event bit 2^(N−1) is set, against the hero's bone capsules; a strike deals the slot's damage (no guard, armour, Motes or petpet yet) through the hero's take-hit: state 17 with clip 32 (0xC0 unarmed), Tor's charge sub-states busy, the dialogue box discarding hits, the deduct, death at 0 (state 16, clip 34; no respawn yet) and the knockback into the long fall (state 48, clips 136 and 137). Tor's hits on these enemies go through their take-hit hook. The status line shows the hero's hp and the last hit taken, and the characters line each enemy's state, outer, inner and sequencer state. The headless trace now waits for region loads. Trace (pack 757, `--trace-to fight --give 98 --seed 1`): Dumb Skeletons emerge into agro, rush, strike Tor for 30 five times (hp 150 to 0, state 17 between hits, state 16 at tick 2423) while Tor's sword takes one from 50 to 14 hp; in 909 the Flower Monsters emerge, keep their distance and run their missile slot with no shot. Not implemented: the defences, counter-attacks, alarm groups and socialize, the attack manager and waiting in line, map search, retreat home, wandering in states 5 to 7, state 9, confusion, statuses, theft, grab-and-throw, ghosts, reanimation, drops and coins (flyers: see the flyer entry), the hero's guard and shield block, armour and Motes, sounds, and the health bar. Open readings are in R-0015 (alerted search, back and side gaits, the end of a clip, the sight points, the hold turn speed).
- **Enemy projectiles (SPEC-0029 Behaviors 9 and 13; SPEC-0038 Behavior 13, state 2; SPEC-0005 table B release bit):** `neopets_sim::projectile` has the ten-slot pool (first free slot, a full pool creates nothing, reset when a world is made current), the per-tick update (owner check, area mode with its 60-tick pulse, lifetime, gravity g' = 32/3600 for kinds 1 and 8, the homing blend 0.75/0.25, the sphere-versus-body-capsule hero test, the ground test past 2.0 from the start), and the four spawners (straight, homing, the speed-ramp ballistic launch with its lifetime slip, the lobbed summon). Tests reproduce the Minion Archer arrow (lifetime 35, velocity (0, 0.04998, 1.99938), contact on tick 10, 36 moves and the reset at update 37), the homing velocities, the Flower Monster seed (s 0.30302, vy 0.27820, lifetime 3961), the spawn-count loop and the area pulse timing. The sequencer's state 2 runs the spawn loop on the release bit 0x20 (pending flag, shot counter, spawn count); `neopets_data::monster` reads the missile values and the alignment. In play a spawn event launches from the enemy's attach frame for its emit point, with the +5.0 scatter outside the camera cone; an unblocked hit costs the hero trunc(damage). Live shots are drawn as small plain cubes (a stand-in: the flight effects of SPEC-0034 are not implemented; not yet seen in a window). Trace (909, `--start -1800,925 --trace-to fight --give 98 --seed 1`): the Flower Monsters fire seeds (up to three live) and one hits Tor for 10 on tick 152. Not implemented: effects and trails, spells (kinds 2 to 7), the poison cloud, mote eater and leach, the shield block and reflection, statuses, the lobbed summon's spawn, and the static-mesh, primitive and moving-object world tests (the build tests frame-record walls). Open readings in R-0016.
- **Hero death, deadly ground and game over (SPEC-0055 sections 4, 8 and 8A; SPEC-0036 Health and death; SPEC-0033 4A.6):** `neopets_sim::death` has the death-state handler (clip 34 on ordinary ground, 173/174/136 on deadly ground by surface word; death mode 2 with timer F for a respawn index, mode 1 otherwise or at clip progress 0.95 when no partner takes over), timers E and F with the single-precision 1.5 s rule (they act on the 91st addition), the deadly-ground kill test (kind 2, gap 0.085 or less), the state-60 penalty min(5, health - 1), and the game-over page's pass counter and exit (counter above 120 and Cross newly pressed, so page pass 122 at the earliest). Tests reproduce the spec's timer, respawn, ordinary-ground and page vectors. The ground probe now reports the chosen entry's respawn index (+0x0D) and surface word (+0x40); the census finds 121 deadly entries in 50 packs, 78 with an index (the spec counts 123 and 79: two entries are not among the patches this reader loads). The hero has the death-end states 59 (clip 175) and 60 (clip 137). In play a lethal hit or the deadly-ground kill runs the default death (state 16, hit list cleared; no death callbacks are registered by any level here), the handler runs every tick in state 16, timer F respawns the hero at the entry record in state 60 with the penalty, and timer E sets the game-over flag; the next pass shows the game-over page (a status-line text: the front end's sprites are not drawn) with the world frozen. Its Continue exit with no save or load pending tears the world down and goes to the card state (see Quit teardown). Trace (`NEOPETS_TRACE_HP=30 --pack 757 --play-trace 1500 --trace-to fight --seed 1`): Tor dies at tick 639, the page follows timer E, and the trace's Cross tap on a page pass at or after 122 tears the world down on tick 961 (the new game in 909 at full health, 0 characters until its regions load). Not implemented: Roberta and the partner switch (the switch request is always refused), death callbacks, script kills and revive, the death camera, the death voice and cues, effects, the hurting surface and the deep overlay, the front-end chain and the session snapshot of Continue; deadly ground is unit-tested but not yet traced in a pack.
- **Shield guard and blocks (SPEC-0033 Behavior 4A.3; SPEC-0036 Enemy attack damage to a hero, Guard timer and per-attacker block, Taking a hit; SPEC-0029 Behavior 9 hit on the hero; SPEC-0038 Behavior 13 states 4 and 9):** the hero has state 20: entry from the ground states with L1 (Q or the left bumper) and a shield, clip 63, the turn to the stick at rate 1.0 with clip 119 beyond about 18 degrees, the block reaction 64, the exit to state 1 when L1 is released. A class-17 strike on a guarding hero that faces the attacker (facings' dot at most -0.5) is blocked unless the slot is unblockable (flag 0x01; flag 0x20 forces the block): the shield's stat A is subtracted, the hero plays 64 instead of state 17, and the enemy's sequencer goes to state 4 (blocked) or, for a charge, state 9 with the slot's blocked-charge kind (D to the charge distance, D to its end, the stun reaction kind 2, or kind 3). A shot hitting a guarding hero from the front is blocked: element-0 straight shots (kinds 0 and 10, the listed visual kinds) are sent back at their owner at unchanged speed and hit it, others cost max(1, 0.25 D). Tests cover the blocked strike, the unblockable flag, the shot block and the reflection. `--give` now takes weapons and shields and works in the window too (no inventory yet). Trace (757, `--trace-to fight --give 98 --give 106 --guard --seed 1`): the Dumb Skeletons' slot-0 strike (30 unblocked in the earlier trace) lands as 10 through shield 106 and slot 1 as 20, with clip 64 on the block; Tor holds the guard and so never swings, and dies at tick 2986. Not implemented: the 40-tick threat-sense guard timer, Roberta's spin, the shield Mote and its charge, armour and the armour Mote, the petpet factor, the owner's own block after a reflection, the guard's L2 exit, and the enemies' melee defence (R-0017).
- **World items, drops and pickups (SPEC-0021 World items, Drop and money flow, Pickup dispatch and the luck gate, Pickup timing gate, Pickup, Auto-collect, Coin pickups, Wallet; SPEC-0006 Kind 8; SPEC-0038 Behavior 34 part B):** `neopets_data::item` reads the drop sub-tables and groups after the item records of resource 529 (census: 11 sub-tables and 7 groups, equal to the spec's tables) and each record's pickup distance base; regions read their kind-8 item points (census: 2,454 in the embedded regions, as SPEC-0006 counts) and each placement's drop ordinal (+0xB0); the coin denominations, ids and motion words come from the boot program. `neopets_sim::items` has the 50-slot pool and its spawn steps, the per-tick step (placed items, fade, clock, the hover and bob of plain items, the charm hop, the coin flight, the scale grow), the pickup timing gate and the collect distance, the drop roll (money roll, fixed count, item roll over the group and sub-table weights), the greedy coin split and the luck gate. Tests reproduce the spec's coin flight (apex on tick 25, first contact on tick 52, at rest by tick 160), hovering potion (ready from tick 49, fade from tick 673, freed on tick 701), hopping charm (first contact on tick 54), roll, split, luck, pickup-gate, collect-distance and full-pool vectors. In play each resident world has a pool: region start-up spawns its flag-0/1 points (luck gate with luck 0, the equipment, once-per-area and item-126 vetoes), an enemy's first death runs its drop placement (a direct key, or the drop action's coins or item), the pools tick, and the hero collects the nearest ready item in the current world (coins to the wallet, charms as 100, others as 1; non-coins refused while busy). The status line shows the Neopoints. Items are drawn as small plain markers (a stand-in for the item display models). Trace (757, `--trace-to fight --give 105 --give 106 --seed 1`): two Dumb Skeletons die to 40-damage swings; the first drops item 157, picked up on tick 1125, the second coins, picked up for 1 NP each. 909 starts with items 6, 34 and Mote 164 placed; 757 with a coin trail and two Motes. Not implemented: the item text database (carry limits, important items and their give, the range collect), maps, the shop and flag-1 stock, the petpet reservation, pickup hooks, popups, cues and voices, quick-item selection, breakable drops and scripted adds, the placement-id table, clovers, the stolen-Neopoints split, item display models, and using or equipping picked-up items.
- **Quick-item panel and healing items (SPEC-0021 Quick-item selection marker, Quick-item panel, Consumable healing; SPEC-0036 Start values):** `neopets_sim::quick` has the panel (opening on an Up edge outside action state 0x19 with the refill of entries 2, 1, 3, 0 and selection 1, direction edges with Left first and the same-direction refill, Cross to set the marker, close and use, Circle to set it and close, Triangle to close or, with the panel closed, to use the marker; the marker clears when its count reaches 0), the 8-step refill over the boot candidate rows (0x315C68), and the pool heal trunc(T x p / 100 + 1) with the forced rule. Tests reproduce the spec's heal vectors 8 to 12 and 52 and the panel vectors 41 and 42. In play the panel opens with numpad 8 or the D-pad (numpad 4/6/2 and the D-pad pick entries, T or the north button is Triangle) and takes Cross and Circle while it is open; a use heals health (pool A, table 0x319BE0) and magic (pool B, table 0x319BE8; Tor starts with 90 magic from 0x319BC8) and consumes the item when a pool changes. `--give` puts non-equipment items in the party's counts. Trace (757, `--trace-to fight --give 98 --give 146 --give 157 --seed 1`; the trace drinks below half health): potion 146 takes Tor from 50 to 150 on tick 1442 and is used up; item 157 is refused while magic is full. Not implemented: the Starlight Potion (57) and the revive (50) (their use is refused), charms and the Mote panel, the heal streak, cues and voices, the item screen, the use hook and charm-only gate, and a drawn panel (the status line shows the panel).
- **Hero statuses (SPEC-0036 Status timers, Status effects, Status apply, Regeneration, Damage factor G; SPEC-0021 Consumable effects by item id; SPEC-0038 Behavior 13 strike status; SPEC-0029 Behavior 9 status roll):** `neopets_sim::status` has the 14 timers with their default durations, the hero's apply method (immunities 8 to 10 block and clear curse, poison and slow; confusion and poison attack ignored; -1 clears all), the per-tick routine (stun: state 1 and speed scale 0; poison 2 HP every 240 ticks of its timer with the 1 HP floor; slow x 0.75 when moving or attacking; speed x 1.85 when moving; +2 HP and +2 MP on multiples of 60 of the status-12 timer, with the magic quirk), the trickle toward int(max x 0.1) + 1 (health every 29 ticks, magic every 59) and the curse and strength scale. Tests cover the immunities, the poison floor, the regeneration quirk, the scales and the trickle periods. In play the routine runs in the hero update before its state routine (the speed scale is the base of the play rate), a hit ends a stun, death clears all statuses, Tor's outgoing damage takes G, the status items work (51, 52, 53, 55, 56 and 145 only while inactive, the cure 54, the regeneration of 58 and 59 before their heal), a class-17 strike rolls its slot's status (int[0, 100] below the chance, for the rolled seconds) and an unblocked shot its own (at most the chance; no stun while stunned). The status line shows magic; the trace prints status changes. Traces (1080, `--trace-to fight --give 105`, seeds 1 to 4): no status reached Tor (the Poison Spyders opened with their charge slot and the shots carry none), so the in-play status path is checked only by its unit tests. Not implemented: the status display, tints, effects and cues, Starlight Potion (57) and the revive (50), and the enemies' own statuses.
- **Front-end database and the HUD bars (SPEC-0035 Layout: header, section table, C, D, E, J, K, O, texture chunk; Node transform; Sprite drawing; Predicate table slots 42 to 48. SPEC-0023 HUD screen and art, Behavior 1 to 4):** `neopets_data::frontend` reads resource 532: the version check (1.05), the key-to-node map (the last record wins), the screen records, the sprite records, the value-text records, the asset paths (sheet TEX0 words and picture numbers) and the 1,366-node tree, and resolves each sheet asset to its sub-texture and palette in the resource's texture chunk; a sprite's art is cut from its frame cell one texel per pixel with clamping. Census: 1,366 nodes by kind {0: 117, 1: 797, 2: 112, 3: 7, 4: 30, 5: 251, 6: 52}, 108 screens, 798 sprites, 52 value texts, 92 keys, 244 sheet assets (all resolve) and 101 pictures; record 9 is page 322 with state 11; keys 0x01, 0x02, 0x06, 0x07, 0x08, 0x09, 0x31, 0x3B, 0x12 and 0xD1 name nodes 617, 654, 651, 652, 656, 657, 615, 649, 578 and 186, as the specs list. `neopets_sim::hud` has the change detector C and the timers G0 and G1, the bar update with its watched values (re-arms on any change, the after-load reset C = -1), L and F at 2/3 pixel per unit, the frame layout (middle L - 6, right cap at L - 3), the dividers, alpha(t) and the red and green tint pulses; tests cover the widths, the 240-tick show and fade, and the 40-tick red pulse. In the window the bars group 0x31 of the HUD page is drawn from the disc tree with its own art on the 512 x 448 canvas scaled to fit the window: the predicates of slots 42 to 48 gate the groups and dividers, the fills, middles and right caps follow the bar layout, the fade alpha and the health frame's tint apply, and the HUD is hidden while a dialogue box is up or the game-over page shows. The boot words come from the program (pulse half-length, tints, bar offsets, divider table). Screenshot (909, after start): the health and magic bars with their frames at the top left of the canvas. Not implemented: the rest of the HUD page (quick-item panel and cross, equipment panels, petpet meter, monster bar, journal and D-pad icons, portraits, countdown texts, prompts), the other predicates, value texts and labels (fonts), the loss effects, the animation tracks, and the front-end screens themselves.
- **Fonts and the monster health bar (SPEC-0022 Fonts, Text layout rules 1, 2, 7 and 8; SPEC-0023 section 10; SPEC-0035 Section K):** `neopets_data::frontend` reads the three font descriptors and their glyph records from the database tail (census: 102, 89 and 91 glyphs, mode 4, line heights 21, 17 and 14, space advances 10, 6 and 5, as the spec lists), looks glyphs up through the code map, measures a line and draws it into an image from the glyph sheet (a sample line in font 0 reads correctly). `neopets_sim::hud::MonsterBar` has the arm (target, name, T = 600), the per-tick countdown, the zero-health cut to 60, the frame width trunc(M / 1.5 + 1) clamped to 64 to 250 and raised to the name width + 24, and the fill trunc(C x (B / M) + 1) capped at B; tests reproduce the section 10.3 examples and the kill timing. In play an enemy's take-hit arms the bar before its damage, with the name measured in the font of the name's value text (key 0x32), and the bar ticks with the HUD. In the window group 0x24 is drawn from the disc tree: the frame middle, right cap, name plate pieces and fill follow the layout (the fill at x scale 1.0), the name plate shows only with a name, the name text is drawn in its record's font and colour, and alpha(T) fades it. Screenshot (757, a test hook arming the bar, since removed): the Skeleton Archer's name plate and bar at the bottom left. Not implemented: the boss arms, the screen-effect gate, the loss effects, labels and the other value texts. Open readings in R-0018 (slot 41, the text baseline).
- **The dialogue box (SPEC-0022 Reveal and paging, Choices input and release items 1 and 2, Speaker name and colour, Dialogue box layout, Box geometry constants, Text layout; SPEC-0024 Container, tables 10 and 13):** `neopets_data::design` reads resource 533's tables (the hero name colours of table 10 and the action labels of table 13, for "Next" and "Finish"); lines keep their raw bytes; placements keep their colour bytes (+0x40); `neopets_data::frontend` has the break search (wrap width, spaces and underscores, newlines, stops), a multi-line text draw that runs type-2 colour codes, and a nine-slice of a sprite. `neopets_sim::textbox` has the box: pages split at type-4 stops, the reveal (1/30 per update, x 5 while Cross is held, 10 characters per progress unit), codes run once as the reveal reaches them and take no time (type 32 and 34 set and clear the hero override), the Square skip (not on the first update of a page), and on a complete page Square to the next page or to close. Tests reproduce the spec's timing (a 5-character page complete in update 15, update 3 with Cross held) and the paging. In play a table line opens the box with its speaker ("Hero", "[Hero]" or a code names Tor with the table-10 colour; a talk partner's own name and colour; otherwise the record's), the box takes Cross and Square while it is up, and the HUD is hidden meanwhile. The window draws the box from the disc art: the frame nine-slice of key 0x19 in the speaker colour at alpha 45, the name in font 0, the revealed body in font 0 wrapped at 410, and once the page is complete the Square icon (key 0x1B) with "Next" or "Finish" in font 1, all with the boot colours and geometry words. The trace presses Square every 10 ticks while a box is up; talking to Lucy still rotates lines 159, 160 and 161. Screenshot (909, a test hook opening line 159, since removed): Lucy's line in three wrapped lines with "Finish". Not implemented: choices (LevelPrompt), substitution codes, the controller and card messages, the frontier glyph's grow-in, the letterbox bars, cues, and the emote, give-items and flag codes (stepped over).
- **The Square prompt (SPEC-0023 sections 30 and 33; SPEC-0022 Interaction prompts; SPEC-0035 predicate slots 21 to 26):** the HUD's button-prompt group (the parent of the page node gated by slot 25) is drawn from the disc tree: the Square icon (slot 25, at canvas (295, 371)) shows on the passes a caption is set, the other slots stay hidden, and the caption (a table-13 label: 8 Talk at a talk candidate, else the door prompt the chooser shows, 13 Enter, 26 Exit or a script door's context) is drawn at the Square cell (327, 395) in the default caption colour. Nothing shows while a box or the quick panel is up. Screenshot (909, a test hook forcing label 8, since removed): "Talk" with the Square icon at the bottom of the canvas. Open reading (font and baseline) in R-0018.
- **Equipment and the item screen (SPEC-0021 Equipment families and ranking, Party inventory lists, Equipment slots, Using an equipped item; SPEC-0035 sections 9c, 14.1 and 14.2; SPEC-0023 section 34):** `neopets_sim::inventory` has the family add rule (types 6, 5 and 4 to Tor's slot, 3, 2 and 1 to Roberta's; an empty entry is filled, a greater id replaces it), the Mote words, the Items-tab cursor and the name-line format; tests reproduce the spec's cursor and name-line vectors. In play every held equipment item goes to its family entry each tick (a replaced item leaves the inventory, an entry whose item is gone empties), so a found or bought sword, shield or armour is equipped at once and drives the combat code's weapon and shield; `--give` now only adds. Start (Tab, or the Start button) opens the item screen on its last tab (Tor's records 6 to 8, whose pictures show Tor; R-0031): L1 and R1 (Z and C, or the shoulder buttons) change tab with cues 0x98 and 0x99; Triangle or Start closes (0x95). The Items tab moves its cursor by the grid rule over the 20 boot grid ids (0x2DCF28), Cross uses the item (a charm fails with 0xA2), Circle sets or clears the quick-item marker (0x9A, 0xA3), Square swaps the cloak's worn flag; the Charms tab steps through the owned Motes and Cross, Square (with a shield) and Circle (with a weapon) equip Mote words 0, 1 and 2 (0xA3 on a change); the Quest-items tab refuses Cross and Circle (0xA2). The predicates of the item screen (slots 1, 3 to 9, 13 to 17, 19, 20, 52) read a snapshot of the party, and the tab states draw the shared groups themselves (the Items tab's 0xC2 group from record 3's library, the Charms tab's Mote panel 0xC0) with the name and description lines in their value-text fonts. Window screenshots: the Items tab shows the held Red and Purple Juppies and the Unguent with their emblems, the cursor ring and "Red Juppie (2)" with its description; the Charms tab shows the held Fire and Leaf Motes on the Mote panel. Trace: buying the shield in 1131 makes it Tor's shield entry. Not implemented: Roberta's tabs and the hero index, the HUD bars and NeoPoints element on the item screen (SPEC-0023 section 34), the action box's selection, the cells' counts, the equipped-item icons, the Mote words' combat effects (attack, shield and armour Motes), the cloak's body-entry swap, the times-obtained count of the name line, the page ring (L2, R2). Unverified: the empty-cell rule, the Mote picker's directions and the hero order of the codes (R-0031).
- **Motes in combat (SPEC-0036 Damage, Mote elements and type match, Mote charge use by attacks, Magic, Power-ready check, Power attack and full release, Enemy attack damage to a hero; SPEC-0021 Inventory, Mote charge; SPEC-0029 Layout 4):** `neopets_sim::mote` has the element kinds by id, Match(E_a, T), the shield and armour element rule, the Magic table's cost and power words (read from the boot data at 0x2DD47C), the charge amounts (Tor's truncation rule with the minimum of 1, the armour rule with none), the melee damage x = P A B C D E with the absorb test, the shield block (x 1.25 with a shield Mote), the armour Mote's (100 - H)% and the body item's (1 - A) scales, and the attack power table index map; every product is the console's single-precision operation. `neopets_data` reads the row target type (+0x3C), each attack's damage alignment (+0x3BC + 4k) and the attack power table (resource 533, table 16). In play, Tor's swing takes B and C from the word-2 Mote (stats F and G from its item record) against the target's T, and P from the attack power table in the power step; a landed swing outside the power step pays the level-0 cost and then uses word-2 charge; the power step's entry uses level-1 charge and then MP; the power-ready check returns F to 0 from F = 50 when the Mote is not held, there is no weapon or MP is short; a common-enemy strike takes the shield block (with the shield Mote's charge and 2 or 1 MP when the strike's alignment passes), the armour Mote (when Q0 is at most its charge, MP covers its cost and the alignment passes) and the body item. A Mote is now added with its charge of 100 on a diagnostic give and on a shop buy (pickups already did). A spent Mote leaves the inventory and every word that holds it. Not done: the Mote power-attack objects (Charm attack hits) and the Mote status sent in the power step, the guarding-target block as a landed hit, the HUD timers of a failed power-ready check and of a charge use (SPEC-0023 section 4), the petpet factors E and 0.9, the attacker's status scale G on strikes, Roberta, and the shield and armour rules on enemy pool projectiles (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9 keeps its own block value). Verified by unit vectors (sword 98 with Mote 161 deals 4, the charge amounts 1, 3, 24, 39, 3 and 5, book 178's blocked 60 giving 20 and 10) and a trace; the armour and shield paths are not traced in play.
- **Tor's Mote power-attack objects (SPEC-0036 Charm attack hits, Object pairing, Ten-slot timed lists, Hurt shapes body cylinder, Power-ready check):** `neopets_sim::charm` has the objects of Lava 161 (family B: a 2.5-radius sphere circling the hero at the orbit radius word 0x2DCB68, one turn per second, for the seconds word 0x2DCB64), Wind 167 (vertical cylinder, radius 0x2DCBB8, height 0x2DCBBC, 10 s) and Supernova 173 (Nova cylinder, radius 0x2DCBB0, height 2.0, 10 s), the sphere and cylinder tests against a target's body cylinder (with the spec's tangency vectors), the ten-slot hit list with 60-tick entries, the stored scale A x D x B x G x E and the charm damage trunc(max(scale x C - defence, 0) x P), with C from the object's own Mote against the target type and P from the attack power table. In play the power release spawns the word-2 Mote's object (after its charge and MP), the objects tick after the swing and hit every hittable character of the world through the shared take-hit, and the power-ready check also fails while an object is live. Unverified or not done: the Wind and Nova centres stay at the spawn point (hero position plus facing x reach; the spec does not say where +0x80 goes, R-0033), the orbit starts along the facing at the hero's feet height (R-0033), the 0.2 step toward a target, the family A objects (163, 165, 172) and Shadow 169 and Sun 171 (sizes open, R-0033), the Lava fire status and the other status requests (the enemy status apply of SPEC-0029 Behavior 27 is not built), the effects (the objects are not drawn) and the breakable walks.
- **Enemy statuses (SPEC-0029 Behavior 27; SPEC-0036 Mote status words, Damage take-hit, Enemy attack damage to a hero G; SPEC-0038 Behavior 37 steps 7 and 13, Behavior 5 state 4):** `neopets_sim::enemy` has the 14 status timers, the apply with the defaults (stun 5 s, curse, slow, strength and speed 60 s, poison 120 s, confusion and poison attack 30 s, fire 5 s), the element immunity by target type, the stun's class state 4 (and back to agro when it ends), the poison and fire hits on multiples of the period (defaults 2 HP every 4 s and 2 HP every 2 s, no floor), the slow and speed clip-rate scales (tests A and B read as moving and attacking), the projectile damage scale (0.5 curse, 1.5 strength) and the strike scale G (0.75, 1.5, 1.125), and no hit reaction or force-start while stunned. `neopets_sim::mote::status_request` reads a Mote's status code and its duration, amount and period from the attack power table records after its power index. In play: Tor's power-step melee hits send the word-2 Mote's status, Tor's Lava orbit sends fire, poison and fire hits run through the shared take-hit, enemy shots are scaled at spawn, strikes take G, and an enemy with status 11 poisons the hero on a landed strike. Not done: confusion (outer 11), the status display and tints on enemies, the spell kinds that give statuses (3, 4 and 7), and the Water stun object of either hero.
- **Mote panel re-arms and shot reductions (SPEC-0023 section 4 re-arms; SPEC-0029 Behavior 9 hit on the hero; SPEC-0036 Enemy attack damage to a hero):** equipping Mote word 0, 1 or 2, and each use of charge from that word, re-arm G3, G4 or G2 (`Hud::rearm_word`). An enemy pool shot's hero hit now reports its raw damage and element, and play takes D through the same shield block, armour Mote and body item as a strike (G 1, the curse and strength scale having been applied at spawn), then max(1, 0.25 D) when blocked; a reflection uses the shield Mote's charge (no MP). Unverified: the four HUD timers of a failed power-ready check, the shot shield rule's details, the reflection's charge and element test, and the strike's curse scale (R-0034).
- **The live petpet (SPEC-0047 Layout pet type to kind, Packages by kind, Kind multipliers, Activation by type, Spawn, Scene start restore, Save, Class 34, Passive bonuses; SPEC-0025 petpet fields):** `neopets_sim::petpet` has the two kind tables, the packages (534 + kind), the kind multipliers over sets A, B and C, the meal rule (T and F to trunc(v x 3600) when larger), the presence gate (T at least 1), the passive bonuses (E 1 + 0.25 for kinds 0 and 7, the intake 1 - 0.1 for kinds 1 and 7, the hit point for kinds 6 and 7 and the magic point for kinds 3 and 7 when T mod 60 is 0), the save fields, the pending values and the scene-start restore, the HUD meter width, and the class 34 controller (state choice with the contact, pending-event and low-time rules; state 1's walk to a point 3 to 10 units off the hero, its 180 to 600-tick wait and its animation pick by T / 1800; state 2's point 4 ahead and 1.5 to the side with the three gaits; the state 6 one-shot; state 5's leave test). In play: activation by type loads the kind's package into a petpet slot and places its character 7 units ahead playing animation 6; the petpet follows by walking with its root motion; T counts down outside dialogue; the bonus words (0x2DD8DC to 0x2DD8E8) and the low-time mark (0x2DB1D8) are read from the boot data; E enters melee and Mote objects, the intake factor follows the armour on strikes and shots, and the points join the trickle before the cap; the save writes the type, T and F, and a load restores the petpet at the next scene start. Unverified or not done: feeding and wild petpets (class 33), the strike and food reservation, class 20, the HUD petpet panel and meter draw (its gate, R-0035), the script query's readers (packs 758 and 1300 are not built), the bumped recovery and the nearby-character response, the act-start removal, and how the petpet behaves across world changes (it follows and is placed 7 units ahead when over 60 units away; R-0035). Every question is in R-0035.
- **Wild petpets, feeding and the petpet panel (SPEC-0047 Acquisition, State selection, States, Strike, Hit value and score, Spawn, Activation by id, Feeding a petpet; SPEC-0022 Feed petpet; SPEC-0023 sections 2 and 7; SPEC-0036 Hittable predicate tests 4 and 5):** `neopets_sim::petpet` gains the food values (the nine base words at 0x2DB1DC read from the boot data, times the kind multiplier of the sets, which are now read too), the feedable ids, the shared food reservation with its 5 s fallback, the class 33 wild loop (the state choice by hero distance, fed flag, food and anchor; state 1's animation 9 once; the roam to a point within 40 units of the hero; the strike with the facing-and-distance eat test, the walk and the turn; the hover 10 units off an anchor) and class 34 state 4 (the live petpet eats the nearest food where it stands). The hero has state 49 (clip 138, back to state 1 at 0.99). In play: placed records of class 33 run the wild loop with a walker; the "Feed petpet" prompt (label 14) follows the door prompt in the chooser, for the own petpet within 2.5 or a wild one within 4.5 in front of the hero; Square with a feedable category-12 food as the selected quick item enters state 49, otherwise cue 0xA2. At 0.99 the nearest own or wild petpet within 10.5 is fed (a wild one also sends the live petpet away with T = -1), one food is dropped 2.8 ahead at scale 0.45, reserved and never collected, and one leaves the inventory. A wild petpet that eats gets the meal's time, is removed, and the live petpet spawns at its place with T and F from the meal; while a type is live no record of that type binds. Characters whose pet type has a kind in either table are skipped by swings, shots and Mote walks. The HUD draws the petpet panel (the predicate-38 group with the portrait, the meter frame and fill 0x12 at width round(60 T / F), hidden below 1). Trace: `NEOPETS_TRACE_FOLLOW=wild NEOPETS_TRACE_QUICK=146 --play-trace 1500 --pack 1066 --seed 1 --start " -756,548" --give 146 --give 146`: the prompt shows, Tor feeds a Chokato on tick 94, the type 450 petpet (kind 0) walks to it, eats it on tick 259 and joins with T 27000 (SPEC-0047 vector 37), then follows Tor; a screenshot with `NEOPETS_TRACE_PET` shows the panel with the kind 0 portrait and the meter. Unverified or not built: which item the feed uses (the selected quick item is used), whether a wild petpet's roam centres on the hero, the roam wait range, the reach test (food always counts as reachable), slot 38's value (SPEC-0023 and SPEC-0035 differ; the kind is used), the panel's fade, the own petpet's 4.5 range below 45 s, the exits of the eat states with no food (all R-0094), class 20, the bumped recovery and the nearby-character response of class 34, and a wild petpet returning to where it ate after the live one leaves (it returns when its region loads again). New trace options: `NEOPETS_TRACE_QUICK=id` (the selected quick item, also in windowed play), `NEOPETS_TRACE_FOLLOW=wild`, and `NEOPETS_TRACE_PET` in windowed play.
- **Class 20, the second petpet behaviour, partly (SPEC-0047 Class 20; vectors 25 and 26):** `neopets_sim::petpet::Class20` has the choice rules without the item test (so state 1, the hero reaction, is never chosen), state 0, state 2 (the nearest world object other than the anchor within 25 units: walk to it while 2.5 or more away, latch and consume it within 2.5, give up when it moved more than 2.0 from its stored point or is gone; animation 4 once latched, then state 3 with a 4 to 5 s wait) and state 3 (the wait with animation 0, a point within 40 units of the reference point, the walk and the 5.0 arrival). The wait words (0x2DB2AC and 0x2DB2B0) are read from the boot data. In play, placed records of class 20 run it with a walker about their spawn point (the reference object, Medium); a consumed object is removed from the world; the seek sets the food reservation's slow flag; the Feed petpet prompt also shows for a class 20 creature within 25 in front of the hero. Unit tests cover vectors 25 and 26. Not traced: no class 20 placement has been located on the disc yet, so it has not run in play. Not built: state 1 and the item test (open questions 18 and 21).
- **Mirrored clips and two fall fixes (SPEC-0005 Mirrored clips, Clip start, Playback 5; SPEC-0033 Floor-contact flag, Behavior 7; found tracing pack 1300):** main clips whose action-table word A has bit 12 now play mirrored: each sample of joint i goes to its mirror partner (the skeleton record's second field), rotation (x, -y, -z, w), translation (-x, y, z), and the root step's x is negated; a request restarts the clip when only the mirror flag differs. Sub-part and face clips are not mirrored (none is on disc). Before this, the climb's sideways clips 52 and 53 (the same clip, 53 mirrored) both moved the hero toward u = 0, so with the stick toward u = LU the hero slid off the panels into the air. Now in pack 1300 Roberta climbs the tree panels (1 to 4 to 6), moves sideways round the trunk (corners to 7 and 8), stops at panel 8's edge and Stop Climbing drops her onto the branch (y 1557.5), where she picks up the wand handle (item 11, flag 225). Second, a hero more than 0.05 inside a probed floor now sets the floor-contact flag (the spec's contact rule, applied to the probed ground too; Medium): a walk-off onto a spot where the probe missed for one tick left Roberta in fall phase 4 with zero speed forever on the palace steps. Third, a safety clamp (no spec): the drawn-height catch-up never steps past the world height; it only acts on gaps above about 500 units, where the formula diverges, which happened when a collision box lifted Roberta 2,700 units after a fall through a flower bed with no ground data (around (-73, 1530, -125) in pack 1300), and the camera went to NaN. That flower-bed gap itself is not fixed. New trace tools: the script trigger positions are printed at the start; `NEOPETS_TRACE_STICK="tick:f,s;..."` (raw stick values from a tick on, "tick:-" back to the route), `NEOPETS_TRACE_SQUARE="ticks"` and `NEOPETS_TRACE_FACING` (facing, stick target, velocity, root step and camera).

- **Act 4 gaps (SPEC-0041 1381 and 1382; SPEC-0042 1397; SPEC-0030 row 3):** 1382 CB4 now activates object 27 at its end pose (the spec's ActivateObj(27, forced); row 3's bool is the end pose), not at time zero. A trace tool: `NEOPETS_TRACE_FACE="tick:x,z;..."` turns the hero to face a point. Traced: in 1382 the six floor switches (each raising its marker with camera 4), then the ring disc (trigger 2 is the disc the hero stands on, object 2's patch at y -219.9) runs CB4 and loads region 1390; all six pedestals lit in one run (two passes over them, the first facing each pedestal) set 98 and 337, the ring centre (trigger 28) loads 1411, and 1411's Init sets 96, 335 and 688 to 691. In 1381 pad 3 sends the hero to locator 7 and a strike on target 18 inside area 19 sets 710 and returns the hero to locator 0. Open (R-0101): how the hero reaches the 1382 pedestal ring from the switch floor (object 27's pose and play, the undescribed activate-world callback, the floor at y -410 below the pit), the disc's radius (90 on disc, 3 in the spec), and what lifts 1397's block 0 to trigger 0 (687; open in the spec too). Not traced: the 1392 to 1394 caves on foot (an auto-jump route trace reached z -239 and stalled; a jumping course, left for hand play), the 1398 stage 4 catches (R-0100), and the walks between spots.
- **The final fight to the credits (SPEC-0040 Behavior 20; SPEC-0036 Roberta shot launch; SPEC-0054; SPEC-0058 Endings; SPEC-0026 Credits):** `neopets_sim::lock` holds the hero's candidate list (15 records; the add, the refresh with its skip after a removal, the engagement at an alignment above 0.25 while attacking, in range below 20.0, the in-range records first and the pairwise order with the 1.0 swap quirk, the lock at the head); play runs it each tick over the living hittable characters (not townsfolk or petpet types). Roberta's shots now aim per the launch rule: homing modes 0 and 1 at the lock, modes 2 to 5 at a random character within 60 whose aim point is visible along three rays (the fan is not built), else the default point; the aim point is taken as the body's middle (R-0102). The credits gate: on the play pass after story bit 368 the credits open with the pending flag (Cross and Triangle ignored), and the roll's end tears the world down to record 0. Tests: the candidate list (engagement, range, the swap quirk, removal, the 15-record cap) and the credits from play. Trace tools: `NEOPETS_TRACE_GUARD=n`, `NEOPETS_TRACE_GUARD_ORB`, `NEOPETS_TRACE_FACE_NAME="name@n"`, `NEOPETS_TRACE_FAERIE_DRIVE`, `NEOPETS_TRACE_FAERIE` and `NEOPETS_TRACE_CREDITS`. Traced in 1432 (god mode, sword 101, shield 106, the Nova Mote 172 in equipment word 1, combo presses 30, 65 and 87): the shove ends her first burst; a guard while her orbs fly reflects them (code 3 to 5); the combo finisher (Tor's sub-state 4) in the stun counts (code 6), three times; the swap to Roberta, the burst and the absorb window (code 7); with Roberta 10 units off her aimed shots are absorbed three times (code 9, then 10 landed); stepping onto trigger 1 runs the finale (94, 365) and loads 1300 (366); in 1300 triggers 2 and 3 set 367 and 368 and the credits gate fires. Not traced: the fight by hand, the timeout ending.
- **Pack 864's instance-load worker (SPEC-0044 864, R-0099 answered):** Init now registers the four instance-trigger groups (4, 2, 5 and 3, main triggers 10, 15, 16 and 18) to the shared instance callback, which runs the worker: group 2 selects set 1 in store A and set 5 in store C with flag 288 clear; group 3 set 1 and set 8 with 292 clear; group 4 set 4 (the Ixi Chieftain) and set 3 with 58 clear; group 5 set 1 and set 7 with 289 clear; a gate with no C set resets C; store B is never created, so the set 2 and 6 requests do nothing. Store A is slot 0 and C slot 2; a store already holding the set is left as it is. 1 test. Traced (Act 3 flags through step 39, flag 58 clear): entering trigger 10 loads region 869 (the Chieftain) and 868 (Mayor Jurgin); trigger 1 starts the fight (line 6) and he charges; with his HP set to 0 he dies, level state 0 marks the fight end and 293 and 668 are set. Step 37 is no longer blocked. Not traced: the fight by hand and the exit cutscene.
- **The R-0080 to R-0102 spec update, first pass (SPEC-0012, 0013, 0021, 0022, 0036, 0039, 0040, 0041, 0042, 0043, 0047, 0048):** 1382 CB4 plays object 27 at speed -0.7 from its end pose, so the lift descends and its ground carries the hero from the ring (y -220) to the lower floor (y -410, traced). ReacquireMovableBlockHeightPatch takes its float as the probe's ceiling offset (1397's per-tick 500, its triggers 1.0, 1324's 3.0): 1397's block 0 now sits on the upper floor (y 16) and, slid onto trigger 0, solves the room (687; traced). Targeting: a character's target point is render attachment point 7 (the position when the model has none); the candidate list adds the class-state veto for Display-in-Default-0 rows (vetoed in the default state, or in state 21 unless animation 82 plays; the active-hero exception and the other branch are not built); the final fight still traces to a win. The Black Knight: the hero body cylinder's length is the weapon reach W + 2.0 (Roberta's reach table for 111 to 118 is now read; 3.5 with no weapon), and the hand and sword return point are attachment point 0. The Gelert: the missile reaction tests every posed bone cylinder of positive radius, and the landing search accepts any navmesh of the region. Petpets: the wild roam centres on the configured reference object (taken as the wild petpet's spawn point) with a 60 to 300-tick wait; the food's reach is its point located on the pet's current navmesh (nearest food only, the Bagguss excluded); the feed takes the first category-12 item held when no quick item is selected (item id order); the own petpet's prompt range is 4.5 while T is below 2,700 ticks and needs no pending reservation. Item gives are capped by the carry limit (the game state keeps the text table's limits), so a line's give and a script's give of one item both run without exceeding it. 886 thread 3 reads flags 52, 51, 50 and 251 (not 49); with all four clear, flag 250 loads set 4 into slot 2, else set 5 into slot 1 with entities 16 to 18 activated and agro on 16 and 17: the three Brightvale minions now load and beating them sets 250 (traced). 1324: the solve no longer runs the FMV-23 scene; both plate paths end with the camera returned, so flag 239 is never set (R-0103: Act 2 step 25 needs 239). 1397 trigger 21 plays camera 7 and the 120-step turn of object 40 for each correct statue. Kept against the update and asked in R-0103: 1382 trigger 2's disc reading, 1398's thread-6 region rule, 886's unhide call. Already matching: the Draik's 5-HP hits and stage groups, the Sisters' strict bands, the serpent's break-scene order, slot 38, the line gives.
- **1091's hazard sequencers, the 1089 hold and the farm pump (SPEC-0044, SPEC-0047; R-0089, R-0091, R-0092):** 1091's Update runs its two sequencers one pass at a time (word 52 busy, 51 the running mode, 54 the mode wanted next, 50 the shared word): mode 2 swings object 3 forward 0.6 and back while the word is 0; mode 1 swings object 6 forward at 1.6 once and then runs its nine steps while the word is 1, reversing 6 and 9 at -0.4 when cancelled. Forward swings of objects 4, 5, 9 and 10 set their hazard words (40 + object) and backward swings clear them; while set, those objects kill the hero on contact. Triggers 41 and 42 (routine 41) scrub object 2 or 17 and request the mode. The 1089 hold keeps object B at time 0 and the idle units (1, 2), (3, 4) and 5 at their own times. The farm pump (909 routine 11) takes item 6 at once (word 3), plays objects 18 and 20 without a wait and waits for 17, stops 20 and turns it off, plays 19, runs effects 3 and 4, sound 10 and a camera shake, then line 166, GiveItem 7, object 9 off, line 162 and flag 112; other talks give line 165 once 112 is set, else 163 holding item 7, else 164. Unit-tested (`module_1091_sequencers`, `farm_pump_exchange`); not hand-played.
- **Ledges (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 32, 37 and 51; R-0080, R-0085; `neopets-sim/src/ledge.rs`):** in the jump states' air phases 0 to 5 the in-air ledge chooser takes as the hero's edge contact the nearby frame record that passes the edge acceptance test (type not 3 or 6, face tilt 0.2, face distance -0.15 to 1.25r, top 0 to 4 above the feet, inside the run, facing below -0.5, the five-tick look-ahead) and faces the hero most, then applies the 0.85 ground-normal and t > y gates and the class order: hang (t - g > H + 0.75 and t <= y + H) enters state 30; climb (t - g > 1.75 and t - 0.5 <= y + 0.85H) enters 47 while rising, 35 while falling. State 30 grabs (72), moves 0.25 a tick to the point 0.7 inside the face and H - 0.1 below t (snapping within 0.25), hangs (71, 73), shimmies with 61 and 60 inside the run limits (sub-modes 5 and 6 compared as in vector 183), pulls up (59) with the stick forward and leaves at 0.95 to state 4 (stick forward) or 1; Square with the Let Go label (20) drops to state 1. States 35 and 47 approach the point 0.5 inside the face at t - 0.75 (0.25 a tick) or t - 1.1 (0.2 a tick, ended by a non-positive gap), play 75 or 134 and leave at 0.95. All three hold no gravity and clear the motion; the clips' root motion lifts the hero. Tests: vectors 181 to 183, the acceptance gates, a hang run. Traced in the opening level: a run jump at a 2.5 ledge enters 47 and ends on its top in state 4; a jump at a 6.4 wall enters 30, hangs with the prompt and pulls up 3.7 (that wall's top has no ground, so the hero then falls); falling past a far edge hangs. `NEOPETS_TRACE_LEDGES` lists the level's jump-to-ledge candidates and the ledge ticks. Not built: the state-30 ladder check and flag clear, effect 9 and the 0x40 windows (the forward exit is taken at 0.95), state 47's touched-flag branch, the helper tests of states 35 and 47, state 35's 0.75 push, the ledge drop of states 37 and 38 (Behavior 49), the boost jump of the ground offer. State 35 is not reached in a trace.
- **Rope-gun aim targets and two requests (SPEC-0028 section 6 n2 entries; SPEC-0030 row 126):** `neopets_data::ground::aim_targets` reads a pack's n2 entries (count +0x20, 0x0C-byte entries at +0x48: the registry model id, the inline marker mesh and the locator-like record's +0x30 target point, its offset read as payload-relative like the header's tables); `neopets-data --aim-targets` finds 54 targets in 19 packs on the disc (the spec's 19 chunks; 1091 has seven with inline meshes, the final arena 1429 to 1433 four at y 46.5). Synthetic test `aim_target_entries`. Nothing uses them yet: the aim scan's compared vectors and origin, its result codes, the rope's outbound step and attach test, its drawing and whether the Rope Gun is on the main route are asked as R-0104, so no aim mode, scan or state 27 is built. 1393's guard rooms are not built: R-0090's answer gives each room's ids in pairs, and which member each rule plays or opens is asked as R-0105 (1393 is on the main route). The playtest guide now describes ledges and lists the 1393 and Rope Gun gaps.
- **Ledge drop and state 37 (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 49 and 51; R-0106):** in the ground states 1 to 4 the hero runs the ledge-drop test on its cell's frame records in order (the reference point, face tilt 0.2, face side within 0.3 with one side negative, the window 0.2 below to 3.0 above the edge, inside the run, the five-tick look-ahead, a negative type accepted at once; else the drop rule: the probe 0.1 past the face at the edge's middle, ground kind 2 accepts, no ground or no drop rejects, more than 1.75 accepts). Walking or running toward the edge's outward normal starts the running jump (state 10) unless the type is 1 or 6; standing or slow walking toward it, or standing with the back to it, enters state 37 unless the type is 3 or 6. State 37: from a slow walk a shallow edge (ground 2.0 and 2.5 ahead within 4.5) returns to the walk; else the hero turns out, walks (request 2) until 0.15 past the face, turns back (request 73) until within 0.99 of facing the wall, and goes to state 30.
