Two choices make the hang work from a top, both asked in R-0106: the no-snap flag (+0x148, now in the motion record's ground constraint) is set while in state 30, and state 30's gap gate applies from sub-mode 2 on. Tests: `ledge_drop_test`. Traced in the opening level: walking off a 2.5 ledge jumps at the face (state 10); standing at a 9.4 cliff edge drops, hangs with Let Go and pulls back up onto the top; the jump-to-ledge climb and hang still trace as before; the 3,000-tick default route meets no ledge event. Not built: the ground-object gates (the hero is taken as riding nothing), the boosted jump of the ground edge offer, the camera side-offset event, the landing-flag exit and the neighbouring-edge walk of state 37, state 38, and the state 6 / state 11 branch (states 5 and 6 are not entered in this build).
- **Swing trails (SPEC-0034 3.8 and section 18; `neopets-sim/src/trail.rs`, `effect_view::sync_trails`):** the trail manager (12 trails, rings of 18 samples), the held-weapon record (0x392790) and the eight element records with the kind-to-record map, the sample rule (width, colour A, u from the larger point movement times the u step), the fade factor (0 at or below d0, 1 at or above d1, compounding on alpha each update), the ageing (one step after an append, else the record's steps; A to B over c updates, B to C up to the cap, retired at n + 1 with colour C) and the trail's end when no sample is live. On every tick with the hero's attack-active bit (event bit 0x1) the hero's trail is kept going from attach point 0 (point 1 = B + 0.25D, point 2 = point 1 + 0.6W D); an enemy's on every tick with event bits 0x1 to 0x10, with L = 1.5. Drawn as quads between consecutive live samples in group 11's additive material (common object 297), colours on the GS scale. Tests: the spec's record, edge-point, colour, fade and slot vectors. Traced: a Tor swing builds a 13-quad ribbon over its window that ages out 15 ticks later (`NEOPETS_TRACE_TRAILS`). Not looked at on screen. Not built: the clip-list trails of +0x48 = 0 entries (element-coloured, for region characters), the Black Knight's own trail, the marker and projectile trail records, the u wrap, and the pause gate (the trails age in the sim tick, which does not run while paused). The spec's ageing-gate test vector says the opposite of its text (codes 8 and 9 swapped); the text is followed.
- **First playtest round (2026-10-27):** (1) **Model-set entry gate (SPEC-0018):** the ground probe and the frame-record lookup now skip a model-set entry whose gate (+0x42) is nonzero and shares no bit with the world collision mask (SetWorldCollisMask; 0 at load). The four gate-4 wall rings of packs 696 and 714 were always solid; now they block only after flag 220 (Act 2 on), as 696's Update sets mask 4. This is the likely cause of the reported invisible wall before the village; the trace driver could not walk all the way into 1023, so it is not traced end to end. (2) **World layer mask (SPEC-0046 Culling):** the world scene is built with the display word OR the story-layer bits (layers 1 to 12 from flags 83/5, 10/322, 64/283, 57/58, 74/263, 85/298, 92/94, 248, 332 with 100 to 106) and rebuilt when it changes; it was always mask 0, so every node with layer flags (story scenery in 678, 686, 691, 696, 699, 702 to 706, 713, 714; the low-bit layouts of 1066 and others) was never drawn. Not looked at on screen. (3) **Farm ladder:** the box primitives are skipped in state 41 (this build's choice, R-0108): the barn wall's box undid the mount every tick. Traced: from the box under the ladder a jump grabs it, mounts and climbs off the top. (4) **Reeds (SPEC-0060 Behavior 9):** the bend away from the hero within 5 units, the weapon cut (the bush capsule taken as the test), cue 22 or 28, the three-state fall of the cut piece, gone until the next load, and the drop roll; the farm's corn is 14 reed patches. Traced: swings cut reeds. (5) **Item showcase, full mode (SPEC-0021; SPEC-0033 Behavior 42):** a world-item pickup with showcase text (the +0x24 first-pickup or +0x28 later text, else the progress-gated pair) and a free hero plays the pose (0xA4 weapons and wands, 0xA6 shields, 0xA5 armour and dresses, else 0xA3 held 1.0 s), the stinger (8, 6 or 7), the text titled with the hero's name and the floating spinning item 2.8 up and 1.1 ahead (0xA3 only), and ends 2.0 s after the text opens once it is closed. Traced for the bucket, a sword, a Chokato and a clover. Not built: modes 1 to 3 (items without text), the effect and the camera call. (6) **Unarmed swings** call no take-hit (SPEC-0036 Unarmed melee); the swing itself is original. (7) **Developer menu:** No clip (the hero's wall, box, block and sphere contacts off) and Give weapon (the item table's weapons by name); Give item ranges over the whole table. Asked: R-0108 (blocks with no wall test, the ladder box, the hang depth). Checked and per spec: the crate push has no wall test; the hang point sits 0.7 behind the face; 934 and 696 load no enemies by script; the swamp (735) and 1066, 893 and 714 do. Not found: Roberta's missing hair and glitching ears (her chain constants match SPEC-0040; the mode-2 axis flip cannot trigger with a yaw-only root).
- **Second playtest round (2026-10-27):** (1) **Chests:** an object of a type-40 group whose set-up is built (kinds 1, 5 and 6) that the set-up did not pose is not drawn in any pack until a script turns it on (it was hidden only in the script-gated packs), so every lock chest's lid block and every undug dig site's pieces are hidden as the house chest's were. Not looked at on screen outside 951. (2) **Floating after a world change:** `Locomotion::teleport` clears the no-gravity and no-snap flags and an arrival drops every traversal hold (ladder, ledge, block hang and climb, panel, block hold); leaving the root cellar (947) up its ladder used to carry the ladder's no-gravity into 934. The climb and the exit to 934 are traced. (3) **Grab lines (SPEC-0033 Behavior 9, state 28; `neopets-sim/src/grab.rs`):** the bit-1 cylinders of the type-11 list (gated by their mask word), the entry from the jump states with the 60-tick cooldown, the attach 3.25 + r below the line, the 0.5 slide, the line end, ground contact and Square let go at u x 15.0 into air phase 5; the reach is the spec's measured bands (the three-segment probe is Medium). 1066's line (A (-697.6, 54.6, 747.6), 60 long, gate 1) is read; its start platform is not found by the trace (likely a placed object), so a ride is not traced. Not built: the leap between lines, request 113, cue 141, the state-48 exit. (4) **1023 slots:** the load thread's first and second slots are slots 2 and 3, so the asset-swap zone loads (slots 0 and 1) no longer evict the guardsmen (set 2) or the chase sets; traced: the six guardsmen and Wymond and Bermund at the gate. Zone loads still replace each other in slots 0 and 1 as SPEC-0006 states (the arena master Shanra loads in her plain zone and a cycle zone entered next replaces her). (5) **934 ambush waves (threads 13 and 14):** set 6 (the Flower and Spore Monsters near (-1231, 1007)) and set 4 (three Crokabeks) load at Init while flags 3 to 5 and 372 / 378 are clear, and once a wave's characters are all dead its slot is reset and 372 or 378 set. Traced: both load. Not built: thread 21 (the low-health hint). (6) **SetAI_MovementType (SPEC-0030 row 117; SPEC-0040 Behavior 14D item 8):** a scripted walk uses its character's movement type (walk 0.08337 a tick with id 1, run 0.22504 with id 6, fast run 0.32160 with id 5, types 3 and 4 idle); the 1023 chase sets the Meerca to fast run (step 2) and the scene's calls. (7) **947** loads its spyders (region 948; the embedded three are summon reserves) and they activate near the hero (traced and seen on screen). (8) **Ledge hang drawing:** while hanging (state 30) the hero is drawn 1.4 out along the edge normal (easing to 0 over the pull-up); the motion keeps the spec's point 0.7 behind the face, since a point in front made the pull-up land at the edge and drop straight back (traced). Not looked at on screen (R-0108). Not fixed: the slab in 1148 (a world-scene part, not an object, character, book or layer node), the 1023 mountain flicker, Roberta's hair and ears.
- **Third playtest pass (2026-10-27):** a scene part of the other drawn world that repeats a part of the current world exactly (the same vertex count and bounds) is hidden: 1023 and 696 carry seven identical castle parts (towers and roofs, y 107 to 241), and drawn twice in one place the two copies fought for depth, the likely cause of the reported flicker in 1023 (this build's draw rule; not looked at on screen). Diagnostics: `NEOPETS_TRACE_PARTS` lists each built scene part (bounds, layout, program, group, texture), `NEOPETS_SCENE_MASK` forces the world layer mask, `NEOPETS_TRACE_ROBERTA=1` now also starts the windowed game as Roberta, `NEOPETS_NO_CHAINS` turns the chain step off. Asked as R-0109: Roberta's head (her outfit has an open gap on top of the head even at rest, and her head chains' rest angles fold links 1 and 2 back so the pieces stand up) and the 1148 registration desk front, which draws as a stretched texture page (the window at the material's TBP0 of this build's GS memory image).
- **Grab-line leap and the 934 hint (SPEC-0033 Behavior 9 modes 2 and 3; SPEC-0045 934 thread 21):** while sliding (mode 1), Cross held with the side stick outside the dead band searches for a parallel line (axis dot above 0.75, on the -B1 side for the stick right and the +B1 side for left, its end ahead, the hero inside its span, within 20.0 sideways and 8.0 in height; the nearest by side distance) and launches (request 114 or 116; one slide step), then flies: each tick a 0.5 slide along the current line, the side velocity (the side gap / 40) and the vertical velocity (0.4 less 0.02 a tick; the falling request 115 or 117 below -0.25), landing within 0.1 of the target's side offset and attaching to it the next tick. Test `leap_to_a_parallel_line` (a 15-unit gap lands after about 40 ticks, as the spec's flight table). Not traced on disc lines. 934 thread 21: with flags 3 to 5 clear, line 214 once per load when the active hero's hit points fall below 0.2 of the maximum (the script state now carries the maximum).
- **Dev menu redesign and eight save slots (this build's own tools; SPEC-0025, SPEC-0035 section 7 departed from on request):** the F1 developer menu is now a two-column panel in sections (Cheats, Time and camera, Travel; Hero, Items and money, Story). Each row is a button with its value and, for choices, < and > step buttons. It works from the keyboard, the controller (Select opens; D-pad picks and changes; LB/RB step by 10; South applies; East or Select closes; the closing press is swallowed so it doesn't reach the game) and the mouse (click a row to apply, the arrows to step). The save file now holds **eight** 0x3100-byte slots back to back (0x18800 bytes), a deliberate change from the spec's four at the player's request. A four-slot 0xC400-byte file still parses, with slots 4 to 7 empty; the picker draws the earliest run of rows (about four fit the page) that keeps the cursor's row visible. Tests: `layout_and_template` (eight-slot size, four-slot read, bad size) and `picker_cursor` (eight-slot moves). Item stacking was checked on the playtest's report: pickups stack, and the disc's carry limits (0 = none for potions, food, Motes and coins; 1 for equipment and most quest items) explain single copies. No change made. The scrolling picker was not screenshotted with eight filled slots.
- **Warp area names in the dev menu (this build's own tool; SPEC-0025 location names):** the warp row reads "Warp to <area>", and the warp note says the same. The area is the target world pack's map area (its type-1001 record) named by design table 5, the same lookup a save's location text uses. The host reads each pack from the disc the first time the warp cursor reaches it and caches the name. A pack with no map area shows plain "Warp". Checked by screenshot (909's first target, 678, reads "Farm Road").
- **Dev menu: current area and story flag quests (this build's own tool; SPEC-0051 quest rows):** the Travel title reads "now in <area>, <pack>" (the current pack's map area, named by design table 5). The story flag row is named by the quest whose step starts on that flag (else the one that ends on it), and shows the flag's number and state. Beneath it, a line gives the state and, for each step starting on the flag, the quest title and that step's objective text; a quest whose last step ends on the flag reads as finished. All of it comes from design table 1, the journal's quest rows (title, start flag, done flag, objective), read once when the menu opens. The keys help moved under the title so the window fits. `NEOPETS_DEV` gains `row=K`, `flag=N` and `quests` (the last prints each quest step's flags). Checked by screenshot: flag 108 reads "Herding Whinnies" with its corral objective, and 136 reads "Deliver Package".
- **Control bindings (this build's own feature; the original reads only the PS2 pad, SPEC-0035 section 4 for the menu words):** new `bindings.rs` adds a virtual PS2 pad with 24 inputs: the left stick's four directions, the right stick's four, and the 16 buttons. Each input takes two keyboard keys or mouse buttons; each button also takes one gamepad button. Gameplay (`step_sim`, `read_input`) and the front end (`menu_words`, `menu_held`) now read it instead of fixed keys. Mouse buttons are left out of the menu words, and the gamepad sticks still pass straight through. Mouse look: in play (front-end page PLAY or BOX_HOST, no developer menu, window focused) the cursor is locked and hidden, and mouse motion adds to the right stick, scaled by the sensitivity (1 to 20) and lifted past the PS2 dead zone. Y can be inverted; `NEOPETS_NO_GRAB` turns the lock off. New `controls_page.rs` is a Bevy UI page opened from a new Options row, "Keyboard & Gamepad", under Display. It has two tabs: Keyboard & Mouse (two slots per input, plus mouse camera, speed and invert) and Gamepad (one slot per PS2 button). It works by keyboard, gamepad or mouse, with fixed navigation keys. A capture waits up to 6 s; Esc cancels and Delete or North clears. A key bound twice moves to the new action; a gamepad button bound twice swaps. Every change saves to private/controls.txt, and each tab has a reset. The game's input is muted while the page is up and for 3 frames after it closes. Default keys keep the old layout, except: the arrows are now the D-pad (numpad 8/2/4/6 also), WASD moves, Z and C are L2 and R2, menu L1/R1 follow Q and Shift, Square adds right mouse and R3 adds middle mouse. `NEOPETS_TRACE_PAGE` gains `keys@n` and `pad@n`. Tests: `settings_round_trip`, `binding_takes_and_swaps`, `mouse_stick_lifts_past_the_dead_zone`, `tabs_list_every_input_once`. Checked by screenshots of the Options row and both tabs. Rebinding, mouse look and the cursor lock have not been tried by hand in this pass.
- **Display page restyle (this build's own page; no disc layout behind it):** the page keeps the Options background, logo, title and footer, and still uses the disc's own item font. The rows now sit on a dark rounded panel under a "Display" heading and a thin rule. Each row has its name on the left and its value right-aligned. The focused row lies on a purple bar that fades at both ends, with arrows beside its value. Under the rows, a line in the footer labels' smaller font says what the focused setting does. Characters the font lacks are dropped. A new `Draw::Shape` (a rounded panel, a fading bar, or a left or right arrow) is drawn procedurally at four pixels per canvas unit and cached by size. Checked by screenshot (`NEOPETS_TRACE_PAGE=display@60`).
- **Display page window (this build's own page; replaces the restyle above at the player's request):** the Display page is now a Bevy UI window in the Controls page's look, over the Options background with its item labels hidden. It has a row per setting, the value between < and > buttons, a Back row, and a line saying what the focused setting does. The front end's Display page still holds the cursor and the changes. Its rows now include Back (Cross on Back returns), and Select or Circle (Esc or the gamepad's B) go back as Triangle does. The window adds the mouse: hovering focuses a row, a click on < or > changes it, a click on the row steps it forward, and Back returns (`Flow::display_back`). The procedural `Draw::Shape` panel from the restyle is removed. A native-look version of the Controls page was tried and dropped at the player's request; the Controls window is unchanged. Checked by screenshots of both pages.
- **Graphics options (this build's own; the original draws none of this; new `graphics.rs`):** five new Display rows. **Anti-aliasing**: Off, MSAA 4x, SMAA (default) or FXAA; MSAA becomes SMAA while ambient occlusion is on. **Distant textures**: Sharp (default) or Original. World textures now carry mipmaps (2 x 2 averages weighted by alpha, so cut-out edges do not darken); Sharp samples them with 16x anisotropic filtering, and Original uses only the full-size level, as before. **Bloom**: Off, Soft (default) or Strong, on an HDR camera with a thresholded, additive bloom; tonemapping stays off, so colours are unchanged. **Colour**: Original, Vivid (default) or Rich. It is graded in a UI material shader (`SceneGrade`) where the 3D picture meets the window: saturation with vibrance, contrast, warmth and a soft vignette, in gamma space; Original passes the picture through untouched. **Ambient occlusion**: On (default) or Off. The game's surfaces are unlit, with lighting baked into vertex colours, so while AO is on the solid ones turn lit under an ambient light matched to the unlit colour. At roughness 1 with no reflectance, Bevy's ambient term is base x 0.4524 - 0.0024 for any view angle, times the light and the default exposure (EV100 9.7). Bevy's SSAO then shades only creases. The material stays one-sided for normals, because the strips' winding varies and a back-face flip turned half the ground dark; blended surfaces stay unlit. `NEOPETS_GFX=aa,textures,bloom,colour,ao` overrides the options for a run; `NEOPETS_GFX_LIT_ONLY` shows the lit surfaces without the occlusion pass, the calibration check. Screenshot comparisons in 909: lit-only matches unlit to the eye, and AO shades under the fence rails, at the cliff foot and under the rock. Tests: `mipmaps_halve_down_to_one_texel`, `transparent_texels_do_not_darken_the_average`. Frame rate with everything on was not measured.
- **All-areas sweep (tooling; no gameplay change):** `scripts/sweep.py <repo> <ticks> [packs]` runs the headless play trace for every world pack in the save's world-key list (printed by the new `NEOPETS_LIST_WORLDS`), from each of its first four entry records (the new `NEOPETS_TRACE_ENTRY=k`; the trace now prints the start-point count), walking and jumping. It flags exit codes, panics, NaN positions, falls with no ground below for a second or more, and runs that never move, judging each run only up to its first game over or door load. Results on 2026-10-27, with 3600 ticks each over 573 runs as Tor and 552 as Roberta, plus 18000 ticks over 33 runs in nine Act 1 areas: no panic, no crash, no NaN. Flagged falls were checked one by one. 683, 684, 718, 799 and 1130 are transition shells with no level at the start pose. In 1299, 1300, 879, 985 and 1344 the hero walks off the edge of the walkable ground; following SPEC-0018 he then stands on the 0.0 height outside the grid, which is asked as R-0110. "Never moved" runs start facing a wall, and 1081 ends in a death to the Spirit Swords. New tests: `menu_words_follow_the_bindings_and_skip_the_mouse` and `every_button_has_a_default_and_no_key_is_bound_twice`.
- **Grab-line slide details (SPEC-0033 Behavior 9 modes 0 and 1):** in the slide (mode 1) the hero requests 113 while Cross is held, else 112. The attach (mode 0) requests 112 unless the leap's falling clip (115 or 117) is playing, which it keeps. While Square is not pressed, cue 141 is kept playing on the main sound slot, started only when it is not already playing; it is not positional here, because the hero is the listener. Test: `attach_slide_and_let_go` checks request 113. Not built: the +0x270 lift while 113 plays (role not established) and the touched-flag exit to state 48, since this build's motion record has no touched flag. Not checked in play.
- **Touched flag and the line's long-fall exit (SPEC-0018 Slide response step 6; SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A branch 0; SPEC-0033 Behavior 9 test 4):** the hero's motion step now keeps the touched flag. It is cleared each step and set by any contact of the contact pass: a push out of a wall frame, box primitive, movable block or pushing sphere, or a block top's floor contact. Here the build's pushes stand in for the shared slide response, which is not built as such. On a grab line in the slide (mode 1) with no leap launched, the flag from the last step and the cooldown below 1 make the hero let go (the let-go's side effects) and then enter the long fall (state 48). The attach's 60-tick cooldown keeps this off for the first second on a line. Not used yet by state 47 (R-0106) or the block approach. Sweep: nine Act 1 areas still pass. Not traced on a disc line touching a wall.
- **Block climb approach and the touched flag (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51, state 58 sub-mode 0):** with the hero's touched flag from the last step set, the approach no longer steps 0.2 toward T. It puts the hero on T at once, with sub-mode 2 and request 134, as when within 0.2. Test: `fit_and_edge_states` (a touched approach snaps to the top less 1.2).
- **Eight-slot save list checked (this build's eight slots):** new diagnostic hooks: `NEOPETS_SAVE_FILE` reads and writes another save file instead of private/saves/savegame.bin; `NEOPETS_TRACE_SAVE_SLOT=k` makes `--save-test` write slot k of private/saves/trace.bin; `NEOPETS_TRACE_PAGE=save@n` opens the save screen; `NEOPETS_TRACE_PICKER_CURSOR=k` puts the picker's cursor on slot k after the scan. With all eight test slots filled and the cursor on slot 8, the save screen shows slots 5 to 8 with the cursor's row lit, checked by screenshot. The fourth row on the page shows no date line, from the SPEC-0035 line limit (the same in the four-slot list).
- **Important-item give in full (SPEC-0021 Important-item give and Pool lifetime):** the world-item pool now resets slots as the spec says. Taking a dropped item (finite lifetime) gives its id through the reset, before the pickup's own add. An expired drop gives at once and leaves its record attached, so the slot's next reuse gives again. Releasing a world's buffer (the pool of a world no longer resident) gives every dropped item still lying and every attached record. The host gives the important ids, count 1 each, capped at the carry limit. The pickup's own add now goes through the capped give too, where it used to add without the cap. So the six important ids with a carry limit other than 1 (8, 70, 78, 79, 80, 82) add 2 per pickup, capped: the spec's Black Feather vector reads 2, 4, 5. Tests: `important_range_and_expiry` (reuse gives again) and `slot_resets_give_dropped_items`. Not traced with a real important drop picked up (the 685 run met no spirit).
- **Render pass 1 (SPEC-0003 Material record, How a pass is drawn, UV modes; SPEC-0004 Two-pass batches):** the material reader now keeps each group's pass-1 program (0x54), UV mode (0x5C), UV-mode data scroll speeds (0x70, 0x74), and the second register set's CLAMP (0x08) and ALPHA (0x42). The model builder resolves the pass-1 TEX0 like pass 0's and gives a part of a two-pass group (mode 2, a pass-1 TEX0, a program other than 0xFFFF) its pass 1: texture, the batches' second-half UVs, UV mode, speeds and ALPHA. World scenes draw it as a second mesh over pass 0, with the same positions, normals and colours and its own UVs, gated like pass 0 by zone and with its own duplicate signature. New `pass1.rs` has a material and shader of its own. An environment map (mode 1) takes its UVs per pixel from the world-space reflection of the view (D = P - E, R = D - 2 (D . N) N, u = 0.5 (1 - R^z), v = 0.5 (1 + R^y); M the identity, E the camera). A scroll (mode 2) adds frac(s x N) each frame. The blend follows ALPHA: B zero with D the destination adds the source times As, or FIX / 128, or for C = Ad the source alpha again, since the destination alpha is not kept (an approximation). Anything else alpha-blends. Found by `NEOPETS_TRACE_PASS1`: scrolling water layers with 0x58 in 934, 1023, 1066, 696, 1121, 1300 and 1335, and environment-map passes with 0x48 in 1133 and 1152. Checked by screenshots of 934's pond, where pass 1 adds faint moving highlights; `NEOPETS_NO_PASS1` turns pass 1 off for comparisons. Not built: pass 1 on placed objects, characters, blocks, breakables and foliage (only world scenes and domes; the dome builder keeps layer A only), pass-0 environment maps (202 groups keep their stored UVs), the placed-object form of the environment map (SPEC-0046), the destination-alpha blend itself. The environment-map look was not checked by eye. Test: `alpha_register_kinds`.
- **Environment-mapped pass 0 and object pass 1 (SPEC-0003 UV modes, mode 1 on pass 0; How a pass is drawn):** a mesh part now records whether pass 0's UV mode is 1 (`MeshPart::env_map`). World-scene parts with it are drawn by the pass-1 shader in an opaque, alpha-masked mode, with UVs from the world-space reflection of the view instead of the stored ones. Found by `NEOPETS_TRACE_PASS1`: 8 parts in 934 (the pond, under its scrolling pass 1), 7 in 1023, 17 in 1066, 2 in 1098. Placed objects, movable blocks and breakables in a world now also draw their pass 1 as children, scrolls included. Their environment map is taken in world space, so the placed-object frame quirk of SPEC-0046 is not reproduced. `NEOPETS_NO_PASS1` turns both off. Checked by screenshots of 934's pond, which draws cleanly with the ripples reflected from the view. Not built: pass 1 and the pass-0 environment map on characters (skinned on the CPU) and foliage (held items and attachments got pass 1 later); these parts are left out of the ambient-occlusion depth prepass.
- **Hero shot world contact (SPEC-0036 Roberta's shots, Ground test and World contact; SPEC-0031 Weapon hits on the world):** a shot that ends on the ground (centre strictly below the probed height) now plays a weapon-bank cue at the shot, chosen from the ground entry's surface word with SPEC-0031's face-material table. The spec says only that the entry's surface code drives a ground-cue lookup, so the table is this build's reading. After the character test, a shot that hit no one tests the world. First, the first solid collider of the type-11 volumes disarms it: a gate that passes, no trigger bit for its kind (the shot pass fires triggers), solid class bit 0, and touched by the shot sphere through the existing `shot_hits`. The collider's surface byte (+0x11, now read) picks the cue, played at the collider. Otherwise the cell's frame records whose byte +0x4C is not 6 and that the shot's path crosses this tick (`crossed_by`, the record's segment test in place of the 0.25 swept sphere) disarm it, and the last match's byte +0x4D (now read) picks the cue. The cue plays once from the weapon bank. Test: `shot_world_cues` (the spec's vectors). Roberta sweep in six areas: no crash. Not checked by ear.
- **Thrown model for the flying sword (SPEC-0046 Held items step 1; SPEC-0012 Flying swords):** the thrown-model word now covers the Black Knight's sword as well as the Werelupe King's boomerang. While the sword is out of his hand (sub-states 0 to 4: attach, launch, orbit, homing, return), his slot 0 model is drawn at the sword's position, spinning like the boomerang, instead of in his hand. Not traced in the 989 fight.
- **Animated-object sounds (SPEC-0030 row 224; SPEC-0031 Animated-object sounds):** each animated object now has 3 sound slots. AddAnimObjSound fills the first empty one with the cue, start max(a, 0.00001) x D and end min(b, 0.99999) x D. Each advance in states 0, 2, 5 and 6, and each set time, checks the interval between the old and the new time, after the end clamp and the loop wrap: the first slot whose start lies in [lo, hi) plays its cue once (do-not-restart, area slot, radius 200), and the first whose end lies in [lo, hi] stops it, both at the object. The object's rest position stands in for its reference centre (+0x180). Only the current world's objects sound. The calls are built where the module specs list them: 1392 (objects 20, 22, 24 with cue 11 from 0.5; 29, 28, 27, 26, 30, 31, 33, 34, 32 with cue 12 from 0.3), 1394 (3 and 4, cue 13), 1130 (11, 7 to 10 with cue 10; 23, 24, 12 to 18, 20 to 22, 1, 2 with cue 8; both states), 1248 (3, 4, 5, 2, 0, 1 with cue 16 from 0.1), 1171 (levers 11, 13, 15 with cue 7 from 0.5) and 1391 (object 0, cue 6). Not built: 909's two calls (the spec gives only the integer pairs (6, 7) and (7, 6)), 758, 1081, 1247, 1249, 1381 and 1398 (their calls are not listed), and 1090's hazard calls (silent by the spec). Test: `animated_object_sound_vectors` (the spec's vectors). Sweep of the six areas: no crash. Not checked by ear.
- **Sweep caveat (tooling):** a 1248 run that passed in the first sweep fell off a ledge in a later one. Bisecting showed the build from before today's changes gives both outcomes across repeated identical runs: at tick 1000 the hero stood at x -24.07 on some runs and -30.72 on others. So the headless trace is not fully deterministic, most likely from region loads finishing on different ticks, and the difference is not a regression. `scripts/sweep.py` now says to compare flagged runs across repeats.
- **Texture flipbooks (SPEC-0003 Sub-texture frame addressing, UV mode 3):** the material reader keeps pass 0's texture animator kind (byte 0x2B) and grid record (0x00). A part of a variant-1 group carries its flipbook: count, rows, columns, divisor and the texture size from TEX0. Each frame the part's own copy of its material is offset to the cell at the render frame counter (the play tick, which holds under a pause, as the cells do): L = min(count, rows x columns), index floor(N / D) mod L, column and row from the index, offset (column x cell width, row x cell height) over the texture size. This stands in for the GS region repeat, so the mesh's UVs are taken to lie in cell 0. World scenes and the objects, blocks and breakables of a world have it. In 1248 the 6 parts of group 1 (32 cells, 4 x 8, divisor 1) step one cell per tick; at tick 60 cell 28 gives offset (0.5, 0.75) (`NEOPETS_TRACE_FLIP`). Variant 2 shows cell 0, as the spec's clean side says, so its stored UVs stand. Not built: resource 9 group 102 (an item model; item draws do not take it yet). Test: `flipbook_cells`. R-0111 asks for the AddAnimObjSound calls the module specs do not list (909's pairs, 758, 1081, 1247, 1249, 1381, 1398).
- **Item flipbooks (SPEC-0003 Sub-texture frame addressing):** world items and the item showcase now carry each part's flipbook with their per-instance material, so resource 9's group 102 (16 cells, 4 x 4, divisor 2) steps on whichever item model uses it. Not checked in play: which item ids use that group was not traced.
- **Foliage distances and far sprites (SPEC-0060 sections 3 and 4; Display option "Distant foliage", Full by default):** the mesh walk now keeps the first distance-cull node's floats (+0x50, +0x54, +0x58, +0x64) on the model. `neopets_sim::foliage::Distances` builds a patch's far F, switch S, out band O and fade band W, from the overrides or else that node, with the no-far-object widening. Its `weights(d)` gives the near and far weights of section 3. Test `foliage_distances_and_weights`: the spec's vector (O 50, W 50, F 150, S 80), an override set, and the near-only and two-model curves. The far sprite models are now built and spawned for the families that also have a near model (trees of key 25, reeds of key 8), tinted by each plant's colour word. Every far sprite turns each frame to face the camera, upright about the vertical (the quad's face is X for 203 and 283, Z for 199); the original's screen-aligned sprite is taken as upright here. With the option on Original, a plant part shows only while its patch's centre lies within F and its weight for its role (near or far) is above 0. The weights switch parts on and off; they are not used as opacity, so there is no gradual fade. On Full (the default, as before), every near model shows at any distance and only the far-only families (grass, key-13 trees) draw their sprites. Count from 934's start (`NEOPETS_TRACE_FOLIAGE_LOD`): Full 2,569 near parts and 0 sprites; Original 725 near parts and 254 sprites. `NEOPETS_GFX` takes a sixth value for the option, and `NEOPETS_TRACE_FOLIAGE_MODELS` prints the far model bounds and each patch's distances. Not built: the far batches' room and group limits, grass sway and the per-entry alpha trunc(A x b).
- **Second pass on held items and attachments** (SPEC-0003 two-pass groups): the hero's held items (sync_hero_items) and character attachments (sync_attachments) now spawn the same pass-1 children as world parts, with UV scroll where set. Checked by trace: the sword model 547 draws its 3 two-pass parts; in a screenshot the sheathed hilt gains its shine. Unverified: side by side against the original.
- **Movie player shell** (SPEC-0026 Movie name table, Movie queue, Movies, Movie skip; SPEC-0008 Movie files): `neopets-data::movie` reads the 92-entry name table from the boot program at runtime and each file's Bink header (synthetic test `movie_header_fields`). PlayFMV now queues in call order (`GameState::movie_queue`) as well as counting the movie as played, and the played words are written to and read from the save slot. The app's movie step plays the queue one movie at a time at the file's frame rate, suspends the game loop while one plays, and applies the skip rule once per frame (`neopets_sim::movie::Skip`, test `movie_skip_rule`: arm on a free frame, five held frames of Cross or Start, end on the next check). Checked: `NEOPETS_TRACE_MOVIE=88,1` plays ACTINTRO/ACT1 (261 frames, 29.97 fps) then ACT1/101A (2,585 frames, 30 fps) in order. Placeholder, not verified gameplay: each movie is a black screen with its name and time left, because no spec describes the Bink stream (R-0112, asked: container index, video and audio bitstreams, codec table locations). Not built: the reload-world hand-off after the queue (the attract pair and paused sound came later).
- **Attract movies and sound during movies** (SPEC-0026 Attract, Movies): the attract page queues movie 0 then movie 86 as it leaves for the title, so boot runs logo, legal page, the two movies, title. Each movie's played word is now set as it starts, whoever queued it. Checked: `--play --title` reaches movie 0 (ACT1/100A, 990 frames, 33.0 s) after the logo and legal pages. Approximation: the original stops the game's sound before the queue and the reload hand-off restarts it; this build has no hand-off yet, so the game's sound is paused while movies play and resumes after. Pictures and movie audio still wait on R-0112.
- **Level-script movie calls** (SPEC-0026 Movies; SPEC-0030 PlayFMV; the module specs): a new `Action::PlayFmv` queues a movie at its place in a script sequence, and the PlayFMV calls the modules had skipped are now made: 909 (88 then 1, the new-game intro), 691 (3), 753 (33 on the teleport with 68 clear, 36 in trigger 1 while unplayed, 37 at the event end), 1327 (27, 29, 90, 30 at the Act 3 start), 1130 (callback 7 on trigger 0: 50 once, when the hero is not occupied), 1344 (22, 24), 886 (32; its Init now tests HasMoviePlayed(30)), 1335 (20, 25, 21; and the line-37 thread: once 22 has played, line 37 every 3,608 ticks until 239), 1139 (49), 1327's target practice end (19), 1300 (79; 83 before the credits), 1411 (59, 60, 69, 71, 72), 714 (48), 1371 (61), 1184 (40), 1098's rest (7, 10) and 1382 (70). The sites that already counted a movie as played (989's 46 and 47, 1122's 52 to 54, 1174's 16, 89 and 18, 1432's 78, 80 and 85, and others) now queue it too. Checked: `--play --flag 707` plays movie 88 at the start; 461 sim tests pass (two updated for the new actions). Not built: the return-world reload (the modules still load the return world at once), the fade before 83, and 1098's triggers 4 to 6 (movies 8, 9, 15) with their sequences.
- **Castle story triggers** (SPEC-0045 1098 Story triggers): Init now registers triggers 4, 5, 6, 9 and 10. Trigger 5 (203 clear): the letterboxed scene with cameras 25 to 27, entity 22's lines 43 and 45 and entity 36's line 44, entity 44 to locator 13, flags 203 and 151, movie 8. Trigger 4 (203 set, 204 clear): 204, movie 9, navmesh 10 for entities 23, 24, 25 and 36, the four placed at locators 96 to 99 and walked to 90 to 93, camera 30, slot A loaded with 1105, the hero to locator 95. Trigger 6 (216 set, 218 clear): 218, movie 15, the hero to locator 11 and the load of eW 127. Trigger 9 (s 28, 622 clear, 202 set): 622, objects 10 and 11 played, object 14 off, camera 6, entity 44 and the hero walked. Trigger 10, on every fourth pass inside: the flag-193 cutscene (lines 19 to 23 with entity 35, cameras 15, 13, 16, 14, 11, the forced slot-B word while entity 35 walks off) and 194, or with 205 the camera-14 line 3 and 206. Entities 35 and 36 now run the same cutscene from their talk. Test `castle_story_triggers`. Medium (the spec's own confidence): the order inside each cutscene. Left out: the placements of entities 22, 36 and 44 in trigger 5 (no locators given); fade-ins after movies 8 and 9 stand in for the reload. Not built: the switches (triggers 17 to 20). Not run in play yet.
- **Castle switches** (SPEC-0045 1098 Story triggers, triggers 17 to 20; Medium in the spec): Init registers the four switches. Inside each, while its object (15, 17, 19 or 21) has not been swapped, the Square context 4 shows; a press, when the switch is usable, walks the hero to locator 100 to 103, plays hero animation 140 and the object, and swaps it for 16, 18, 20 or 22; 17 also sets flag 15 and clears the teleport-6 word; 18 plays camera 7, 19 camera 4, 20 object 7 and camera 5; the camera then returns. Not usable: line 18. Entering 19 stops effects 9 and 10. Test: `castle_story_triggers` (switch 18 swaps once; with 167 set, line 18). Assumed: usable means s 30 or 31, or flag 167 clear, for every index; the spec ties the test to the index without saying how (R-0113, asked with the objects' start state and whether the swap persists). Not run in play yet.
- **More movie calls** (SPEC-0026 Movie play sites; SPEC-0041 951, SPEC-0045 1023 and 909, SPEC-0043 1335): 951's first entry plays movie 2 as it sets 120 and gives item 15; 1023's chase catch plays movie 4 after item 32; 909's stage 2 (147 and 145 clear) plays movie 5 with 145; 1335's teleport 1 plays movie 26 before its return world loads. Of the 72 indices the spec's play-site table lists, the remaining ones without a call are 6 (951; the spec gives no site detail), 23 (1324's plate puzzle, not built) and 65 (1394; its site is not described). Older STATUS notes that listed movies as not built were updated.
- **Roberta's shot visuals** (SPEC-0034 Roberta's shot visuals; SPEC-0036 Roberta shot projectile): each launched shot gets its three effect ids from the boot tables read at runtime (`neopets_sim::roberta::VisualTables`: the flight tables by kind at 0x3154C8, 0x3154E8 and 0x315508, the full-release table 0x315440, the Mote aura table 0x3153D0 as the second flight layer, the impact tables 0x315478 by Mote and 0x315528, 0x315548 and 0x315568 by kind; 402 and 403 for any other kind; none for a full release with Mote 161, 162, 164, 165, 166, 169, 171 or 173, whose power-attack object supplies the visuals). The flight effects follow the shot every pass; when the shot ends by any route they stop and the impact effect plays once at its last position. Test `shot_visual_ids` (synthetic tables). Checked: in the 1122 fight trace (`NEOPETS_TRACE_FX=1`) each quick shot flies with effect 1101 and ends with impact 991. Assumed: the shot step is 0 for every shot (its mapping to the sub-states is not given, R-0114). Not checked in a window.
- **Placeholder shot markers trimmed** (SPEC-0029 Behavior 9 Visual update, Spit appearance): the yellow cube that marked every live projectile is no longer drawn for a pool slot whose flight effects (ids 0 or 1) are live, nor for the Undead Draik's spits (their flight and impact effects are built). It stays only for pool shots with no flight effect, which are the common enemies' missiles: their trail model part and effect table are still open (R-0082). Not checked in a window.
- **Spawn by key on a break** (SPEC-0048 Break effect step 3; SPEC-0029 Behavior 31): a breakable whose spawn key (+0x54) is 0 or more now runs a world-level spawn by key when it breaks: at its break position, with its facing (the matrix's third axis) and the arrival flag, so the bound character enters with animation 82. On the disc 20 entries in 7 packs have a key (`NEOPETS_TRACE_BREAK_KEYS=1` lists them at load): 732 (26, 33: key 1960), 947 (12, 16: 1965), 1074 (eight entries: 1950 and 1941), 1080 (5), 1096 (28, 29, 33, 36, 37), 1247 (19) and 1248 (19: 1816). Checked: `NEOPETS_TRACE_SMASH` breaks of 732/26, 947/12 and 1074/10 and 34 each bind a reserved character of the key at the break point. Still not built: the piece effects (their drawn transform is open in the spec), the timed effect (+0x5A), the persistent broken bits (they never change the outcome on disc), Tor's recoil on an element mismatch and the script hook.
- **Element recoil** (SPEC-0048 Element recoil, Break routes step 2; SPEC-0036 Tor's arm table sub-state 13): Tor's attack gains sub-state 13, the sword bounce: it requests animation 26 (restarted) and ends when that clip finishes; a request while recoiling does nothing (test `recoil_bounce`). When Tor's swing meets an intact breakable whose element is neither 0, 7 nor his slot-2 Mote's kind, the hero melee route now requests it, with no break and no cue; entering it plays voice cue 13 (material 4) and the rumble (preset 2, 0.8 s, 0.6), as the host already did for sub-state 13. Roberta has no reaction. `NEOPETS_TRACE_BREAK_KEYS` now also lists element-sealed entries (732/1 water, 947/1 wind, 934/1 water, 1074/5 shadow, among others). Checked: in pack 732, Tor with no Mote swinging at breakable 1 (element 4) enters sub-state 13 on tick 48, plays action 26 until tick 85 and returns to the stance. Not built: the blade recoil off solid volumes (SPEC-0028) and its other materials.
- **Equipped Motes in the save** (SPEC-0025 Equipment Mote words, Loading step 12g): saving now writes each hero's armour, shield and weapon Mote words at 0x30A4 + 12h + 4i (-1 for none) instead of -1 for all; loading passes them through the equip setter with no cue, and a changed word of the active hero re-arms its equipment-panel timer (240). The `--save-test` line now prints the Mote words. Checked: `NEOPETS_TRACE_MOTE_AT=10:2:163 --give 163 --save-test` writes [[-1, -1, 163], [-1, -1, -1]]. Not checked: a load in the window. Not built: Tor's Mote follow-up on load (Medium in the spec), the parked hero's status timers and the status words, the per-world tables and the count table. The save and load doc comments were brought up to date (the stash, petpet, pools and swap mode were already saved). Asked R-0115: the blade recoil's busy gate, the world-face test and the other hooks' tests (SPEC-0028 Blade recoil).
- **Save prompt and the Square fallback** (SPEC-0025 Save points, Order against the hero's Square handling): a "Save Game" caption shown in the pass now also stops the chooser's last fallback (Square with no target enters the sneak or sheathes the weapon), as it already stopped Feed petpet and Read Book; so a Square press at a save point opens the save screen without Tor also crouching. Stale notes corrected: the save-point glow (400 and 404), cue 0xA8 and the save-complete cue 0xA9 are built. `scripts/sweep.py` takes `NEOPETS_SWEEP_EXE` to run a copy of the exe while the build goes on.
- **All-areas sweep, 2026-10-28 (tooling; no gameplay change):** after this run of work (the movie player and calls, castle 1098, Roberta's shot visuals, break spawns, element recoil, Mote save), `scripts/sweep.py` over 573 runs at 600 ticks as Tor: no panic, crash or NaN; 8 falls, all known cases from the 2026-10-27 sweep (transition shells 683, 718 and 799; edge walk-offs in 879, 985, 1300 and 1344, R-0110). Movies queued by a level script do not block the headless trace (the movie player is part of the window).
- **Module 990, partly (SPEC-0042 990; Low for the module overall):** this module had no code. Init posts value 2 for eW 78, sets objects 15, 13, 17, 19, 21 and 23 up at their end and 0, 1, 2, 7, 3, 4, 5, 11, 9 and the record objects 39 to 50 at their start, and registers handler F on trigger 2 and handler P on triggers 3 to 8; 990's objects are now drawn only once a script sets them up. P (High in the spec): triggers 3 to 5 show context 1 while object 12, 14 or 16 is inactive, and a press swaps the pair (12/13, 14/15, 16/17) and plays camera 5, 4 or 6; triggers 6 to 8 always show context 4, and a press negates the lever word (-1 at load), plays sounds 6 and 7 and object 0's animation at that speed. F (Medium): camera 7, the hero's long fall, 1 s, the normal camera. Test `module_990_prompts` (the spec's test vector). Checked: the sweep runs all four 990 entries clean; a screenshot shows the dungeon drawn. Not built: the press paths' hero walk and animations 139 and 140 (Low order), the switch-selected sequence (Medium), the record thread, the animated locators of objects 39 to 50, handler S on triggers 16 to 87 (its body is open in the spec) and the animation-linked sounds.
- **Practice health refill** (SPEC-0044 1327 Target-practice thread, steps 231 and 233): Roberta's hit points are now refilled to her maximum on every tick of step 231 (the held shot) and of step 233 until AI 0 is dead (a new `Action::RefillHealth`). The Act 1 playtest guide's Act 2 line is updated: the movies now play as skippable black screens, and the refill is built. Not traced in play.
- **Roberta's shot launch, per shot** (SPEC-0036 Roberta shot projectile, Launch steps 1 to 4; High): each shot of a burst now picks its own target and aim (modes 0 and 1 the lock, modes 2 to 5 a random visible character within 60), and that target is stored as the shot's homing target. Before, the target was chosen once per burst and every shot was launched with no target, so homing shots never steered. Modes 2 to 5 now fan the direction: d1 = r1 s + (1 - |r1|) d, d2 = r2 up + (1 - |r2|) d1 with r1, r2 uniform in [-1, 1] and s = unit(d x up), not renormalised (`neopets_sim::roberta::fan`; test `shot_fan_vectors`, the spec's three vectors). Checked: the 1122 fight trace (quick shots, mode 0) launches on the same ticks as the previous build. Not traced: a full release with a homing row in play. The Mote power-attack objects' visuals stay unbuilt: their effect arguments are fixed values the spec does not give (SPEC-0034 section 13).
- **Request status bookkeeping:** `specs/REQUESTS.md` still listed R-0080 to R-0102 as Open, though the 2026-10-27 spec update answered them (commit a50b7e3); twenty rows are now marked answered. The open requests are R-0104 to R-0115: the Rope Gun (R-0104), the 1393 guard rooms (R-0105), R-0106 to R-0108, Roberta's head and the 1148 desk (R-0109), level edges (R-0110), the unlisted AddAnimObjSound sites (R-0111), the Bink decoder (R-0112), the 1098 switches (R-0113), Roberta's shot step (R-0114) and the blade recoil (R-0115). Also blocked by the specs: the leach's "magic-empty notification" (SPEC-0011, SPEC-0029) is named but never described.
- **Boss-fight refills and SetEntityInvulnerable** (SPEC-0030 rows 165 and 132; SPEC-0041 800 Stage A, 985 CB1 and the 1394 boss-room return; SPEC-0045 1300 routine 44): three BossFightTweakHero calls at stage 0 were missing and now refill the heroes: 800's stage A (instance 2), 985's arrival with flag 214 clear (instance 1) and 1394's boss-room return (instance 12). (Stage-1 calls do nothing, so the comments that listed them as unbuilt needed no code.) SetEntityInvulnerable is a new action that sets a character's invulnerable flag; while set the character is skipped by hero swings, shots and the Mote walks, like the pet-like characters (`Character::unhittable`). 1300's routine 44 now sets it on entity 76. Not traced in play.
- **DisableInvisibility** (SPEC-0030 row 121; SPEC-0041 1414 Init; SPEC-0043 1122 trigger 5 waves): an enemy now carries the invisibility-off word; once set, an invisible kind (Display in Default 0) is no longer vetoed as a hit or lock candidate, and SetAI_Agro sends it to state 2 instead of the alerted search 21. The new action follows each SetAI_Agro at the spec's sites: 1414's entities 0 and 1, and every AI of the three 1122 waves (11 to 13, 14, 15 and 13, 17 and 18). The spec counts 25 disc sites; the other module specs do not name theirs. Not traced in play.
- **Tor's Mote glow** (SPEC-0046 Hero-item effect matrices; SPEC-0023 section 25; SPEC-0003 CPU vertex animator 3; SPEC-0004 Multi-packet leaves): with Tor selected and a Mote in his weapon word (2) or shield word (1), the glow model 299 + (weapon id - 98) or 307 + (shield id - 106), from resource 9's children 16 to 28, is drawn at the held item's own drawn matrix (sheathed or in the hand, with its rescale), unlit and additive, coloured by the Mote's row of the table at 0x2DCA60 (read at runtime), with animator 3's U scroll (+0.06) and alpha pulse 64 + 186 (1 + sin p) / 2, p += 0.2, both advanced once per drawn frame here (the original advances them per mesh draw). Roberta has none. To draw these models the mesh reader now decodes packet-kind-1 leaves: the even-parity draw packet's offset at packet + 0x3C is relative to the packet (on disc the two copies fill the payload's end), and its batches use a new layout of three V4-32 arrays (position, a colour array that is zero on disc, UV). `NEOPETS_TRACE_MOTES` now also works in the window. Checked: Tor with sword 101 and Fire Mote 160 shows a red glow along the sheathed blade (screenshot). Not checked: the shield glow, the other Motes' colours, the drawn sword. The water leaves' own use of animator 3 elsewhere is not built.
- **Equipment panel counts and hero icons** (SPEC-0035 9c predicate slots 14, 17, 19, 20 and 52; SPEC-0023 sections 5 and 11): the HUD's equipment panels now test the live game: slots 17, 19 and 20 pass while the owned count of the Mote in the active hero's equipment word 2, 0 or 1 is above 0, and their value texts show that count; slots 14 and 52 pass for the active hero (Tor or Roberta), so Roberta's panel icons (robe, magic shield, wand) show instead of Tor's when she is selected. Before, 17 to 20 were always false and Tor's icons were always used. Not checked on screen: the opening talk hides the HUD in the screenshots taken; a screenshot does show the Mote glow outlining the sheathed sword.
- **Journal-notification icon and D-pad hint** (SPEC-0023 sections 8 and 9; SPEC-0024 Table 2): a progress flag whose table-2 row has a journal kind, rising for the first time, sets the journal-notification flag (phase 0); while it is set the phase grows by 0.15 a tick and icon 0xDF pulses with alpha round(64 (1 + P(t))), t the folded phase, keeping the series quirk at the wrap (25, 18, 15, 14, 17). Opening the quest journal clears it. L3 sets the D-pad hint's timer D to 240 with the other HUD timers; D falls by 1 a tick, and icon 0xBB is drawn at alpha 128 while D >= 30, then ramps to 32 (round(D x 96 x 0.033333335 + 32)); opening the pause page clears D. Both icons hide while the quick panel is open. Test `dpad_and_journal_icons` (the spec's values and the quirk run). Not checked on screen; the fade and letterbox gate of both icons is not applied (only the quick-panel one).
- **Hero-switch portrait swing** (SPEC-0023 section 12; SPEC-0035 9c predicate slots 57 to 60): every swap request now starts the swing (`neopets_sim::hud::Swing`): side s = 1 from Tor, the available words (the target's 0 when the request is refused or the target is dead), the active flag under the in-play controls gate, and for an accepted request the half-turn from -0.5236 or 2.617993 at pi/30 a tick, the blend from 0.5 rising or 1.0 falling by 1/60, a 2 s hold (4 s and no swing when refused). The HUD places Tor's group 0x2A at angle a with scale b and Roberta's 0x2B at a + pi with 1.5 - b on the ellipse round(Px + 0.8 R cos t - qx k), round(Py + R sin t - qy k) around pivot 0x29 (R = width / 2; the pivots are the nodes' authoring fields +0x1C and +0x1E, which the front-end reader now keeps). Predicates: 60 (the swing active and swap mode 2), 57 and 59 (each hero's available word; 0 draws the 20, 20, 20 overlay), 58 (the hero given by the argument is dead: its dead icon). Test `portrait_swing` (31 to 33 placements, the end angle, the end blend 0.483, a refused request's 4 s hold). Checked: in pack 1300 (swap mode 2), a chord on tick 150 shows Roberta's portrait large and Tor's small at the upper right once the swap's flash clears (screenshot). Approximations: the controls gate here is state not 0x19, no box or menu and no swap or door job (group A of SPEC-0053 3.12 is not tested); exact cos and sin replace the series (the spec allows it).
- **The Mote panel** (SPEC-0021 Second quick panel and Mote equipping; SPEC-0023 section 24; SPEC-0035 9c slots 15, 16 and 37): a Down edge with both quick panels closed (and not in action state 0x19 or a dialogue box) opens the Mote picker with cue 0x9A; while it is open its handler takes the pad: Triangle closes it; Cross equips the selection into word 0 (armour), Square into word 1 when a shield is held, Circle into word 2 when a weapon is held (buttons win over directions); an owned Mote not already in the word is equipped (cue 0xA3 and the glow, through the existing equip), anything else clears the word (cue 0xA4 when it held one); a direction moves the selection through the boot direction table at 0x2DCE58 (read at runtime), with the spec's column rule, and plays cue 0x9A. Each call re-arms the three equipment panels. Opening the first panel closes it. The HUD draws node 392 while it is open (slot 37), each Mote's icon while it is owned (16) and the highlight on the selection (15). `neopets_sim::quick::MotePanel`, test `mote_panel_vectors` (the spec's direction vectors on a synthetic table, the equip, re-press clear, shieldless Square and No Mote clear). Not built: the panel's label text (count and name at (30, 404)), the icon tweens, the camera-mode hiding. Not checked in a window (the traces have no D-pad hook).
- **Mote panel on screen:** a new diagnostic hook, `NEOPETS_TRACE_DOWN="tick,..."`, gives a D-pad Down edge on those play ticks in the window. Checked: in pack 1300 with Motes 160, 162 and 166 held, a Down on tick 100 opens the Mote panel: the ring of fourteen Mote cells draws, with icons only on the three owned Motes, and the equipment panels show their Circle, Cross, Square and Triangle prompts (screenshot).
- **Mote panel label, shortcuts and the HUD busy gate** (SPEC-0021 Second quick panel item 5; SPEC-0053 Where the chord sits; SPEC-0023 sections 4.1, 8 and 9): the Mote panel's label (node 393, the value text bound to variable 11, at canvas (30, 404)) shows the owned count of the selected Mote, and nothing for "No Mote"; the name beside it is not drawn (its format in the label is not given). The D-pad Left (map) and Right (journal) shortcuts no longer run while the Mote panel is open (before, Left with the panel open opened the map). The journal icon and the D-pad hint now also hide while the letterbox or a screen fade is up and while the Mote panel is open. `NEOPETS_TRACE_DOWN` and the new `NEOPETS_TRACE_LEFT` fire once on the first frame at or past their tick. Checked: in pack 1300 with Nova (172) held, Down on tick 100 and Left on tick 110 select Nova: its cell glows and the label reads its count, 200 (screenshot).
- **Mote panel equip in play:** `NEOPETS_TRACE_CIRCLE="tick,..."` gives a Circle edge (once, at or past the tick) and `NEOPETS_TRACE_MOTE_PANEL=1` prints the panel's inputs and results. Checked in packs 1023, 1174 and 1327 with Nova (172) and sword 101: Down, Left and Circle select Nova and equip it into word 2; the weapon panel then shows the word's count, 100 (screenshot in 1174). Open: the weapon panel's Mote icon shows a Fire Mote face rather than Nova's (the icon-by-id art, R-0020). In pack 1300 the hero is held by a script at that point, so the hero's buttons (Circle) do not reach the panel there.
- **HUD icon tweens** (SPEC-0023 section 11.1): `neopets_sim::hud::Tween` moves a node from one canvas point to another at a fixed speed (800 fly-in, 100 fly-out), its colour moving linearly over T = distance / speed, a step of 1/60 a tick, positions and channels rounded half away from zero (alpha limited to 128), snapping to the end at T; the pool holds six and a new tween on the same node replaces the old. The host posts requests: the quick-item marker set to a chosen entry (the selected-item icon 0xA1 flies in from that cross icon, fading in from alpha 0) and a Mote equipped from the Mote panel (the word's equipment icon, keys 164, 166 and 162 for words 0, 1 and 2, flies in from the selection's panel cell); the HUD view turns them into tweens from the nodes' canvas positions and draws the moved node at the tween's position and alpha. Test `icon_tweens` (the spec's quick fly-in and Mote fly-out vectors, replacement). Not built: the Mote fly-out of a replaced or cleared Mote (the spec does not name the node it moves). Not checked on screen (a fly-in lasts about nine ticks).
- **Item showcase, modes 1 to 3** (SPEC-0021 Item showcase; Medium for the screen anchoring): a pickup whose item has no showcase text, or one made while the hero is busy or a dialogue box is up, now starts the screen-anchored showcase at (0.5, 0.6, 5.0) with scale 0 (before, it showed nothing; the full mode 4 is unchanged). Each tick the angle advances by 2 pi / 5 rad/s; mode 1 grows the scale by 1.5 dt (coins and Tal, 135 to 144, stop at 0.4 and shrink; other items slide by (+0.5, -0.5) dt and stop at 0.8 to hold), mode 2 holds 1.3 s, mode 3 shrinks by 2.5 dt while sliding right and ends below 0. The model is placed at E + z F + 640 (x - 0.5)(z / 512) R + 480 (y - 0.5)(z / 512) U from the camera (the spec's minus signs flipped for this build's right and up axes, which the PS2 rows point the other way), turned by the angle, given the camera's orientation and scaled. `NEOPETS_TRACE_SHOWCASE` now also works in the window. Checked: in pack 1300 a coin (135) spins small near the screen centre (screenshot). Not built: the showcase effect (scale 1.5), the cached 640/480 factors quirk; the item is depth-tested like the world, so a camera closer than 5 units hides it behind the hero.
- **Special-victim hit cues** (SPEC-0031 Creature blocks and animation sounds): a Tor swing hit on a character of kind 240 (Black Knight), 480 (Undead Draik), 405 (Dark Knight) or 1941, 1942, 1949 and 1950 (the skeletons; the record's kind at +0xAC) now plays that creature's "impact special" cue on its block at its position: block 365 cue 34, 391 cue 1, 355 cue 15, 361 cue 22 (nothing when the block is not held); any other victim and every Roberta hit keep weapon cue 14, and attack state 10 keeps power cue 2. Checked: in pack 1074 the skeletons (kinds 1941, 1942, 1950) name block 361 and it sits in a creature slot (`NEOPETS_TRACE_SLOTS=1` prints the slots and each character's kind and blocks). The block is taken from the table, not from the per-kind remembered index of the spec (they agree on the disc data seen).
- **Low-magic warning** (SPEC-0036 Low-health warning; SPEC-0031 Cue use table): beside the low-health cue 157, cue 158 now plays on the main slot every tick, with do-not-restart, while the active hero's magic is below 0.15 of its maximum. Not checked by ear.
- **Coin bounce cue** (SPEC-0021 Coins; SPEC-0031 Cue use table, cue 167): a coin's ground contact that bounces (its speed after the -0.65 restitution is at least the 1.0 stop speed) now plays cue 0xA7 on the main slot at the coin. The item pool reports the bounce points (`ItemPool::bounces`) and the host drains them each tick. Tests unchanged (items 8 pass). Not checked by ear.
- **Element-sealed chests (SPEC-0048 Element gate on hits).** Script groups of kind 2 now set up at world load (object 0 drawn, object 1 drawn stopped; once the key row is set, both breakables off, object 1 hidden, effect 0 stopped). A hit on the chest body only recoils Tor; a hit on the seal passes when the seal's element is 0, matches the slot-2 Mote's kind, or the Mote is Nova, and otherwise recoils. A pass runs the open sequence as a script thread: body made active, seal broken, effect 0 stopped, weapon away, camera 0, main-slot cue 182, the object swap and loop, the display-mask steps 0x10000/0x20000/0x40000, effect 791 at X + 2.9 d, the body broken, hero animations 192 and 186 around camera 1, the reward drop and key row, then the normal camera. The trigger's repeated fires show context prompt 29 (elements differ) or 30 until Square is held. New actions MainCue and EffectAt. Unverified: not yet watched in game; the HUD reset, the reward's 2.2 d launch point (the lock chest's 2.4 d is used) and pair 1's character are not built.
- **Thorn hazards (SPEC-0011 Main-loop launches, Thorn hits; SPEC-0029 Layout 1a).** New `neopets_sim::thorn`: the five-record hazard pool. On the first tick a thorn shot's (kind 16) pool slot is free again, a live Malice (class 13, kind selector 0) claims the first free record at the shot's last position, its height from the ground probe started 8 above (Medium-Low); all five in use spawns nothing. A hazard deals the thorn damage (word 0x2DAB80, 10) on contact with the hero's body (radius word 0x2DCB20) and waits 120 ticks; after its 60-tick growth each melee swing whose weapon volume (radius 1.0, length 2.5 from the grip) reaches it counts one strike, once per swing (a serial bumped with each hit-list clear), and the strike count from ThornAttackMaxHits (0x2DAB84, 3) removes it. The pool clears on world load. Unverified: not watched in the fight; the hazard is not drawn (the spec names no model or effect) and its strike counting is Medium in the spec. Tests: three swings after growth remove it, a swing during growth does not, contact re-hits after 120 ticks, the sixth spawn fails.
- **Immunity block one-shots (SPEC-0036 Status display and cues, Immunity block).** `Statuses::immunity_block` gives the one-shot of a curse, poison or slow that an active immunity stops (0xC2, 0xC3, 0xC4). Every hero status apply from a script, an enemy hit, a shot, a poison attack or a region character's status hit now goes through `hero_status`, which starts that one-shot at the hero (at the origin, as the status display's effects; R-0045) before the apply. Consumable buffs and the save restore are left direct (they never apply curse, poison or slow). Test: the block effect for poison under its immunity, none otherwise. Unverified: not watched in game.
- **Counter-attack choice (SPEC-0038 Behavior 14; Layout section 5).** The monster rows' Counter Attack Trigger (+0x22C + 4k) and Counter Attack Chance (+0x23C + 4k) are read with the attack slots. Every agro tick, right after the normal choice and with no attackable, recovery or outer gate, the walk over slots 1 to 4 keeps the last slot that is in use, cooled down (below 1), inside its strict trigger band and melee arc, and whose code holds: 1 the active hero's recover window (table-B bit 0x800 on one of the hero's playing clips this tick), 2 that window in outer 5 or 6 (after a dodge), 3 in outer 7 or 8 (after a block), 4 the sequencer recovery (outer 2, sequencer state 5), 6 the hit reaction (outer 10). When that slot is not already selected, one roll int[0, 100] below its chance selects it, draws its cooldown and starts the sequencer in state 0 (or 6 with a charge type); the distances are not redrawn. Not built: code 5 (the blocked recoil with the hero flag bit of value 4; the flag word's meaning is open, R-0117 filed). Test: code 6 in the hit reaction, code 1 only with the recover window, chance 0 never. Unverified: not watched in a fight.
- **Distance-hide gate and state 14 (SPEC-0038 Behaviors 24 and 38).** The monster rows' hide distance and chance (f32 +0xA4, +0xA8) are read with the distance hide flag (+0xA0). Each agro tick in the rush (outer 1), outside state 14, with the flag set, the hero farther than the hide distance and a roll int[0, 100] below the chance, the enemy enters state 14 (the roll is drawn again each tick). State 14 runs its phase word: phase 0 plays 81 through; phase 1 walks home with 125, enters state 8 hiding with its position set to the home point when no route to the hero exists within 3.0 of home, and plays 82 (phase 2) when the hero is within 5.0 with a route or animation 0 is current; phase 2 waits for 82, then goes to agro with the hero within 20.0, else the default state. On disc the gate is live for rows 9, 11, 237, 238 and 239 (approach 1, e.g. the tentacles). Distances are 3D here (the spec does not say horizontal). "A route to the enemy's own position" is taken as the same route flag as the route to the hero (Medium-High in the spec). Test: the spec vector (d 8 enters, d 4 never, outer 4 never), the walk home and the hide snap. Unverified: not watched in game.
- **Attack manager and wait in line (SPEC-0038 Behavior 45).** New `neopets_sim::attackmgr`: one shared list of up to 20 enemies (a duplicate or a 21st is refused), granted when an enemy enters agro (the state-19 setter, the take-hit's send). Each frame, before the enemies tick: the release pass (an entry whose enemy is gone, not in state 19 or dead is removed by copying the last entry into its place, which that pass then skips; kept entries store their distance to the selected hero), the order pass (for i, j from i: swap when the enemy at i may move, meaning not in sequencer states 0 to 3, not blocking and not in the hit reaction, and distance j < distance i + 5.0) and the budget (entry k waits when C < used and k > 0, then used += its row Difficulty value, u16 0x3A). The capacity is C = 3 (w + a) from the selected hero's weapon and body item (Tor's swords 98 to 105 and armour 119 to 125, Roberta's wands 111 to 118 and dresses 127 to 134, item 126 gives 0, an empty slot -1), so with the starting gear (C = 0) one enemy attacks at a time, plus any with only weight-0 enemies before it. A waiting enemy whose approach is not 3 that would start its sequencer (the normal or counter choice) waits in line instead (outer 12): a stand-off point at the hero plus (u - R/2, 0, v - R/2), R the row's f32 0xB0 (read on disc: 0, 5, 10, 12, 15, 20, 25, 30, 40, 50), moved halfway toward the enemy while off its navmesh (up to 10 times, then its own point); it walks there (97, flyers 98), faces the hero and idles while its countdown runs, plays 73 (163 for flyers) on arrival, then waits int[600, 800] ticks with a new point at R = 50; once no longer waiting, outer 0 starts again. `neopets-data --monster-word <hex offset>` dumps a monster-row word across all rows (used to confirm 0x3A and 0xB0). Not built: the waiting approach-3 shooters' 3.0 extra aim scatter; the hit-reaction marker +0xAF8 is taken as outer 10; "arrived" is within 1.0. Tests: capacity by gear, the 20-entry grant, the budget at C 15 and C -6, the release's swap quirk, the order pass, the hold into outer 12 and back, approach 3 never held. Unverified: not watched in a fight (fights with several enemies now hold most of them back).
- **Cloud Beast test spawn and stand-in models (SPEC-0010 Placement and reachability, Test spawn; placeholder art).** The Cloud Beast is cut content: nothing on the disc places it, and it has no model, skeleton, clip bank, arena or effect definitions (SPEC-0010; SPEC-0061). With `NEOPETS_CLOUD_BEAST=1` the game reads monster rows 1 (type 1, the boss, class 9) and 2 (type 2, the companion, class 10) at start and spawns both in every world, 12 units in front of the hero, in a block slot no region uses (240); it respawns when missing. They are drawn with `placeholder` stand-ins, this build's own procedural art and not recovered assets: the boss as a white storm cloud (puffs over a grey underside, two dark eyes with brows, a frown, floating 2.0 above its ground point, sized to the row's radius 3.0 and height 3.5) and the companion as a small yellow lightning orb in a ring of dark puffs, riding 3.4 above the boss's body (the rig contract's bone 11 is not recoverable). They have no animation families (`HeroAnim` now tolerates none: no bones to pose). The fight was added later (Cloud Beast fight). Seen: a screenshot in 909 with the stand-ins in front of Tor. Test: the stand-in meshes are well formed.
- **Cloud Beast fight (SPEC-0010; cut content, finished here with guesses where the disc has nothing).** New `neopets_sim::cloudbeast`: the tuning block (82 words from 0x2DA4D0, read from the disc; the timers are (min, max) pairs per stage, checked with the new `neopets-data --boot-words <hex address> <count>`), and the controller as specified: per-tick timers, the stage rule (33 / 66 %, never back to stage 1), the reaction trigger (Tor's attack sub-states 2 to 12 within 10 and in front; Roberta's shots heading at the companion), the decision bands with the cooldown fall-through, hesitation by stage, the shock (under 15 units, 10 damage within 8, once), the fidget, air-flow and rapid air-flow (wind-up, the gust column from bone 6 growing 0.25 a tick to 10, the jump to the next ring point 2 to 4 steps on, the 0.5-a-tick slide, the run-out, one 20-damage connect per decision with the knock-back 7.0 x normalize(-9 f + (0, 12, 0)) into state 48, k + 1 rapid cycles), lightning (wind-up 1.0 / 0.5 s and none in stage 3, fire 3 / 2 / 1 s from the companion's pose point, the aim lift and guard shift, the six-tick gate, the latch cleared on multiples of 60, the guard block and the Fog Mote reflect into the special reply with its 20 self-damage), the bouncing balls (2 / 3 / 4 into a 5-slot pool, 10 units/s, bounce at 1.0 below the ground, 50 damage on touch, gone past 50 units of travel), the reply roll and the arena facing rule. In play (`NEOPETS_CLOUD_BEAST=1`): the arena is the level's locators 101 to 106 when all six exist (the Juppie arena, pack 1217, as in the spec's walkthrough), else a ring of radius 16 around the hero at the spawn; the hero's hits use the shared take-hit with the non-agro doubling and arm the monster bar; at 0 HP the boss and its companion die and the world is marked so it does not respawn. Guesses (no data on disc): the clip lengths and the ball event at half the bouncing clip; bone 6 (3.4 up, 2.4 forward) and the companion's pose point (its orb); a body cylinder of the row's radius and height around the floating cloud as its hurt shape; the beam's wall sweep halves the segment against the level's segment query and takes the reversed beam as the face normal; the stand-in effects (definitions 447 to 454 are missing): a translucent column for the gust, a bright core in a yellow glow per beam, glowing spheres for the balls, a pulsing dome for the shock, short sparks for bursts and reply hits; the body moves by procedural poses per clip (bob, lean, scale); no sounds (the controller makes no cue calls). Trace hooks: `NEOPETS_TRACE_CLOUD` (state changes, hits, arena points, the defeat) and `NEOPETS_CLOUD_HP=n` (starting HP). Seen in pack 1217: the lightning hitting Tor 3 times a cast in stage 1, the gust and knock-back, the shock, a sword hit (240 to 232), the reaction to Tor's swing, stage 3's instant bolt and rapid gusts, the bouncing balls, and the defeat. Tests: 7 (the words and stage rule, the decision bands, the shock, the lightning latch, the guard block and Fog reflect, the gust knock-back, the ball life).
- **Cloud Beast follow-ups (guesses, cut content).** The boss's monster rows and tuning words are now read at every start, and the developer menu (F1, HERO section) has "Spawn the Cloud Beast (cut boss)": it turns the test spawn on in the current world, clears that world's defeat mark and respawns the boss, on locators 101 to 106 when the level has all six, else on the ring around the hero (`NEOPETS_CLOUD_BEAST=1` still turns it on from the start). The stand-in models take the hit flash (their rigid parts are recoloured while the flash runs, as skinned characters' parts are). Sounds, since the controller makes no cue calls (all borrowed from cues this build already plays, guesses): stinger 4 when the boss spawns (the level scripts' fight openings), the Mote-use cue 0xA2 when the shock starts and when a bolt first fires, stinger 1 at the defeat (the Werelupe King's death stinger, SPEC-0019). Seen: the defeat path in pack 1217 still runs. Unverified: the menu spawn was not clicked through in game; the flash and the cues were not watched or heard.
- **Cloud Beast defeat (guesses, cut content: the spec gives no aftermath).** At the defeat the boss drops 200 Neopoints in coins and the Wind Mote (167, whose pickup text likens it to a tiny thundercloud) at its ground point through the item spawner; the stand-in cloud and its companion shrink and sink over the first two seconds of their corpse timers (each with its own row's timer) instead of standing frozen. Seen in pack 1217: the hit to 0, the Mote picked up ("I haven't seen this kind of mote before!") and the cloud gone.
- **Cloud Beast gaps closed (SPEC-0010 Companion; stand-in body).** `Character::stand_in_body` gives the floating stand-in's body cylinder (the row's radius 3.0 and height 3.5, its base raised by the 2.0 hover), now used by every hit path that had read the ground-level body or the (missing) rig: Tor's swing (as before), Roberta's shot contact (the shot sphere against that cylinder), her homing aim point and the target pick's aim point (the body's centre instead of 1.5 up or attach point 7), and the Mote power-attack objects' touch test. The companion orb now turns toward the active hero with weight 0.1 along the horizontal direction, as the spec says, instead of copying the boss's facing. Still left: Tor's jump attacks do not trigger the reaction (the hero word +0x1508 is Medium-Low in the spec); balls start at the boss's ground point as specified. Unverified: Roberta was not driven against the boss in game (her two-hero mode needs story flags that also start the Juppie arena's own scenes); Tor's swing still lands (checked earlier).
- **Confused enemies (SPEC-0029 Behavior 27.2 step 7; SPEC-0038 Behavior 44 outer 11).** A common enemy in agro with confusion (status 6) active and not attacking (outer 2) enters outer 11: each tick it turns toward a random horizontal direction (x and z from [-1, 1), normalized) with blend 0.05 and plays animation 69 (its root motion carries it); its normal attack choice runs only when the hero is within the two body radii plus 0.25; once the status has ended it returns to outer 0. Sources: Tor's Sun charm, Light and Sun Mote hits. Test: confusion enters outer 11 with 69 and the 0.05 blend, and leaves it when the status ends. Unverified: not watched in game.
- **Enemy shot scatter (SPEC-0038 Behavior 13 and 45).** A common enemy's spawned shot now adds 3.0 to its scatter e while the attack manager has the enemy waiting in line (only approach-3 shooters can fire then, as they are never held back), and the scatter is drawn in the spec's order (e, then x, then z; it was x, z, e). **All-areas sweep after the attack manager, the counter-attack and hide states, confusion and the Cloud Beast work** (`scripts/sweep.py`, 573 runs at 600 ticks as Tor, on a copy of the exe): no panic, crash or NaN; the same 8 falls as before (683, 718, 799, 879, 985 twice, 1300, 1344; R-0110).
- **Reanimating enemies (SPEC-0038 Behaviors 34 and 35).** The monster rows' reanimation flag (+0x15C) and countdown range (+0x160, +0x164) are read; rows 224 (Skeleton Archer, 3600 to 18000 ticks), 226 (Dumb Skeleton, 8000 to 12000) and 227 (Skeleton Fighter, 12000 to 18000) have it. Their first death (countdown idle at -1) draws the countdown and sets class state 16 instead of making a corpse; a death while it runs changes nothing. In state 16 the enemy refuses hits (the hit filter), plays 58 (153 flying) while the countdown runs, then plays the rise 85 once the area is clear (the hero at least 5.0 away and no other live character closer than 5.0; else it keeps lying), and when the rise reaches 0.99 its health returns to the maximum and it goes to agro (19); with no animation 85 it heals and goes to agro at once. It never becomes a corpse or a released body. A new dead test (`Enemy::is_dead`: a corpse, or state 16 with the countdown above 0) replaces the corpse flag in the scripts' AI-dead watches, the live counts, the target and pool searches and the attack manager (the corpse tick and held items still read the corpse flag), so a level's "all dead" watch passes while the skeletons lie. Payout on the first death as before. Test: lie, no restart on a second death, wait while not clear, rise, heal, agro. Unverified: not watched in game (the countdown is minutes long).
- **Alarm groups (SPEC-0038 Behaviors 2 step 5, 3 and 5).** The spawn record's alarm group byte (+0xB7, 0xFF none) is read. A group member entering agro (the state-19 setter) posts a broadcast for its group (counted per world and group). Each enemy's alarm-group check countdown reloads with int[15, 45] when it reaches 0 and then runs the check: with a group broadcast it has not yet seen, states 0, 3, 5, 6, 7 and 14 go to the alerted search (2) with animation 18, and a hidden enemy (state 8) plays its emerge (emerge sub-state 2) or goes to agro when it has no emerge animation. The notice check's gate: an enemy whose row's Display in Default word is 0 cannot notice while another such enemy in state 19 or 2 has a different group (neither 0xFF). Not built: the emerge's camera and sound cue (spawn word 0xC4 at 0). Reanimation sweep (573 runs, 600 ticks): no panic, crash or NaN; the same 8 known falls. Test: the broadcast flag, the reaction of a wandering member once per broadcast, the notice gate. Unverified: alarm groups not watched in game. Sweep after this change (573 runs, 600 ticks): no panic, crash or NaN; the 8 known falls plus one fall in 799 entry 0 at tick 326 that did not come back in 5 sweep reruns of 799 or 13 direct reruns (intermittent; R-0110).
- **Hero head look (SPEC-0040 Behavior 23A mode 0; SPEC-0005 word A bit 13).** New `neopets_sim::look::Look`: the stored yaw and pitch (kept between looks), each call stepping toward the target by the rate cap 0.10 and clamped to 1.57 and 0.35, with the pitch-sign quirk (a target above gives a negative pitch, so the look point O + 2.0 U lies below the head).
