- **Treasure maps and dig sites (SPEC-0051 section 10; SPEC-0021 Treasure maps; SPEC-0028 section 11 kind 6):** the level state keeps the held maps; a script give of item 175 takes the treasure number and marks it (no item kept), a world pickup of item 175 marks map +0x2C + 1, and marking a map plays cue 159 and turns on the marker of an undug site with key 200 + n in the current world. At set-up a kind-6 group shows object 0 at its start with the marker on exactly when the map is held, or, when its collected entry is set, object 2 at its start and object 1 at its end with the marker off. A repeat fire (state 2) of the site's trigger by the hero, with object 0 drawn and not deactivated, the entry clear and the map held, runs the dig: the hero to P - 2.2 D facing D, camera [0], the marker off, effect 4036 at P + 2.9 D, object 0 played and waited for, deactivated, objects 2 and 1 at time 0, object 1 played and waited for, 1 tick, effect 790 at P + 2.9 D facing D, 60 ticks, camera [1], hero animation 186, the drop action on reward [0] at P + 2.2 D with direction -D (coins 0.4 s apart), 120 ticks, the normal camera, the entry set, 1 tick; 1 test. Trace (the spec's vector, `NEOPETS_TRACE_MAPS=11 --pack 880`, standing in the sphere): the dig starts on tick 31, the reward is placement key 58 (Kauvara's Potion) at (1857.558, 158.869, -1043.435), as the spec gives, entry 211 is set on tick 543 with map 11 still held, the potion is picked up, and object 0 is hidden with object 1 at its end; without the map nothing happens. A bought map (shop stock of item 175) is map +0x2C + 1 as well, and a map placement is neither spawned at region start-up nor picked up while its map is held (not traced: no map placement was reached). Not built: SetHeroEquippedState 0 and the HUD reset (step 2), the map page; a started dig is marked for the load so it is not started twice while the hero stays in the sphere (clean side).
- **Treasure sets in the save (SPEC-0025 Treasure sets, Loading step 11):** `Slot::set_treasures` and `treasures` write and read the collected set at 0x2F2F (rows 1 to 247, bit 0 never set) and the held-map set at 0x2F4F (rows 0 to 47), LSB first; the test is the spec's vector 5 (maps 3 and 13 and row 213 give 0x2F4F = 0x08, 0x2F50 = 0x20, 0x2F49 = 0x20, nothing else). Saving writes the live collected entries and held maps; loading replaces them and switches each map row's dig-site marker in the resident worlds (current first) on for a held map and off otherwise, without testing the collected set (so a dug site with its map held shows its marker after a load, as the spec says). Trace: after the 880 dig, `--save-test` round-trips collected {211} and maps {11}. Deviation: bits 248 to 254 of the collected set are written 0 rather than keeping the live set's earlier value (the live set is not kept as bytes).
- **Switch path and the placed-record zone pass (SPEC-0030 section 2.1; SPEC-0028 sections 9 and 11; SPEC-0034 section 6 and vector 82):** a switch on a type-40 entry's first placed effect sets the record's active flag (starting nothing) and a switch off clears it and drains the record; the dig-site markers use it (site set-up, a map becoming held, the load's switch). Each tick after the zone update every active record of the resident worlds is zone-queried with its zone byte (+0x44) and its world's id: it is updated with the camera as the listener (so its cull and cone apply) when the query passes and stopped when it fails; records of a released world lose the flag. Trace: in 880 with map 11 held, record 6 (effect 399, the marker) is on with 2 live spawners, and the dig switches it off. Not built: switch ids from level scripts (no caller is known besides the dig sites) and the batch form.
- **Camera shake (SPEC-0027 Camera shake, Camera shake record, Per-frame use of the shake):** `neopets_sim::shake` holds the record: StartCameraShake(T, A, S, p4, R, phi, Z) fills keys (0, 0), (p4, A), (T - R, A), (T, 0) at rate S and the angle terms cos|phi| and cos|phi - pi/2|; StartCameraShakeMode(type, duration, strength) uses the six presets with A = 0.3 x strength (types outside 0 to 5 start nothing). Each tick (dt 1/60): the elapsed time, the key search, the envelope blend, the end test (that frame still moves), the phase and the wave sin(2 pi frac(phase)); a start keeps the elapsed time and key index, so only the first shake of a session moves the view (the original's defect, reproduced). The play view, not the camera, moves along its level right axis and the world up, and with Z above 0 turns to keep the point 0.5 A + 100 (1 - Z) ahead of the unshaken eye. 2 tests (the spec's envelopes for (0, 1.0, 1.0) and the (0, 0.5, 1.0) drop to 0.12, the 61-frame end of a 1 s shake in single precision, the second shake's zero amplitude). The dig sequence's step 6 now shakes (2.0, 0.3, 25.0, 0.5, 0.8, 1.5708, 0.2): in the 880 trace the vertical movement ramps to amplitude 0.3 over 0.5 s, holds and falls to 0 at 2.0 s. Not used yet: the shakes whose duration and strength the module specs do not give (909's pump, 1244's break).
- **The prize wheel of Meridell Village (SPEC-0059 sections 1 and 2; SPEC-0045 1023 Init objects and registrations):** `neopets_sim::wheel` holds the 1023 copy's values, the light show, the new pattern (three distinct dark cells by Random(0, 7)), the spin (the rate grows by 0.1 in float32 to 9.0999985 on tick 91, 418.60 degrees in; the stop prompt from tick 92; the spin ends after the press tick), the cell rule c = 7 - floor(A / 45), the result and the coin pay rule. 1023's Init now registers trigger 36 (routine 56) with flags 64 and 11 clear, initialises objects 0 and 5 (by flags 150, 10 and 15) and 1 to 3 (64 set, 248 clear), and draws a pattern. The trigger shows context 5; Square below the fee shows line 1042, else the hero is held and the round step runs: the start prompt (yes pays 15 NP, the 70-tick light show, a new pattern, Wait 1 s, the spin, Wait 1 s, the result with sound 11 or 10, the next round step), go-on prompts 1027 to 1030 (yes: line 1032 and the next spin; no: cash-out line r, pay-out 20/40/60/80 NP, flag 479), the five-win line 1031 with 160 NP and flag 480, or loss line r with flag 478. The game words live in module data (A carries over between spins and games). The wheel is object 0 turned to A about its depth axis, and the mask word M (dark cells, bits 2 to 9) goes to the world display word 2M, which rebuilds the objects with it as their layer mask. The script button query reads Square even while the hero is held. Trace (`NEOPETS_NEOPOINTS=100 --play-trace 4000 --pack 1023 --start "98,355"`): a loss (A 67.7, flag 478), then wins at 135.4, 203.1 and 270.8 with lines 1027 to 1029 and a loss at 338.5. Not covered: the music override, the walk-up greetings (triggers 37 to 40), the pointer direction in the model frame (the turn sign is this build's), and the 758 and 1358 copies.
- **Walk-up greetings and fame lines (SPEC-0059 section 3; SPEC-0045 1023 arena-talk test and templates A to C; SPEC-0042 1150):** 1023 registers triggers 37 and 39 (routine 54) and 38 and 40 (routine 55) with flags 64 and 11 clear: the arena master (entity 66) greets while the arena-result word is 0 with the line of the highest arena flag (28 to 32) or 33/34 (flag 380) or 35/36 in turn, the wheel host (entity 65) greets twice per module life (1016 for 480, 1017 for 384, 1018 for 479, 1019 for 478, else 1020; then 1021), each held by its latch until the partner trigger clears it. The fame rule now runs in the 1023 templates and the four 1150 villagers: with A arena flags set and the talk's d = Random(1, 6) below A, the arena line of the highest flag (the 384 pair and 385 triple in turn); else with flag 480 and the talk's Random(1, 3) at 1, the wheel cycle b + 8 to b + 10; else the story chain (template C's flag-138 line first). The host now draws d before each talk; the per-routine draw order is not reproduced (the sequences cannot match anyway). Not covered: the arena return that stores the arena result (the arena is not built).
- **Roberta's attacks and shots (SPEC-0036 Attack input (Roberta), Charge completion, Combo windows, Attack router, Attack animation choice, Hero shot slots, Roberta shot projectile, Magic):** `neopets_sim::roberta` has her attack step (the hold with requests 17 then 25 and the 80-tick threshold; the quick chain 2, 3, 4 with the 20 to 40 windows and the rapid shot 10 with up to 5 repeats; the full release 9, or 14 with the fired flag for ids 162, 169 and 171; the spins 6 and 7 and the leap 8 started by the gesture modes, each ending with its clip), the launch classes and step factors, the Mote animation of the full release, the eleven shot rows read from the boot data at 0x2DD6D0 (with the default and class-2 rows and the shot counts by Mote), the shot step (life, the 0.75/0.25 homing steer, the move by d x speed / 60), the anchor and default aim of the launch test vectors and the full release's rounded charge rule. In play, Roberta (when selected) starts her attack by gesture mode, runs the power-ready check from F = 7, launches the sub-state's burst on the release frame (event bit 0x20) into the six slots with S = A x D x B x G x E and T (the attack power table for a full release), pays a full release at its first shot (or at release for 162, 169 and 171), and each tick steps the shots, ends them below the ground, and hits the first hittable character whose bone capsule meets the shot sphere: trunc(max(0, S x C x T - defence)) with the element match C, the quick-shot payment (cost by class, exactly Q of word-2 charge) before the take-hit, and a full release's status request. Mote words and the item screen's Mote panel are per hero. Not done: the shots are not drawn (their effects are not built; R-0041), the auto-target and the random finder (shots fly straight; R-0041), the fan, bounce, deflect and hostile shots, world contact other than the ground, the near-target swat, the spin replays, the leap's steps (R-0041), Roberta's Mote power-attack objects, the charged-loop sounds and the shot cues.
- **Shops (SPEC-0050 sections 3 to 5, 8 and 9; SPEC-0035 section 15.2; SPEC-0023 section 15; SPEC-0022 chooser step 1):** world items now keep their placement's fixed count as their price. `neopets_sim::shop` has the nine-cell number field (with the original's overflow and negative quirks; the spec's vectors pass), the buyable choice (ready, vertical gap under 5, distance under 4, facing cosine above 0.8, lowest distance minus 2 x cosine, flag byte 1), the buy decision order (0x7FFF, then the balance, then the add; a negative price raises the balance), the take-or-copy rule and the multiplier with its cap. In play the Buy step comes before talk in the chooser: its caption is the boot template "Buy %s" (0x319528) with the item's name from table 0, and the price caption shows the price and the balance for the ticks it applies (font 0, right edge 410, baselines 115 and 140, the spec's colours, "Exorbitant!" from 0x315A48); Square runs the buy: the four buy hooks' lines (packs 856, 859, 1140 with flag 10 at Init, 1429), the price tests, the carry limit, the charge, taking equipment, stat-up items and maps from display, the sold-out removal at the carry limit and the once-per-area lists. Trace (pack 1131, Armourer Inga): with 500 NP the shield (item 106) is bought for 400 and leaves the counter; with 30 NP the need-more refusal for 370 shows. Not implemented: the never-affordable and can't-buy-now texts and the need-more glyph (R-0030), equipping a bought item, map numbers for item 175, the coin model beside the caption. Unverified: the Buy caption and price lines were not checked in the window.
- **Recruitment Centre errands and Meridell villager talk (SPEC-0039 packs 1148 and 1133; SPEC-0042 pack 1150; SPEC-0045 pack 1023; SPEC-0058 step 7; SPEC-0022 kinds 0x11 to 0x13):** `neopets_sim::level` adds the Recruitment Centre (1148: the recruiter's cases by flags 202, 151, 150, 143, 142, 138, 137 and the first talk, with the parcel, shield 106, letter 39, papers 38 and consent form 0 hand-ins and flags 137, 138, 143, 17, 2, 150, 142, 3, 8 and item 120; the shop step with LevelPrompts 43 and 46, the 100-Neopoint buy or the sword trade for item 42, flags 140 and 644, and the walk-back sequence that returns the traded item; Yavik's and Tark's lines; set 0 with flag 496), the tavern (1150: Master Torak's 138 to 139 step and the item 60 and 39 hand-in for line 4, whose dialogue command gives item 38; P0 to P3's story chains with bases 9, 38, 67 and 96; set 0 while 142 is clear), the smithy (1133: the item 40 to 43 trade for item 60 and flag 141 with lines 34 to 38; the story lines; set 0 or 1 by flag 10) and 1023's villagers (templates A, B and C over 33 routines with shared ones, entity 74, the wheel host's lines, entity 90's prompt by flags 485 to 488) and 1023's return locators by the interior just left. LevelPrompt is now a thread call: the box asks, Circle answers yes and Triangle no, and the pack's answer calls continue the thread. The box's dialogue commands now set and clear progress flags and give items (count 18 gives 1, item 135 gives Neopoints). The wallet moved into the level state. Traces on the disc: Officer Kendrick takes the parcel and, with the shield, gives the letter (line 34); Rognus forges the sword from items 40 to 43 (line 34, item 60); Master Torak's line 4 leaves the hero holding item 38; Bert answers with P1's flag-138 line 52; Guardsman Darol (entity 2, template C) says line 350 and sets 644. Not implemented: shops (item 106 in 1131, item 99 in 1133; SPEC-0050), 1150's wander step, 1133's thread 2 and item-ask 99, the arena and wheel branches and their draws, the cloak and map sellers (entities 72, 75, 94), the arena master (66), the zone loaders that bring in the villagers of entities 57 to 96. Blocked: the shop seller of 1148 (entity 3) and so item 42 (R-0029).
- **The Meerca chase in Meridell Village (SPEC-0045 pack 1023; SPEC-0058 step 6):** `neopets_sim::level` runs 1023's Init registrations (the signs on 3, 4 and 6 to 16 with context 3 and their lines, triggers 0 to 2's line 22 under flags 64 and 248, trigger 17) and the load thread's first-slot load (set 2, region 1026, or on a reload mid-chase set 0 with the Meerca placed at the first waypoint whose flag is clear and the chase restarted; set 6 with flag 10); trigger 17's chase-start scene (cameras 8 to 22 with the cuts as fade 0.7 s, camera, fade 0.4 s, the hero's moves and walks, Heermedjet's lines 25 to 27, flag 7, the parcel taken, flag 127) and the chase thread (the Meerca walks to locators 477 to 485 with stop 1.1, loops animation 28 or 48 until the hero is within 30 units, sets 128 + k and plays 44 at each waypoint; at 485 flag 136, the fade, set 2 and item 32 returned); the teleports 1 to 8 (shut with line 5 until 136, line 3 with flag 10; then the interiors eW 118, 125, 116, 113, 117, 111, 112, 115) and the line branches of 0, 9 and 10. Threads can now set flags and give items at their point in the scene, wait for the hero near a locator and loop a random one of two animations. Not implemented: movie 4, PlayHeroAnim, movement types, navmesh walks for the Meerca, the zone loaders (triggers 27 to 34), the villagers' talk, the cloak, the arena, the prize wheel and the exterior replacement loads of teleports 0, 9 and 10. - **The road encounter (SPEC-0042 module 678, carried by packs 678, 686 and 691; SPEC-0058 step 5; SPEC-0030 rows 49, 104, 105, 107, 110, 114 to 116, 139 and 140; SPEC-0052 letterbox; SPEC-0038 via SetAI_Agro):** `neopets_sim::level` runs module 678's Init by flags (first visit: set 0, region pack + 1, with the raiders' animation loops and triggers 0 and 1; after flag 126: set 3; flag 148 sets 149; the line handler on 2, 3, 5 and 6 and trigger 7 always), trigger 0's line 23, the intro S1 (steps 1 to 6 with the spec's waits, fades, cameras 17, 4, 11, 12, 16, 5, 8, 13, 14, 15, 6, 19 and 18, the walks to points 21 to 27 with stops 0.35 and 0.4, the entity animations and lines 3 to 8, then the hand-over: hero to locator 3, line 22, aggression on entities 1 and 2), the fight watch (start sum of entities 0, 2 and 1, the end scene once 31 hit points are lost; the spec's vector passes in a test), the end scene (moves, cameras 20 to 22 and 38, line 10, Darrick deactivated, set 3, Kell's line 1 with item 170 x100, line 2, entity 10 at 25 percent and aggressive) and flags 124, 125 and 126, the line handler (lines 9 and 20), trigger 7's prompt (context 3, line 21) and proximity P (line 0). Script threads now keep their world (entity and locator lookups), and new calls hold entities under script control (their AI paused), play entity animations (with WAIT), release, deactivate and activate entities, set hit points by percent, turn aggression on (class-17 state 2 or 21 with the search countdown, animation 18) and run the letterbox bars (mode 0; SPEC-0052's bar vectors pass). Trace (`--pack 691` from (-1645, 1130) with `--trace-to fight`, sword 98): region 692 gives Darrick (80), Sid (60), Drub (50) and Kell; the intro runs from tick 56 to 1491 with every line; the fight ends on tick 4134 when Sid reaches 28 (32 lost); the end scene loads region 695 (Kell, Drub), shows lines 10, 1 and 2, and the second Drub turns aggressive. Not implemented: movie 3, SetHeroEquippedState, AI behaviour 7 and movement types, entity walks by navigation mesh, the animation loops' timed ends and CTL, the hero's equipped-state cue. - **Scene staging (SPEC-0052 Threads and waiting, Camera track record and playback, Sampling a track, Screen effect fade, Control locks; SPEC-0027 Scripted cameras and Cutscene camera tracks; SPEC-0030 rows 34, 35, 106, 108, 112, 136, 137, 156, 158, 159 and 164; SPEC-0033 4A.4 and 4A.5; SPEC-0056 Walk-to-point):** `neopets_data::track` reads the type-28 camera tracks (census: 2,010 records, 329 keyed, 15,429 keys, the spec's counts; pack 985 track 25's first key as SPEC-0052 lists). `neopets_sim::scene` samples a track (the head for a static shot, the key lerp with the sign flip and the unnormalised matrix), runs the camera play (single-precision time, run states, end modes 0, 1 and 2, the WAIT test) and the screen fade (frames by the console multiply, the clamp on the update after the target, black at 0); tests reproduce the spec's sampling, play counts (117/118, 233/234, 59/60) and fade vectors (41 frames for 0.7 s, the clamp on update 42, 8-frame white fade-in, 61 updates for a 1.0 s fade-in). In play each handler's calls now run as a script thread that yields at a blocking call (fade, camera play, hero or entity walk, LevelMsg until the box closes, loads, sleep), so scenes overlap as the original's threads do; the hero is held during scenes, camera plays and moving fades; a camera play poses the view with its focal length (vertical field of view 2 atan(240 / focal)); the fade draws above the HUD and menus. Scripted hero walks steer the hero's own stick walk to the locator (arrival within 0.25, or once the distance grows inside 1.0) and turn to the final facing; entity walks move straight at the townsfolk walk speed (0.08337 a tick) on the ground with walk clip 1, arriving within 1.0. 909's scenes are staged: the Act 1 start (instant fade-out, entity 31 to locator 69) and the arrival fade-in over 1 s once the loads end; the talk thread (Lucy's lines 181 to 188 as the round-up flags rise); the flag-110 step (hero to locator 7, camera 7); the pump exchange (fade, locator 29, camera 27, sounds 9 and 10, lines 166 and 162, return); the flag-114 and flag-117 scenes (Hubert's region 927, his walks under camera 12, regions 926 and 928, Hubert's walk under camera 15); and the flag-122 farewell (camera 10, lines 168 and 169, the family moved, camera 11 while Tor walks to locator 14). Traces show each scene's calls in order with their waits, and window screenshots show camera 10's shot of the family and the fade over the scene. `--flag` now applies before the start world's Init in the trace and the window, and `--start x,z,y` takes a probe height. Not implemented: letterbox bars, camera shakes, target plays (kinds 3 and 4), the return's preset pull-in (the follow camera is placed behind the hero), IsAnimatedCameraActiveAndFinished, navigation-mesh entity walks and their hold for a near hero, object and effect animation, movies. Unverified: the fade times, camera end modes, waits and omitted cameras named in R-0025.
- **Script triggers, the farm chores and the house (SPEC-0028 sections 6, 11 and 13; SPEC-0030 Layout section 3 and Triggers; SPEC-0045 pack 909; SPEC-0041 pack 951; SPEC-0048 Lock chests; SPEC-0049 New game; SPEC-0058 steps 2 to 4; SPEC-0021 Placement id table):** `neopets_data::trigger` reads the type-11 volumes with their headers (spheres, cylinders, kind-4 and kind-7 rectangles; census: 6,678, 10,038 declared cylinders, 5,849 and 675, the spec's counts) and the type-40 script groups (458 entries in 81 packs, as the spec counts); `travel::placement_table` reads tag 0x13 (3,421 entries). In 909 the pump is cylinder index 5 and the hint box rectangle index 7 at the spec's coordinates; 951's chest is group 0 (kind 1, key 43, locator 15, trigger 0, reward placement 1). `neopets_sim::trigger` has the record's state word (fire, update rule, decay; tests reproduce SPEC-0030's 1, 2, 3, 0 sequence and the object-0 cycle 1, 1, 3, 0, 1), the body-contact fire gates by kind and flag bit, and the body tests (upright cylinders exactly; spheres, other cylinders and rectangles by the Medium-Low shapes noted in the module). `neopets_sim::level` adds trigger registration, LockChest, an Update hook and module words: 909's routines 10 (camera pair, line 192; the spec's two vectors pass), 11 (the pump exchange: hero to locator 29, item 6 for item 7, lines 166 then 162, flag 112, then line 167), 4 (line 191) and 7 (enter world 71); its Update's flag and item steps (111 by the bucket, 618, 115 with Hubert's region 927, 118 and 119 with slot R emptied, 279) and the Act 1 start's flags (707 on and 1 off: 1 set, 706 cleared); the activation's arrival flag 706 and stage events 123 and 145; 951's Init (the lock, 120 and item 15 on the first entry with 119, region 953, the 148 branch), its three proximity callbacks and the lock-chest press. A New Game sets flag 707 (start index 0). In play the hero's body runs the trigger pass over the current world's volumes each tick, then the routines and chests run and the records decay; trigger prompts post their Square context (4 for the pump, 6 "Open" for a chest); LevelMsg calls made while a box is up wait and show in order; Init now also runs for worlds entered through doors. Items are one store: pickups, `--give` (equipped items count as held) and the level handlers share the level state's items. Traces on the disc: the pump shows "Whoops." then the full-bucket line and swaps the items; 951 entered with 119 set gives the key and loads Patricia, Lucy and Hubert; the chest takes the key, sets row 43 and drops the Wooden Sword, which is picked up; Hubert says line 60 without the sword and line 59 with it, giving the parcel (121, 122); the exit door then leaves to the farm at locator 2, where the stage-1 family placements 916, 922 and 930 load. `NEOPETS_TRACE_PATH="x,z;..."` walks a trace along searched routes (it cannot climb the house stairs: its grid allows 1.5 of rise per 4-unit step). Not implemented: the cameras, fades, movies 1, 2 and 88, object and effect animation, sounds and walks of these scripts (their flag, item and slot effects are applied), the act-start call (swap mode), the chest's open animation, effect 790 and hero animation 162, the grid cell lists of the trigger pass (every volume is tested), trigger fires by NPCs, shots, blocks and projectiles, the other group kinds, dig sites, saving the collected table, and the walk from the farm to the barn roof (the pliers sit at y 74; SPEC-0058 leaves the route open). Unverified: the pump's 163 to 165 choice, the rectangle extents and moving test, and trigger 7's word (R-0024); the whole chain was not played in one window session.
- **Credits roll (SPEC-0026 Credits; SPEC-0024 table 15; SPEC-0035 state 18 and Behavior 3a):** `neopets_data::design` reads table 15 (623 rows: text or spacer, style, colour set). `neopets_sim::credits` runs the roll from its words (boot 0x2DCF84: pitch 24, speeds 1.0 and 1.5, ramp 1/512, fades 32, drop -20, start offset 1): rows walked from the top row, edge fades, style 5 and 6 rows switching the target speed, the speed easing, the scroll and one drop per pass; a test matches the spec's timing (first drop on pass 469, target changes on passes 5,113, 7,092, 11,564 and 12,135, the last row dropping on pass 14,122, alpha 64 at y 16 and 32 at H - 8). In the app, state 18 sets the music bank override 8 each pass (the title sets 25, entering play or the pause page clears it), starts the roll at pass 0, draws the rows centred at x 256 in the colour of their style and set (boot 0x2DCFA8) with the roll's alpha over record 14's picture, and returns to the Options page when the last row drops. Seen: a screenshot at 12 s shows the Designers to Character Art Lead rows in both colour sets over the picture. Unverified: the font, its spacing and whether y is the baseline (R-0023; font 0, spacing 0, baseline); the full 14,123-pass run in the window and the return to Options were not watched.
- **Emote gestures (SPEC-0040 Behaviors 22, 22B and 3D step 6; SPEC-0022 Dialogue control records):** `neopets_sim::emote` now has the actors' body and face queues (four entries each, the oldest dropped, cleared at spawn and when a conversation starts) and the consumer (the pop rule by the playing id, the loop flag and progress 0.99; -2 returns to idle; the wanted body id B and face id F, with F = -1 after a pop empties the face queue; a main request with face override F starting the face head's clip or else the body head's, or a face-override set alone starting a new face clip; body emotes off for a busy actor); a test walks the spec's cases (a body emote's request, hold and pop, a face emote's set and its empty-head F, the face clip winning over the body clip, a busy actor, -2). The animation side gains the face override id (its face clip replaces the main id's and reverts at progress 0.99, step 6 of Behavior 3D) and family roles, so a character without a sub-part family keeps its face family. In play, the reveal's emote requests are looked up at once and queued on the hero or the talk partner (a row with no clip still queues its gesture); while the conversation runs the consumer owns the hero's main id and the partner's, and clips start only when their gesture starts (a body restart or a new face emote), under the shared handle's refusals. Seen: Lucy's lines play main ids 52 and 27 with "Frown_01" and "Threaten_01"; a screenshot shows her frown gesture during the line. Not checked: the stale-emote quirk (hero emotes queued outside a conversation play at the next one) follows from the rules but was not traced; busy NPCs are judged by the seated and lying main ids only (AI modes 10 to 12 are not read).
- **Options and the audio gains (SPEC-0035 Options menu and option block, Behavior 3, 3a title override, 3b pause level and stream gate, 3c front-end sound volume, 9c slots 66 to 68; SPEC-0025 Options block and Loading step 15; SPEC-0009 Volume and ramps, Default cue volume, Pass gate):** `neopets_sim::options` has the 32-byte block with its start-up defaults (0, 0, 1, 1, 0, 75, 75, 75) and byte round trip, the toggle (new = old != 1), the cycler, the slider (held Right +1.0, else held Left -1.0, clamped), the knob x = trunc(value x 1.29 + 2.0) and the apply rule (gains = value x 0.01 clamped, knobs, the inversion flags); the gains are 1.0 until the first apply. The menu flow runs the option rows by focus state: 13 Vibration (toggle per Left or Right press, no apply), 14 the invert row (the focus index picks Invert Left-Right or Up-Down; apply on every pass), 15 to 17 the sliders (held input, apply on every pass), all silent; predicates 66 to 68 read the option words, so the Controls page shows On or Off and Yes or No, and the knobs (elements 0xCE to 0xD0) sit at the positions the last apply wrote. Left and Right (arrows, A and D, the D-pad) are added to the menu words, with a held word for the sliders. A test covers the first apply (1.0 to 0.75), a held Right for 10 passes (75 to 85), the invert toggle by focus index and the Vibration toggle. The gains now drive the sound: the bank target is row Level x Music gain, the stream ceiling Cap x Sound-effects gain, the default cue volume trunc(Scale x Sound-effects gain), the stinger volume trunc(1024 x Sound-effects gain) and the emote-clip volume trunc(1024 x Voice gain) (refused at 0 or below); the menu-table sounds play at the fixed 1,024. Once the title has been shown the music controller also runs on front-end passes: the title's bank override 25 (bank 35, the title music) and the pause page's one-tick level trunc(400 x Music gain) with the stream gate closed. The invert flags complement the right-stick bytes. A save writes the live block; a load copies the slot's block and runs the apply rule. Screenshots: the Audio page with Music at 30 (its knob moved) and the Controls page with Vibration Off and Invert Up-Down Yes. Not implemented: the credits (their bank override 8 and the scroll), the fade step (state 28), rumble (Vibration is stored only), the unlabelled cycler and toggle (no rows), the music overrides' clears on the other front-end exits, the Resume focus state's level (only the pause page's).
- **Emote clips and the script sound calls (SPEC-0009 Emote clip lookup and playback; SPEC-0022 Dialogue control records; SPEC-0024 tables 7 and 8, path records, villager voice set; SPEC-0031 Script sound calls; SPEC-0030 rows 227 PlayStingStream, PlayHeroSound, SetMidiExciteLevel and SetMidiAdventureLevel):** `neopets_data::design` reads the three path records (census: 1,185, 367 and 88 entries, 189, 9 and 0 of them empty, 10,056, 3,327 and 6,688 units, the spec's counts) and tables 7 and 8 (59 and 18 always-play rows; row 124 is clip 10 with variance 13); the monster table gives each row's villager voice set. `neopets_sim::textbox` reports the emote requests (kinds 0x10, 0x16, 0x17, 0x18 with their first argument) when the reveal reaches them; `neopets_sim::emote` does the lookup (roll, random step, 74 x voice set for a partner, 183 for Roberta's body emotes, none for face emotes) and the unsigned directory bound, with the spec's vectors (153, 192, 10, 9, -38 past the end, clip 74 with no voice set at position 0). In play, a partner request needs a talk partner (its voice set from its row); a clip is refused while the stinger handle is busy or the bank music is not active, and of a body and a face clip in one tick only the face clip plays; the window plays the clip (bank 144 or 145, read once and kept) as a one-shot on the shared stinger handle. Traces in 909 show Lucy's lines requesting "Frown_01" (1.56 s) and "Giggle_01" (0.96 s) from bank 144. The level runtime's action list gains the sound host calls: PlaySound (one-shot), LoopSound (keep-alive start), VisitLoopSound (refresh only) and KillSound (first matching voice, emitter included) on the area slot at an entry record, PlayHeroSound (hero -1 or Tor), PlayStingStream (stingers 0 to 18), SetMidiExciteLevel and SetMidiAdventureLevel; no implemented level routine calls them yet (the routines that do, such as the cage and chase sequences, are not built). Flags already set when play starts or a slot is applied no longer count as rising. Not implemented: the emote gestures themselves (the actors' body and face emote queues of SPEC-0040 Behavior 22; the clip plays at the reveal instead of when the gesture starts), the voice gain (1.0), AddAnimObjSound.
- **Creature, attack and placed sounds and the stingers (SPEC-0031 Layout section 10, Creature blocks and animation sounds, Placed sound emitters; SPEC-0036 Attack router and swing hits; SPEC-0009 Effect and stinger index, Stinger and emote-clip playback, Volume and ramps ducking, Intensity inputs adventure zones; SPEC-0024 table 2; SPEC-0055 death cue test):** `neopets_data` now keeps word B of each animation-table entry (the packed sound word), reads table 2's flag cues, and reads the placed emitters of the type-11 chunk (the fourth list's ambient loops and adventure zones, the sphere list's emitters with flag 0x10); census: 2,380 ambient loops, 209 adventure zones and 216 sphere emitters in the 12 packs the spec lists, with its cue counts (0: 202, 1: 5, 3: 6, 4: 2, 500: 1). A test decodes the spec's packed word 0x80002101 (block 351, cue 4, keep-alive). In play: creature blocks are held in slots 6 to 24 while a resident character's main table (its first 167 entries) names them; a character whose animation changes to one with a sound word plays its cue once at its position, and a keep-alive word keeps its voice refreshed while the animation stays current. Tor's attack transitions play the router's cues (into sub-state 2 or 3: weapon 3 and voice 5; 4: weapon 4 and voice 6; 6 and each repeat: weapon 9 and voice 4; 10: voice 6 then 8), a swing hit plays weapon cue 14 (power cue 2 in sub-state 10), and a shield block plays shield cue 0. Ambient loops start on the third tick inside their sphere (keep-alive, do-not-restart, radii from the record, volume trunc(default x factor)) and reset their counter outside it; sphere emitters start on the first tick within radius + 1 with R = max(30, the record's radius); adventure zones set the adventure counter from the hero's distance (f = 1 inside the inner radius or past the outer one, the spec's quirk). Stingers: the default death requests stinger 3 ("Character Dies"; Roberta, absent here, counts as alive), a progress flag that rises plays its table-2 stinger v - 1; one stinger at a time, stinger 4 not within 40 s of its last start, and the bank music ducks to 0.7 V while one plays. The window plays stingers as one-shot VAG voices. Checked by trace: a fight in 757 plays creature cues from blocks 361, 363 and 379 at the characters, and Tor's first swing plays 466:3 and 348:5 (466: no sword); pack 1244 starts its torch spheres (448:0) on tick 2 and an ambient loop (448:2) on tick 3; setting story flags at the start requests stinger 16 (one at a time). The window runs with no audio errors. Not implemented: the special creature-kind hit cues (Black Knight, Undead Draik, Dark Knight, skeletons), material impact cues of swing hits, the big-block shield cue, unsheath and sheath, Roberta's cues, the Mote power cues beyond the swing hit, the level-script sound calls (PlaySound, LoopSound, AddAnimObjSound, KillSound, PlayStingStream), the item-showcase stingers, the movable-block sound, breakables, chests, ladders, the rope gun and the emote clips; the emitters are tested over the whole list rather than the camera cell's list A.
- **Sound blocks and cues (SPEC-0031 Layout section 7, Cue playback, Sound slots and their blocks, Positional volume and pan, keep-alive and loop voices, Cue instance limit, the cue use table, Footsteps, Landings, Hero impact, Hero voice slot uses, Save-point cue; SPEC-0021 use cues; SPEC-0022 box cues; SPEC-0035 menu sounds):** `neopets_data::block` reads the 144 sound blocks (cue entries, command records, the voice records the commands name, the block pool decoded per voice); census: 144 blocks, 1,950 cue entries (390 empty), 7,716 command records with the spec's per-type counts (1: 4,820, 25: 572, 27: 1,252, 31: 357 ...), block 347 with 192 cues and 1,142 commands. `neopets_sim::cues` runs cue instances in the driver: record delays and results, voice starts (types 1 and 9, block form of the volume step, key 60, instance bend, levels and pans from variables), loops and breaks, holds, random and round-robin variant groups (per-record last pick), random delay, random and set bends, variables, waits, labels and gotos, release, kill and end, stop-on-replay (flag 0x2) and the instance limits (flags 0x8, 0x10, 0x20); tests use the spec's limit vectors. The synth now carries each voice's sample, so bank and block voices share it, and block voices follow a new request volume and pan. `neopets_sim::sound` is the game side: 25 slots (main 347, Tor's voice 348, weapon, shield and power blocks by the spec's rules, the area block of the music row), the cue call's checks (slot, loaded block, cue id, volume below 1, do-not-restart same-voice test, the 48-voice pool, range 200), positional volume and the pan angle as the original sums it, keep-alive voices (4, -1 per update, +1 per refresh), loop voices kept out of range, and the per-pass update; tests use the spec's vectors (1,024, 768, 512, 0 and out of range by distance; radii 30 and 10; pans 269, 89, 179, 359; the slot blocks 463, 466, 469, 476, 456, 460, 459, 477, 490, 485). Cues now sent in play: footsteps (gait +0, +20 or +40 by state 3 or 4, surface offset by ground code, on each new layer-0 frame with bit 0x80 or 0x100 while grounded), landings by ground code, the jump voice (10), hits (170 and the voice slot's hit small or big on a fair draw), the knocked-out voice (2), low health (157, do-not-restart), coin pickup (165), item use (159, 160, 155, 156, the refusal 162), the save point's keep-alive 0xA8 within 15 units (no emitter, the spec's quirk), the dialogue box (0x98 advance, 0x97 close), the front end (0x9A cursor moves, 0x98 select, 0x99 back, the card answers, 0xA9 save complete) and pause and unpause (148, 149). The window loads each slot's block and plays the cues through an endless 48 kHz source. Checked: traces show walk footsteps 347:20 on the farm's dirt and a hit giving 347:170 with 348:0; `--render-cue BLOCK CUE` renders a cue (footstep 0.24 s, coin 0.32 s, impact 0.23 s, menu move 0.75 s, the save-point loop sustained); 348:0 is a two-way variant group whose second variant is a no-op, and the not-the-last-pick rule makes its first play silent, so the grunt sounds on some hits only, as the data has it. Nobody has listened to the cues yet. Deviations and gaps: the ground material code is taken as the ground entry's kind word (+0x38) (unconfirmed, R-0022); landings are detected as the hero leaving a jump or fall state onto the ground; record delays count at 240 Hz (Open question 47) and the random draws use this crate's generator (Open question 49); noise voices are not synthesised; type-9 voices read their samples like type 1 (the original's missing base is a quirk not reproduced); types 4, 5 and 38 do nothing. Not yet sent: creature blocks and animation sounds, attack voices and weapon cues, shields, Motes, the area slot's script sounds and placed emitters, breakables, chests, ladders, the rope gun and the stingers.
- **Bank music: sequencer and synth (SPEC-0031 Layout sections 1 to 6 and 8, Playing a bank sound, Driver volume, pan and pitch, Voice allocator; SPEC-0009 Interactive segments, Volume and ramps, Intensity inputs, Transitions):** `neopets_data::bank` reads the 134 sound banks: header, SBv2 section, object record, directory groups (count, volume, pan, voices read from the group pointer, the 0x310 sentinel included), voice records, the sample pool split by pool offset and decoded (up to the first end frame; a repeat bit loops from the first loop-start frame) and the MMID container's MID children (tempo, PPQ, events). Census: 134 banks, 10,790 directory records, 28,860 voice records, 20,718 samples and 663 music children, the spec's totals; 7,988 samples loop (the 7,680 loop class plus the "other" class, whose last frame carries the repeat bit). `neopets_sim::synth` renders the music voices at 48 kHz: the note-on scan by key window (at most 32), the pitch register (the spec's vectors 1,364, 8,192 and 3,763), the bend term and bend value, the controller-10 pan angle, the bank volume step with the equal-power table and the rear-half inversion (the spec's level vectors), the SPU2 ADSR envelope from the two register words (public rule), sustain, loops and the range-1 allocator (24 voices, priority stealing). `neopets_sim::sequencer` runs a bank's instance at 240 ticks per second: running status, 0x8n, 0x9n and the 0xDn note-off, controllers 7, 10 and 64, program, pitch bend, tempo and end of track (loop at once; a second end in one tick stops), the driver's delta timing (rate, carry, W), command messages parsed command by command (start, jump and stop children, registers, tests on registers and G with the skip-one and skip-block rules, macros, channel groups that mute by G or a register), children started in a tick reached later in it. `neopets_sim::music` adds the bank controller (start at child 0 and rise by 15 per tick, a change falling by 20 per tick before the new bank starts, the stop rows' timed fade, the sent volume) and the intensity inputs (the excite float from the agro count with its +1.15 and -1.3125 steps and the refused 100, register 2 = trunc(E / 50) + 1, G = 100 in combat, the adventure counter counting down after it). 9 new tests use the spec's vectors (rate 400 at 500,000 us and PPQ 480, W 120, the setup and jump template, groups by G, skips, the ramps 80, 60, 40, 20, 0, V 525 sent as 65, the stop fall of 2, register 2 = 2 at tick 44, E stopping at 98, C 100, 74 and 49). In the window the bank music plays: the controller follows the current world's row (with the run-time flag), and an endless 48 kHz source renders the sim's engine on the audio thread; a bank start loads and converts the bank (the old one is dropped), the volume follows the controller each frame, and register 2 and G are sent when they change (the cache resets with a bank start). The agro count is the characters in AI state 19 that are not dead. Checked: `--render-music BANK SECONDS G R2` writes a bank to private/audio; bank 66 (909's) runs the setup and then children 2, 3 and 1, with RMS 1,460 to 2,420 and no clipping over 30 s; bank 28 with register 2 = 1 enters children 4 and 5 (combat), and with 2 from the start stays in exploration, as the spec says for a fight already past 43 ticks; traces show bank 66 at 750 (sent 93) in 909 and E 98, register 2 = 2, G 100 in a fight in 757; the window runs with no audio errors. Deviations and gaps: linear interpolation (not the SPU2's Gaussian filter); the music volume scales the mix rather than each voice's level; no reverb; a stopped child's voices are released (R-0021); bend ranges come from voice bytes +8 and +9, which are 0 on disc, so bends do not move bank voices; the adventure zones, overrides, stinger ducking and option gains are not implemented; a bank loads on the main thread when it starts (a short hitch). Nobody has listened to it yet: the checks are numeric.
- **Audio decoding and the stream channels (SPEC-0009 Layout PS-ADPCM, VAG and stereo stream; Track row, Per-tick controller step 7, Run-time flag, Streams, Area to music row; SPEC-0024 table 4):** `neopets_data::audio` decodes PS-ADPCM frames (the five filter pairs, the shift, the end flag, silent 0xC0 frames, a filter above 4 stopping a VAG), VAG clips and " KPV" stereo streams (every 2,048-byte block to the end of the resource, left then right, trailing silence trimmed), reads a pack's type-1001 music key and selector and the table-4 music rows (defaults for ids 193 and up); tests cover the spec's frame vectors (4,096 at shift 0, 1 at shift 12), the filter history, the containers and the row arithmetic. Census: the 19 VAG clips decode (2.66 to 8.45 s, rate 22,050, with their names; 260 is 6.28 s as the spec's header example), all 110 streams decode (104 at 22,050 Hz and 6 at 44,100, about 9,500 s in all); music rows 678 -> key 11, row 5, bank 28, stream 160, block 411 (the spec's example), 909 -> row 68, bank 66, stream 191, block 443, 1096 -> bank 23. A decoded stream written to private/audio checks out (RMS about 1,580, no clipped samples). `neopets_sim::music` has the run-time flag (bit 216 and the selector table), the row-to-resource selection with the flag offsets (+67 bank, +55 stream, block unchanged, added to negative values too) and the two stream channels (start at 51/1024, +1 per tick up to trunc(Cap x gain), a lowered ceiling at once, -1 per tick out, stop at 0); tests use the spec's vectors (678 with and without the flag, 3 -> 13 or 80, cap 700 reached after 649 ticks, 474 ticks to 525 at gain 0.75). The window now plays sound: Bevy's `bevy_audio` feature is enabled (rodio and cpal, recorded in docs/DEPENDENCIES.md), decoded PCM is a Bevy audio source, and the current world's stream plays as a looping voice whose volume follows its channel; checked at run time (stream 191 in 909 playing, volume rising 0.17, 0.28, 0.40, 0.52 over 8 s; trace: 111/1024 at tick 60). Not implemented: the bank music (the sequencer and instruments of SPEC-0031 section 8) and with it the intensity inputs, volume ramps, ducking and reverb; sound blocks and every cue (menu, hero, enemy, item, save point); the stingers and emote clips (decoded, not triggered); option gains (1.0 throughout); the wait for a bank change to settle before a stream starts; a stream decodes on the main thread when first needed (a short hitch).
- **HUD panels and value texts (SPEC-0023 sections 4, 4.1, 5 and 11; SPEC-0035 sections 9c slots 11, 14, 28 to 30, 32 to 34, 36, 49, 51 to 56, 12 and K):** `neopets_sim::hud` gains the equipment-panel timers G2 to G4, the group alpha pushes in the spec's order ((0x30, G1), (0x1C, G2), (0x1D, G3), (0x1E, G4), then (0x31, G0)), the L3 re-arm of C and G0 to G4, the quick-item re-arm of G0, G1 and C, and the pause page's clear of all of them; a test covers the fade (alpha 110 pushed at 26, 4 at 1, hidden at 0) and the re-arms. In play, selecting or using a quick item re-arms G0, G1 and C; L3 (X, or the left stick button) re-arms every group; opening the pause page clears the timers and the monster bar's. The HUD now draws, from resource 532, the quick-item cross (the topmost predicate-32 group above key 0x9C: rings, the selection highlight of slot 28, the count badges and counts of slots 29, 30, 33, 34 and 36, the health and magic emblems, and the Use, Activate and Close hints with their labels, slot 11), the selected quick item panel 0x30 (slot 51, G1) with the marker's count, and the three equipment panels (slots 53 to 56: 0x1C with Tor's weapon and G2, 0x1D with G3, 0x1E with Tor's shield and G4), each fading with its own timer. Value texts (kind 6) are drawn as plain decimal in their section K font, spacing and colour, aligned by the node width with the baseline at the node's y; labels (kind 2) in the panels draw their binding's text. Which number a count text shows follows its predicate (the cross entries) or its place (the marker's count in 0x30; the equipment words' counts, which are 0 since no Mote is equipped); the variable table at 0x2DD200 holds run-time pointers and is not read. Screenshots: the panels after L3, and the open cross with three potions, a sword and a shield. Not implemented: the item icons (keys 0x9C, 0x9B, 0x9E, 0x9D, 0xA0, 0xA1) whose art is child k of the item-icon node 1000 for an item kind no spec defines (R-0020); the Mote icons and counts of the equipment panels (no Motes yet); the effect emblems' item flag test (slots 31, 35 and 50; the emblems under slots 33 and 36 show) and their centred scale (section 3.1); the icon tweens; the D-pad hint, petpet meter, journal icon, portrait swing and countdown texts; Roberta's panel icons.
- **Card message boxes (SPEC-0025 Card message records, Card jobs as the player sees them, Clean-side mapping; SPEC-0022 Choices input and release, Substitution codes, Memory-card and controller messages, prompt row; SPEC-0024 table 9):** `neopets_sim::textbox` gains choice mode (Circle answers 1, Triangle 0, Circle wins, only on the complete last page; Square does not close that page) and the substitution codes (a code whose V - 2 has bit 5 is replaced by slot k, its first decoded argument; other codes kept whole), with a test from the spec's vectors ("slot 1.", two codes, a colour code kept). `neopets_data::design` reads table 9 records (text, the two labels, the colour; a null colour gives 128s); the census shows records 5, 6, 7, 9, 10, 14, 15 and 17 with the labels the spec lists (none on the busy records 5 and 15; Yes / No; Retry / Cancel save; Retry / Cancel load) and colours (74, 100, 255) or (100, 0, 0). The save and load screens now run the clean side's card jobs with these boxes: the load scan holds busy record 15 for 3 + 120 passes (the hold from the seconds word 0x2DD410), reads the file, and two passes later shows the picker, or record 14 (Load failed: Retry scans again; Cancel load asks record 17, whose Yes leaves); Cross on a save slot rebuilds it (the snapshot is set then), asks record 7 (overwrite) for a filled slot or record 10 (save to the card) for an empty one; Yes holds busy record 5 for its minimum, then writes the whole file, and two passes after a good write play resumes; No scans again with the cursor kept; a failed write shows record 6 (Retry scans again; Cancel save asks record 9, whose Yes returns to play with nothing written). Substitution slots are "1" (the card slot) and "106" (the needed KB the spec computes). The box is the shared dialogue box drawn above the page, with the record's colour, no speaker name, and in choice mode the Triangle label at x 128 and the Circle label at x 384 with their icons (logical sprites 0x78 and 0x79). Keys: Y or F (pad east) is Circle, T or Backspace Triangle, E (pad west) Square. A test runs the load busy hold (the read on pass 124), the failed load with its cancel confirm, the load picker, the save question, busy and write, No with the cursor kept, and the failed write with its cancel confirm. Screenshot: the overwrite question over the save screen. Deviations and gaps: the save-complete cue (0xA9) and the box cues are not played (no sound yet), and the original's cue quirk on Cancel save then Yes is dropped; a missing file shows record 14 as the clean-side mapping says (the original shows record 11 for a missing save folder); no card, format, card-gone or boot-check records are shown (there is no card); the controller box (record 25) is not shown; the question box closes on the answer (the card section says the text stays until the next box replaces it).
- **Save and load (SPEC-0025 Layout, Save points, Saving steps 1 to 3 and 5, Session snapshot, Progress tier, Loading, World keys, Hero maxima, Lookups, Validity rules, Clean-side mapping; SPEC-0035 sections 7, 12 and 14.3 row drawer; SPEC-0006 Kind 4 and Area names; SPEC-0024 tables 0 and 5; SPEC-0033 busy list):** `neopets_sim::save` holds the 0xC400-byte file of four 0x3100-byte slots with the field map, the empty template, the empty and present tests, the item-list groups in save and load order, the forward and reverse world-key lookups, the progress tier, the date text, the hero maxima rules 1 to 4, the status-timer conversions, the luck clamp, the picker cursor (load mode skips empty slots, save mode moves by one) and the per-tick save-point step (nearest point per flag branch, glow index, 15-unit cue test, 5-unit prompt, Square with the hero not busy, the away flag); 8 tests from the spec's vectors (template bytes, slot offsets, size check, flags, tails, empty pairs, load order, once-per-area bits, lookups, tiers, dates, maxima including the banded Roberta fallback, status truncation, cursor rules, save-point distances and ties). `neopets_data` reads kind-4 save points (at most 8 per region), a world pack's own region records, the type-1001 map area, item categories and carry limits (design table 0) and location names (table 5). Census: 88 save points in 60 world packs, none with a nonzero flag, none past eight; world keys 678 -> (0, id 0), 691 -> (5, 11), 909 -> (68, 602), 1391 -> (166, 10024), 1414 -> (179, 10045), 1433 -> (192, 11070), 193 distinct ids up to 11070; map areas 909 = 9 "Ellis Family Farm", 678 = 8; names 5, 7 and 43 as the spec lists; categories 98 = 1, 126 = 9, 164 = 8; the row drawer words (wrap 316, pulse 1.2 to 1.8 by 0.02, bar width 334, title colour (127, 125, 81, 128), text colour 128s), all as the spec gives them. In play the current world's save points run each tick before the hero update; within 5 units the Square caption is "Save Game" (label 25, replaced by a talk or door caption), and a Square press with the hero not busy records the restart point and opens the save screen (a direct set of record 18). The save screen scans the clean side's own file (private/saves/savegame.bin), shows the picker, and Cross rebuilds the chosen slot from the live game, sets the session snapshot and writes the whole file; Triangle returns to play. Load Game (record 17, from the title or the pause page) scans, starts on the first filled slot, leaves when none is filled or the file is missing or not 0xC400 bytes, and Cross loads the slot. The pickers draw four rows with the journal row drawer from the disc (caps, the 334-wide bar sampling 334 texels, the act icon of the tier or the Empty icon, the location and the date, the pulsed bar on the cursor row). A load rebuilds the game in the slot's key-B world with its key-A world in T, then applies the flags, wallet, the party's entries (carry limit on the general and quest lists), the cloak, the once-per-area sets, the status restore (truncated seconds through the status apply), luck, maxima and pools, and places the hero at vectors A and B as the restart point. Game-over Continue with a snapshot applies it the same way. Verified by trace (`--save-test`): in 909 the slot holds keys 11, 602 and 0xFFFF, location "Ellis Family Farm", tier 2 with flag 220, and a save, write, read and load gives back the position and facing, weapon 98, items 19, 146 x 2 and 164, and flags {5, 107, 220}; inactive statuses come back as 0 and immunities as -1, as the spec says. In 678 the prompt shows at the save point (-1706.4, 45.6, 1193.2), and Square opens the save screen on the next tick with the hero's own position and facing as the restart point. Screenshots of both pickers. Deviations and gaps: the slot is written for Tor only (Roberta's words hold her start pools; her entries are empty); the stash, petpet, movie words, per-world tables (kill memory, breakables), treasure sets, count table, Mote words and options are written as their empty or start-up values because the build has no live state for them, and are not applied on load; list order follows item id, not the original's slot order; the date is UTC (no console time-zone offset); the status seconds are computed as ticks / 60 (the spec's 1.5 for 90 ticks; a multiply by the f32 of 1/60 gives 1.5000001); empty buffers write key 0xFFFF; vector A's w is written 1 and B's 0 (not confirmed). The load rebuilds the worlds rather than applying over the live objects, so engine state outside the slot is reset. Not implemented (the save-point glow, cue 0xA8, the save-complete cue and the idle-list block were added later): the import of an original card's save, and the name-field glyph codes. The row texts use letter spacing 0 because the objective binding's spacing word is 9990 (R-0019).
- **Front-end screens (SPEC-0035 Behavior 1, 1a, 1b, 2 states 0, 1, 5, 6, 7, 11, 12, 26 and 29, sections 4, 4.1, 9, 9b, 9c slots 0, 2, 12, 27, 39, 40, 56 and 72, 9d, 12, 13; Layout sections A, T, G, H, I, O):** `neopets_data::frontend` now reads the strings, label bindings, pages and item targets (census: 165 bindings, 7 pages with pages 14, 759, 774, 794 and 824 as list pages, 30 item targets; the title's items are New Game (-3), Load Game (record 17) and Options (record 11), as the spec lists). `menus` (app) has the screen stack with the parent-index and back rules, the default focus and re-check, the generic step (Cross to the focused item's target, Triangle back, Down and Up with wrap on list pages, off for a screen's first two passes) and the states of the card check (passes at once: no memory card), the logo countdown (301 passes), the attract (300 passes on the first visit, then the title; the movies are skipped), the title (New Game on focus 0 from pass 5, the 30 s idle return to the logo), play (Select opens the pause page; the game-over flag opens page 23), the pause page (Select resumes), Resume and Quit (Quit goes back to the card state without the quit box, and the world is not torn down), and the game-over page; and the page draw list (conditions with the nearest predicate, the focused flag, row spacing over visible siblings, alignment, scale). Tests cover the start-up timing (602 passes to the title), the title's moves, wrap, open, back and New Game, the pause toggle and the row spacing. In the window a `--title` start runs the card state, logo, legal screen and title; otherwise play starts directly, and Escape (Select) opens the pause page there. The current page is drawn from the disc: full-screen pictures (resources 577 to 677, one indexed texture each), sprites and labels in their fonts, with the focused item's highlight, over a black backdrop; the world is frozen and the HUD hidden while a page other than play or the game-over page is up. Screenshots: the Neopets logo, the legal screen, the title with New Game highlighted, and the pause page. Keys: arrows or W/S move, Space or Enter confirm, T or Backspace go back, Escape is Select. Not implemented: the memory card, movies, the quit box (built later; see Quit teardown), Load Game, saving, options and their toggles, credits, the item screens, journal and maps (built later; see the map page and the quest journal), the start-point and slot choosers, key masks and animation slots, value texts on pages, sounds, and (until the Quit teardown below) a world reset on New Game after a Quit.
- **Quit teardown (SPEC-0026 Quit teardown; SPEC-0035 Behavior 2 state 7, section 13.1; SPEC-0006 a new game; SPEC-0025 pending flag):** the pause page's Quit now tears the world down: after that frame's ticks the play state is replaced by a new game's (909 in E with its Init and activate-world routine, 691 in T, both heroes at their start pools, no flags but the new game's, an empty party, 0 NP, no session snapshot, so the pending flag is cleared), the loader thread is restarted, and the options block (owned by the front end) is kept. A game counter makes the renderer drop and re-spawn every character and the hero after a rebuild, because the new game's character ids start again; a load now does the same (before, a load kept the old hero meshes, which were wrong when the loaded hero differed). `NEOPETS_TRACE_QUIT=n` runs the teardown on play tick n, once (in the trace, and in the window, where it goes straight back to play). Verified: a trace from 909 with 500 NP and story flag 220 quits on tick 300 and comes back with 0 NP, the new game's flags, the farm's regions reloaded and Lucy's opening line again; window screenshots at 8 s with and without a Quit on tick 120 match (each character drawn once, the music restarted). The quit box (SPEC-0035 Behavior 2 states 6 and 7, Behavior 1b step 1, section 13.1): Cross on Quit plays 0x9A and opens record 26 of the system message table in the shared box over the pause page; while it is open the input block holds the focus states, the generic step and Select, Square pages the text, Triangle (Resume) plays 0x99 and closes the box with the pause page and its focus kept, and Circle (Quit) plays 0x98 and runs the teardown (record 0). 1 test (the spec's Triangle and Circle vectors and the blocked Select). `NEOPETS_TRACE_QUITBOX=n` opens the pause page with the box on play tick n; the window screenshot shows the disc's text ("Are you sure you wish to quit your current game? Any unsaved progress you made will be lost.") with Triangle Resume and Circle Quit. The game-over page's exit with nothing pending runs the same teardown and sets record 0 (SPEC-0026 Game over flag; it replaces the earlier placeholder restart), and the title's New Game plays cue 0x9A (SPEC-0035 section 5, index 0). Unverified: whether the answer acts on its own pass (ours) or after the card boxes' two passes (the spec gives no delay for this box). Whether the original's effects, sounds and world-object state reset by the teardown match ours in every detail is unverified.
- **The map page and the quest journal (SPEC-0051 sections 1 to 9; SPEC-0035 sections 6, 9c slots 26 and 69 to 73, 14.3 and 14.4):** `neopets_sim::mappage` holds the 77 projection records (the spec's published values, by the user's decision), the projection with rounding, the marker angle and colour, the two pulses with their boot words (0x2DD310 to 0x2DD32C), the quest rows (table 1) and treasure rows (table 3) with their texts, the map's icon list (accepted quests with a stage on the area, then held, uncollected treasures; 32 at most), the cursor with its wrap, the journal's rows (accepted quests, then listed treasures, no area filter), a row's area, the journal layout (the line limit, row heights max(79, 75 + 14 L), the 360 cut with its exact-360 quirk, the more-below flag), the journal's keys, pulse and one-row scroll. The front end runs View Map and View Quests on the pause page, D-pad Left and Right in play (numpad 4 and 6 or the pad's D-pad; only with the quick panel closed and not opening, and no box up), the journal (state 25: rows built on its first pass with the pending row as the cursor, Down and Up with cue 0x9A, Cross on a row with an area to that area's map, L2 and R2), the map pages (state 30: the icon list on the first pass with the pending icon as the cursor, the pulses, Up/Right and Down/Left, Select to play, Cross or R2 to the journal with the icon pending, L2 to the item screen) and the item screen's L2 and R2 (V and B on the keyboard, or the lower triggers); Triangle uses the shared back rule. The journal draws its rows with the row drawer from the disc (the cap, bar, icon by type, title and wrapped objective; the bar colours (222, 160, 1) for main quests and (155, 155, 129) otherwise, the selected bar pulsed), and its page's own arrows, empty message and hints follow predicate slots 26 and 69 to 71. The map page draws its picture, the icons, the cursor label, the hero marker and the area name. 4 tests (projection, marker, pulse, icon, row, cursor, layout, key and scroll vectors; the menu ring: D-pad Left, the map's build and cursor, Cross both ways with the pending row, the six ring moves, Select, a negative area). `NEOPETS_TRACE_PAGE=map@n` or `journal@n` opens either page on play tick n. Window screenshots at the farm (909): the map shows the farm picture with the accepted quest "Herding Whinnies" as an icon with its label beside the hero marker and "Ellis Family Farm"; the journal shows the quest's row (medal icon, pulsed bar, title, "Herd the three Whinnies back into their corral!") with the disc's Cross View Map, Triangle Exit and L2/R2 hints. Placeholders (R-0070): the map's icons and hero marker are plain squares (sized by kind, in the row colours; the marker not rotated), the cursor label uses the journal's objective font, the area name sits at (40, 420), and the map's overlay group (Triangle Exit, Cross View Quest, the hints) is not drawn. RegisterTransitionMap (SPEC-0030 row 263; SPEC-0051 section 1; built later): the game state keeps the transition map (none at a new game, kept by world loads), and the map area falls back to it when the current world's word is -1; the calling triggers are registered and set it on any call with no state test: 685 (triggers 0 and 1: 54 and 43), 1255 (Init's flag 274 step, triggers 4 and 3 then 4 again, 5, 0 and 1: 5, 5 and 7), 696 (only its map triggers 4 to 8: 64, 73, 21, 37 and 73; the rest of 696's Init is not built), 1122 (triggers 1 to 3: 50 to 52) and 1098 (triggers 7 and 8: 48 and 49, registered in every case). 1 test. Seen: in 1255 the hero starts inside trigger 4, and View Map at tick 180 shows Werelupe Burrows Upper (area 5) with the hero marker at the entrance. Not built (the journal-notification flag and its clear were added later): Roberta's item pages in the ring (R-0031), the Treasures line (never shown on disc data).
- **Books and the book reader (SPEC-0035 section 6.1 and Behavior 6 step 3; SPEC-0028 Book station; SPEC-0022 the idle list's Read Book; SPEC-0024 Book records and Book index):** `neopets_sim::book` has the station test (kind 3, bit 6 set, bits 1, 2, 3 and 8 clear), the pending index, the 1-based cursor and the saved word (all -1 at start), the chooser's book step (the prompt while a book is pending and none is open, pending cleared each time, a Square edge copying it to the cursor first), the gate, the index rule (cursor - 1, else saved - 1) and the page timer ((1.2 - 0.12) / 75 a call, clamped). `neopets_data::book` reads a record's text chunk (title, style, paragraphs) through the books' path record. In play each world keeps its stations (they never fire a script, so the trigger list drops them); the hero's trigger step sets the pending value on overlap; the chooser runs Read Book after the door prompt when no caption was set earlier in the pass (label 31 as the Square caption, cue 0xAF on the take), and the Square-with-no-target fallback does not run while it shows. The front end's play gate opens the reader (record 20, state 24) while the cursor or saved word is set; the reader leaves at once with no index (cursor -1, the music override cleared, no cue), waits while the book loads, steps the page timer on every pass while a book is loaded, and on Cross (once loaded) plays 0x9A, closes the book (cursor and saved word -1) and returns to play; Triangle falls to the generic back rule and play's gate reopens the reader (the bounce quirk). The host loads the requested book from resource 576 when the index changes (the one-slot cache). 3 tests (the station, step, gate, index and timer vectors; the text payload; the reader flow). Seen on the disc: the station census of 786 (values 87, 24 and 34), 777 (17 stations) and 1414 (14), as the spec counts; `NEOPETS_TRACE_BOOK=7@120` in 777 opens "Werelupe Wars Vol2: Illusen's Revenge." with its paragraphs and record 20's own Cross Close hint. Placeholders (R-0071): the pages' layout, art, transforms and font (the title in font 0, the text in font 2 in two 19-line columns at x 20 and 234 from y 54, no page art); only the first 38 lines show. Not built: the book words' survival across a teardown or load (ours start again at -1 with a new game), the four music overrides beyond the bank override.
- **The box host (SPEC-0035 Behavior 2 state 23, Behavior 6 Overlay box busy, section 2a):** while the dialogue box is busy (a page shown or one following), play goes to the box host (record 19) after its game-over and book gates and before the Select and Start openers; the box host keeps the world running (it sets the play-active word), takes no menu input (so Select and Start cannot pause or open the item screen under a box, and the map shortcuts stay off), and returns to play when the box is idle. The HUD draws the box as before (record 19's own tree is not drawn over the world). 1 test (busy to the host, Select refused, idle back to play). Seen: the farm start sits in the box host under Lucy's first line with the world drawn and the Whinnies moving. Not built: its entry and exit fades; its cue 0x97 is the box close's in play (one cue, as before); the level script's button query is not forced false under it.
- **Developer menu and free camera (a test tool of this build; no spec, not part of the game):** `dev_menu` (app) opens with F1 over play: god mode (no hero hits, full health each tick), infinite magic (no magic cost, full magic each tick), infinite Mote charge (no charge use), the free camera, add Neopoints (100, 1,000, 10,000 or 100,000 by Left/Right), full heal of both heroes, every Mote (charge 100 each), any item by id with its name from the item table (Left/Right, Shift for 10), switch hero, and toggle a story flag by number. Up/Down pick and Enter or Space applies. F2 (or the menu) toggles the free camera: it starts at the play camera's pose, WASD or the arrows move, E and Q rise and sink, J/L and I/K or a right-button drag look, Shift moves faster, and it replaces the play camera's pose after each frame. While the menu is open or the free camera is on, the hero, the game's menus and the camera pad get no input; the world keeps running. The cheats survive Quit's teardown and loads. `NEOPETS_DEV=menu,free` opens the menu and/or starts the free camera (backed up and raised) after 30 frames; `NEOPETS_TRACE_CHEATS=god,magic,charge` sets the cheats in a trace. Verified: with god mode the 757 fight that killed Tor on tick 639 (`NEOPETS_TRACE_HP=30`) lands no hit in 1,500 ticks; window screenshots show the panel with every entry and the farm from the raised free camera. Not covered by god mode: deadly ground, KillCurrentHero and scripted deaths (they do not go through the hit routine).
- **Developer warp and performance overlay (test tools of this build; no spec):** the developer menu's Warp entry steps through the save's world-key list (the nonzero keys, sorted) with Left/Right and, on Enter, rebuilds the game in that world pack: its Init runs with the current story flags, and the session carries over (items, Neopoints, held maps and collected treasures, the transition map, the book words, the equipment, the active hero, the heroes' health and magic, options and cheats); the hero arrives at the world's first start point and the loader restarts. The original has no such travel, so a warp can put the story in states the game never reaches. F3 toggles the performance overlay (top right): frames per second over the last two seconds with the 1% low, the average and worst frame time, the simulation's ticks per second and this frame, the entity count, the window size, the current and other world, the characters (all and in the current world), the region loads in flight and the play tick. `NEOPETS_DEV` also takes `perf` and `warp=N`. Seen: `NEOPETS_DEV=perf,warp=1066` moves the game from the farm to 1066 (other world 696) with its nine characters loaded, and the overlay reads about 32 frames per second (1% low 20, 31.7 ms average), 60 ticks per second and about 5,000 entities in the window at 2000 x 1200.
- **More developer menu entries (test tools of this build; no spec):** game speed (paused, 0.25x, 0.5x, 1x, 2x, 4x by Left/Right, Enter for 1x; the frame's elapsed time is scaled before the fixed step, so the tick rate follows, capped by the catch-up limit), F4 to pause or resume and F5 to run one tick while paused (the speed shows on screen while it is not 1x); defeat every enemy in the current world (each active, living enemy takes a hit of 1,000,000 through the normal hit routine, so bosses that refuse hits still refuse; inactive enemies are left); go to the current world's start points in turn (Left/Right, Enter); move the hero to the ground under the free camera; hold every treasure map (1 to 46, with their dig-site markers). `NEOPETS_DEV` takes `speed=K` (0 paused, 3 normal) and the trace takes `NEOPETS_TRACE_KILL=n`. Seen: in 757 the kill on tick 400 hits the six active enemies (two Skeleton Fighters, a Skeleton Archer, two Dumb Skeletons and a Slorg), which die, and leaves the six inactive ones; at speed 0.25x the overlay reads 15 ticks per second; the farm lists 71 start points and the warp list 191 worlds.
- **Character colour slots (SPEC-0003 group byte 0x27; SPEC-0004 Colour, character meshes; SPEC-0040 Behavior 26 items 1, 4 and 9):** the material reader keeps each group's colour slot (0 to 14) and every mesh part carries its group's slot; the region reader keeps the kind-3 record's 15 colour words (data +0x48 + 4 i); each region character keeps the 15 slot vectors (bytes 0 to 2 / 255) and every part is drawn with its slot's vector multiplying its lit colour in the GS's gamma space (skinned parts each frame, rigid parts once at spawn), textured or not. This is what made untextured NPC parts (skin, hair, clothes) white or grey. The hero is drawn as before (hero outfits use slot 0, white, almost everywhere). 1 test (the spec's Lady Marissa vector: word bytes 255, 152, 83 on a 1.25 vertex at 128 give 160, 95, 52). The segment's stored width field is now read from bits 16 to 21 (the buffer width was already taken from the segment's pixel width). Seen: at the farm Lucy shows yellow fur and a red top, and the Whinny its yellow and red check. Not built: the skin override of item 5 (words 1 and 2 from the override set at 0x316CA0 while flag 216 and the variant key check hold), the variant presets of the kind-6 spell (item 3), the hero's colour block push.
- **Character draw mask (SPEC-0004 Drawing a node tree, item 3a):** region characters' models are now built with the region's draw mask, bit 30 (0x40000000): a type-4 node with nonzero layer flags keeps its subtree only when the flags share a bit with the mask, so of each outfit pair (427 nodes with bit 30 and 427 with bit 31 on disc) only the normal part draws; before, both parts drew on top of each other. Heroes and petpets keep every part (mask all ones). Not built: the colour override's mask (bit 31) and skin words, because the world's variant key that decides it has no known writer (SPEC-0022 Variant key check).
- **Zone columns in drawing (SPEC-0004 Drawing a node tree item 3 and Instanced meshes; SPEC-0028 section 7 byte 0x1B8; SPEC-0006 Binary grids; reverses the old R-0050 reading):** the tree walk gives every leaf the zone column of its nearest enclosing type-3 node (+0x50, low byte); type-20 instances keep their +0x40 byte and placed objects their 0x1B8 byte, read as stored; every world mesh part carries its column (its leaf's, else its instance's or object's). In the window a world part with a column draws only while the zone query passes for it with key -1 (the active block) from the camera's zone row, checked every frame, and a script-drawn placed object is hidden while its query fails. The second world is not gated (its block choice is the neighbour's and is not built). 1 test (leaves under a type-3 node take its column). Seen: the farm draws as before. Not built: nested type-3 nodes (the innermost column decides here; both would have to pass), the type-20 fade (the distance cull D1 was added later: Instanced-mesh draw distance).
- **UV scroll (SPEC-0003 UV modes, mode 2 and the material record; SPEC-0046 Level water):** the material reader keeps pass 0's UV mode (group 0x4C) and scroll speeds (0x60, 0x64); a part whose pass 0 scrolls gets its own material, blended by alpha (the source-over setting of 292 of the 294 water leaves) and unlit, whose UV offset is frac(s x N) per axis with N the play tick as a 16-bit counter, set every frame. 1 test (the spec's pack 1414 vectors: +0.75 and -0.75 after 600 passes, 0 after 800). Not seen on screen: pack 1414's start point is inside the library, away from the water. Not built: pass 1 (the second texture layer of two-pass water and the pass-1 environment maps; only pass 0 is drawn), the destination-alpha blend of two leaves, UV modes 1 and 3.
- **Sprite half-extents (SPEC-0034 2.3 node table, 4.1 step 7, 16.4):** the node's half-extent factors are now taken as lane x = +0x6C, lane y = +0x6E and lane z = +0x6A (before, in field order), and the sprite quad is laid out as 16.4 gives it: lane z is the half-width each side of the centre, lane x the height up to the top edge and lane y the height down to the bottom edge (a node whose y lane is 0 grows only upward), with the spin turning the picture counter-clockwise. 1 test updated (the spec's example factors 144, 289 and 0 at +0x6A, +0x6C and +0x6E give lanes 289/256, 0 and 144/256). Not built: the hero attack-effect driver of 3.17 (the Mote weapon lines, auras, charge-up, bursts, loops and the shot visual tables).
- **Hit flash (SPEC-0046 Per-frame draw order step 6; SPEC-0040 Behavior 26 item 9; Question 91):** a hit on the hero, or a hit through a character's hit routine, starts a 14-frame flash (the 4-frame and 10-frame stages together), during which every part of that character draws with (1.5, 0.5, 0.5) as its colour scale in place of its slot colour; the count runs down one a tick (60 a second). Not built: the two stages' separate shapes (the spec gives only the slot-1 vector, used for the whole ramp).
- **Sphere contact and item snap (SPEC-0018 Primitive class bits, Sphere contact; SPEC-0006 Kind 8 +0x21; SPEC-0021 Spawn routes):** each world keeps its collision spheres (the kind-1 type-11 records whose id word has class 0 or 1 and not class 2), filtered by the world collision mask like the push boxes; the hero's contact step, after the walls and boxes, moves the hero out of any sphere it is closer than r + 1.25 to, onto that distance along the line from the centre. Cones push nothing (they never fire on disc). A placed item whose snap byte (+0x21) is set spawns at the ground height probed from its stored y + 4.0, plus 0.5 (0.5 with no ground); others keep the stored height. 1 test (the class rule and the push vector). Seen: a farm trace walks as before. Not built: sphere contact for other characters (the walker), the further position and ground checks after a sphere push (Medium-Low in the spec), the scripted add by placement number.
- **Untextured part brightness, sneak reverse walk, Roberta's hold effect and camera shakes (SPEC-0046 Light slots and the lit draw; SPEC-0033 Behavior 5.1 and 4A.1; SPEC-0036 Roberta charge-up hold effect; SPEC-0027 Camera shake):** vertex colours are kept as GS output / 128, the unit a texture is modulated by; a part with no texture shows its output itself on the 0 to 255 scale, so its colours are now scaled by 128 / 255. NPC skin, hair and clothes are untextured parts with vertex bytes of 31 (1.94 after / 16), so their lit output of about 220 to 255 had been drawn as up to twice white and looked washed out; textured parts (byte 16) are unchanged. The sneak now enters state 39 (sneak reverse walk, clip 82) when the stick points more than 90 degrees from the facing while Square is held (the sign rule is Medium in the spec), and 40 otherwise. Roberta's effect 239 now shows while she is not yet charged: from the pass on which 0.8 s of sub-state 1 with F at or below 80 has built up (pass 49 with F rising from 1) until F is above 80 or the sub-state ends; before it showed only when charged. Every camera shake start now clears the elapsed time, the key index, the phase, the finished flag and all six keys, so each shake plays in full and a start replaces a running one; before, only the first shake of a session moved the view. 2 tests updated (the hold vectors; a second shake plays like the first). Seen: Lucy at the farm in tan fur, a pink skirt and a red top instead of near-white.
- **Ground probe list test (SPEC-0018 Ground probe step 3; R-0009):** when a grid cell holds two or more entries, a height patch is now a candidate only when it passes its outline list test: of its C outline records (0x70 bytes at entry +0x54, count at +0x50), count those that pass the z-span gate (origin z at +0x08, scalar at +0x68) and for which exactly one of Q . R > 0 and R.x > 0 holds (R the row at +0x10); the patch passes on an odd count or an empty list. Before, every patch of the cell was sampled over its whole rectangle, so a narrow raised surface such as the top of a wall or a ledge applied across the cell: at the farm the bushes beside the stone wall stood at about 51 over ground at 39, and others on ledges; they now stand at 39.2 to 39.4. The hero's spawn height stays 40.50 (the spec's worked example gives 40.4989 from entry 139). The probe is shared, so characters, items and the camera use the same rule. 1 test (the record rule and the z-span gate).
