Not built: the mask gate (+0x42) and count word (+0x44), whose run-time values the spec does not give, and owned entries (placed objects' patches are still skipped).
- **Plant colour (SPEC-0060 Layout +0x08; SPEC-0046 Fade level quantizer and draw colour scale; R-0072):** a plant's colour word is now taken as the draw context colour k = byte / 128 and sent as the colour scale of SPEC-0046: 1 when its RGB lie in 0.49 to 0.51 (the usual word 0x80404040), else 2k, with the alpha byte / 128 as opacity. Before, the bytes were applied directly as a multiplier, so every plant with the usual 64-grey word drew at half brightness: the farm's flower beds read as near-black blue and the grass tufts dark. The reading is Medium (how the word reaches the draw is not stated), so R-0072 asks. Seen: the farm's flower beds show blue and orange and the grass tufts their green.
- **World item models (SPEC-0021 Item model registry, Equipment display indexes and item display models, Ring layout and item draw; SPEC-0004 Item model registry key):** world items now draw with their registry model instead of a coloured cube. Ids 98 to 190 are built from resource 7's boot containers by header u16 = id - 98 (the coins 135 to 139, the Motes 156 to 173, the treasure map and the other boot models), and the potions 50 to 59 from resource 515 + id with resource 7's materials. Each item is turned by its spin angle, scaled by its drawn scale and faded by its life value (alpha). Ids with no registry model here (weapons and shields, which are registered only while equipped, and other ids) keep the diagnostic marker. Not modelled: the single world-item slot that lets only one potion kind be resident at a time, and the world item material list rules (each model draws with resource 7's list). Developer menu: a new entry drops a 141 NP coin split and the chosen item 6 units in front of the hero (NEOPETS_DEV=drop does it once at start). Seen on the farm: the gold and silver star coins, the 100 NP bag and the Fire Mote.
- **Chain bodies, modes 1 and 2 (SPEC-0040 Behavior 25):** Tor's tail (one chain on sub-part joints 8 to 12, link 0 from joint 0) and Roberta's two head chains (joints 7 to 10 from joint 3, 11 to 14 from joint 5) now run the pendulum step after each pose, with the spec's per-link constants (a, L, rest yaw and pitch, spring, yaw and pitch limits, velocity keep, drag). Each link takes its link joint's world frame from the pose (with the earlier links already stepped), springs its point toward the rest target, adds its velocity and the owner's unit displacement times the drag, measures and clamps the yaw and pitch, and writes them as the joint's local rotation with translation (0, 0, -a). The math is in neopets-sim (chain.rs, five tests); the app's HeroAnim runs it from the hero's position and facing. On load, each link is checked to hang from its link joint in the sub-part skeleton (both heroes pass). Choices: points seeded on the rest target (the spec allows it; the original's seed quirk is not reproduced); not done: the mode 2 axis negation when the root's forward axis points down (Low) and the stale tip matrix of the terminal joint. Seen: Tor's tail now hangs and curves from the hip instead of standing out straight. Swing while moving is not yet checked on screen.
- **Whinny pen (SPEC-0029 Behavior 5; R-0013, R-0073):** the Whinnies would run up to the pen's entrance and then turn away. In 909 the start mesh (1) overlaps the entrance mesh (5) only in a strip about 2.5 units wide along mesh 1's edge, and flee sub-step A's path test was read as "the 4-unit candidate lies on the mesh": a Whinny driven toward the pen had its candidate off the mesh before reaching the strip, took sub-step B (the far safe point) and ran back. The path test is now read as clear, the blocked latch alone choosing sub-step B, so the confinement stops the Whinny at the edge inside the strip and it switches to lead (R-0073 asks which test the original makes). The mesh handling now follows the spec: the start mesh is picked once (no later search), entering lead sets the entrance mesh directly, and becoming penned sets the inner mesh (the old fallback that searched for a ground mesh holding the pen point is gone). New sim test: the spec's test vector (a fleeing Whinny inside the entrance area gets the entrance mesh at once and leads into the pen). Herd trace (`--trace-to herd`, 6000 ticks): seeds 1, 3 and 7 pen all three and set flags 107 to 110 (before: two of three at most); on seed 11 the trace's hero keeps pushing the last Whinny into mesh 1's south edge (a trace limit, not a player one). NEOPETS_TRACE_PEN prints the pen's meshes when the script sets them.
- **Per-bone translation scale (SPEC-0005 Per-bone translation scale; SPEC-0006 kind 3 +0x8C):** a main skeleton definition's extra block (K floats per joint, header word 0x14; joint i's set at the end of the records plus its record's third field) is now read, and before combining, a main-family sample's translations (both keys) are multiplied by block[joint][sel - 1], sel being the character record's s16 at data +0x8C (0 or negative: no scaling; the heroes are 0). The shared townsfolk set (-7: header 492, body 526) carries adult proportions, so before this every smaller or larger townsfolk was posed with the wrong bone lengths: Lucy's spine, arms and hips were stretched (hip 1.08 against her mesh's 0.66, upper arm 0.64 against 0.25), and Hubert's neck was 0.19 against his mesh's 0.44, which sank his head into his chest. With the scale every main joint's translation matches the mesh to within about 0.01 (Lucy, Hubert and Patricia checked with NEOPETS_TRACE_RIG). New data test with the spec's vector (selector 1, 1.145 x 1.046 = 1.198). Seen: Lucy has her big head on a short body, and Hubert's head sits on his neck. Diagnostics: NEOPETS_DEV=view=NAME[:D] puts the free camera D units in front of a named character; NEOPETS_TRACE_RIG lists each region character's families and, with a name, its main joint translations on tick 30; townsfolk positions are in the trace's character line.
- **Bush bend and spring-back (SPEC-0060 Behavior 6 step 1, Spring-back):** each bush now keeps an up axis and is drawn along it. Within 5 units, with body contact and the hero moving faster than 0.1 units per second, the target axis is 0.76 U + 0.24 x the hero's facing, and when its y is below 0.994 the up axis becomes normalize(0.02 U + 0.98 T); a free bush springs back by up = normalize(0.925 up + 0.075 U). Key 5 (model 198) runs only its contact helper: the bend within 5 units and no spring-back beyond (its tilt is kept); a cut bush only springs back; a regrowing bush at counter 450 or below does nothing. Readings (Medium, labelled): the spring-back is skipped while the hero holds the bush (the bend latch reading of "not held by the hero bend"; applying it every step as well cancels most of the bend); the body test is the hero's feet within 0.75 plus the hero radius of the bush base horizontally, within 3 units of height; the speed is the hero's movement this tick times 60, because root motion, not the body velocity, moves our hero. Sim test with the spec's vector (speed 0.05 no lean; speed 2.0 facing +X gives (0.2940, 0.9558, 0)). Trace (NEOPETS_TRACE_BEND, walking through the farm bushes by the corral): up to four bushes lean to y 0.956 (about 17 degrees) as Tor passes and are upright again 40 ticks later. The G grass family has no hero reaction in the spec (only the sway phase and the close fade, not built). Not checked on screen.
- **Menu input (SPEC-0035 section 4; SPEC-0033 Controller map):** the front end's rising-edge and button words were built from this frame's presses but read only inside the 60 Hz tick loop, so a press that landed on a frame running no tick was lost: on a display faster than 60 Hz, or with uneven frames, menu moves were dropped or felt slow. Edges seen on a frame with no tick are now kept and handed to the next tick. The left stick now sets the direction bits by the spec's analog-to-digital rule (a byte below 0x20 sets Up or Left, above 0xE0 Down or Right; bytes X right 255, Y up 0), as rising edges in the edge word and in the held word for the sliders, never in the button-only word; before, the stick did nothing in the menus. Not tested with key presses in a window (no automated input); the title and play both run at 31 FPS on this machine with the simulation at 0.3 ms per tick, so the frame cost is in drawing or a window throttle, not checked further.
- **Display settings (this build's feature, no spec; the original has no display options, SPEC-0035 Behavior 3):** the Options page gets a fourth row, "Display", drawn under Credits in the item labels' font and colours (focus moves into it from the last item and out of it to the first), opening a Display page that shows the Options art without its item labels and five rows: Window (Windowed, Borderless, Fullscreen), Resolution (the primary monitor's video mode sizes, largest first; exclusive fullscreen uses the highest refresh rate at that size), Aspect ratio (Fill window, 4:3, 16:9, 16:10, 21:9), Render scale (50 to 200 percent) and Upscale filter (Smooth or Sharp). Up and Down move, Left and Right (or Cross) change the focused row, Triangle returns to the Display row. Settings are saved to private/display.txt; without the file the window is left as built and the resolution starts at its physical size. The 3D camera now draws into an offscreen image the size of the viewport (the window fitted to the aspect ratio) times the render scale, and a window camera (the default UI camera) shows it behind every UI node with a linear or nearest sampler; menus and the HUD draw on top at full resolution. The rows fall back to spellings the disc font can draw ("( )" for the focus marks and "percent" for "%", which it lacks). Tests: the viewport fit, the row stepping (wrap, off-list sizes), the settings file round trip. Seen: the Options and Display pages, 4:3 at 50 percent sharp (pillarboxed), 21:9 at 200 percent smooth (letterboxed, wider view). Not tested with key presses in a window, nor the fullscreen modes. NEOPETS_TRACE_PAGE now also takes options@n and display@n.
- **Enemy melee and missile defences (SPEC-0038 Behavior 44 outers 5 to 8, Layout 0xE0 to 0x10C; SPEC-0036 Hit detection step 4; R-0017):** the monster row's defence words are read (missile defend time 0xE0, missile dodge and block 0xE4 and 0xE8, melee block 0xEC to 0xF4 and dodge 0xF8 to 0x100 by swing class, defence timer 0x104 and 0x108, Blocking Angle 0x10C). Each agro tick the defence hold falls by one and base state 20 is dropped outside outers 7 and 8; then the missile defence (outside outer 10 and sequencer state 5, hold below 1: a hero shot's path from its position to position + velocity x 0xE0 meets the enemy's body capsule; int[1, 100] < 0xE4 gives outer 6, then int[1, 100] < 0xE8 gives outer 8) and the melee defence (latched on the edge of the hero starting a swing; outside outer 10 and outer 2 past sequencer states 0, 1 and 5; hold below 1; within 8.0 units and inside the 0.5 arc of the hero's facing; r1 at most the block chance gives outer 7, else r2 below the dodge chance gives outer 5) run before the attack choice, each setting the hold to 0xE0. Swing classes by hero and attack sub-state follow R-0017 (Tor's power step 10 never draws one). Outer 5 plays 55 and outer 6 plays 53 or 54 (int[0, 1]) to the end; outer 7 faces the hero and plays the block 56 with base state 20 while the hero swings (126 once on a blocked hit); outer 8 plays 56 while a hero shot is live; the exit is outer 0, base state 1 and a hold of int[0x104, 0x108] (a reversed pair drawn low to high). The hero's swing on a blocking enemy, outside the power step, is refused when dot(enemy facing, hero facing) is below the Blocking Angle and sets the block-hit flag. Flyer animations are not used (flyers do not run the AI). Disc: the farm's Flower Monsters have no defences; in 757 the Dumb Skeleton blocks 40/25/10 percent, the Skeleton Fighter 50/35/10, the Skeleton Archer dodges 100/100/50. Tests: the block on a swing edge and its exit, the reach and arc gates, the swing classes. The fight trace in 757 shows no defence, because its hero only swings while the skeleton is attacking (outer 2 past state 1) or reeling (outer 10), both excluded; not seen on screen. NEOPETS_TRACE_DEFENCE lists the enemies' defence words on tick 60 and each defence start.
- **Boss answers R-0069, R-0065, R-0066 (SPEC-0054 Chooser code 2; SPEC-0011 Main-loop launches; SPEC-0029 Behavior 6.4 to 6.6):** the Darkest Faerie's shove now runs as the spec's three cases: animation 9 not current requests it and does nothing more; current before its 0.99 gate applies the push once (first such tick); at the gate code 0 and animation 6 in the same tick, so the next tick picks the shove again and a cycle is K + 1 ticks (153 for her clip; the push lands on tick 1 of each cycle). The hero's collidable word is not cleared (not built). The Faerie Sisters' main-loop shots carry element -1 (only an all-element shield Mote reflects them), their speed uses the 3-alive word for alive -1 and 0 (0.5; 0.75 with 2 and 0.9 with 1), the release is event bit 0x20 or progress above 0.5, and each sister's controller start draws energy, status, group and special in that order by the alive word (energy treats -1 as 3 alive; the others 0 for -1 and 0; all four 0 at alive 0) with the dodge cooldown 0. The thorn hazard was added later (Thorn hazards). The Undead Draik's hit groups now use the spec's bones (left leg 25, 27, 28; right leg 26, 32, 33; wings 37, 47 to 51; arms 9 to 11 and 17 to 19; body 1 to 4, 7, 54, 57; head 54, 57) with bone 7's cylinder of radius 2.0; the mouth is bone 52; the beam test is the segment from the mouth to the moving end against a vertical cylinder around the hero raised 1.5 (height 1.75, radius 5.0); the spit is one of the Draik's own two records (released on event bit 0x20, speed and damage words by element, radius 0.5 against the hero's body capsules, ended at the ground probed from 8 above, no lifetime), drawn with the shot markers; the hazard throw is speed x unit((0, 12, 0) + 9u); the breath back-off walks each tick toward the point 5 units behind the Draik, away from the hero, until it is 10 or more away. Tests: the shove cycle vector, the sisters' start draws and speed labels, the spit records. Not checked on screen (no boss fight was run).
- **Boss answers R-0067 and R-0036 item 4 (SPEC-0029 Behavior 2; SPEC-0012, SPEC-0014; SPEC-0015 Start of the fight):** `enemy::slot_entry` gives the clip entry of a locomotion mode from the shared slot setter's seven values (modes 0 to 4 read values 1 to 5, mode 6 the 6th and mode 5 the 7th, -1 none). The Black Knight (3, 3, 3, -1, -1, 5, 4) and the Ixi Chief (9, 10, 30, 14, 15, 17, 16) now request their mode's entry whenever they travel by a mode without their own clip, and the Ixi Chief's charge walk (sub-step 1) plays its own slot write, entry 30 (sub-steps 4 and 5 already played entry 22). The shared root basis already sends a positive root x along up x forward (the character's left), so mode 5 circles to the boss's left and mode 6 to its right, as R-0067 states; the Werelupe King's and Gnarfas's strafe clips already matched their slot values. Neither the Black Knight nor the Ixi Chief runs in play yet (their controllers are not wired to the app), and the Gelert Assassin has no controller, so R-0067's Gelert clips and R-0068 (its word order, light arc ends and mirror axis) are recorded for when it is built. The Juppie's controller now requests animation 7 once when it starts, and its hub follows the spec's rule (nothing while 7 is current, 6 from any other animation, 7 when 6 reaches the 0.99 gate), so a fight opens on animation 7 instead of first playing 6. Tests: the slot entries, the Juppie start vector.
- **Levels and story (SPEC-0057 5A, SPEC-0047 Acquisition, SPEC-0049, SPEC-0053 3.4, 3.5 and 3.7; R-0042):** checked against the updated specs: the 1090 hurt zones (20 HP once every 61 ticks while the hero stands in a firing hazard) and the 1381 falling hazards (5 HP and 2 s, only after the pad with trigger index 3) already matched; packs 706, 800 and 886 already load their wild-petpet regions by the highest set story flag (708 to 712, 802, 890); the new game at start index 0 applies no stage rows, as SPEC-0049 states. Not built: module 1089 (the crusher sequence with its hazard thread, button stops and timeout kill), which needs animated locators (SPEC-0030 Note on animated locators) and the group records' object ids, which the spec does not copy (R-0074). New: the act-start stage 2 moves the party inventory and wallet to the stash (container B), clears the party and gives the kit through the normal add (items 150 x 3, 149 x 3 and the dress 127, and coin 135 x 1150 as 1150 Neopoints); stage 3 merges the stash back (an item the party does not hold takes the stash's count, the wallets add, the stash is cleared); the stash and its wallet are saved at 0x540 and 0x204 and restored on load. Stages 2 and 3 no longer write the hero's action state (the spawn at entry 0 is kept). The swap's first flag now plays sound 0xB0 and starts swap effect 4025 at the outgoing hero (step 2) and 4027 there at the end flags (step 10); the second effect, 4026, is never started, as the spec says. The effects are started as one-shots (released at once with their spawners running on) rather than held until step 16. Trace (NEOPETS_TRACE_ACT=2@60): Roberta selected, NP 0 to 1150, the party holds 127 x 1, 149 x 3 and 150 x 3. Not checked on screen.
- **Audio and the map page (SPEC-0031 Stopping a child and the sample classes; SPEC-0051 sections 3, 4, 7 and 8; R-0070, R-0047):** the music rules were already met: every stop of a bank child (command 0x03, 0x11, the end) keys off its voices into their own release envelopes (`release_child`), and the 312 "other" samples, whose last frame carries all three bits, loop that frame (now pinned by a data test). The map page now draws the real art: each icon is child (kind) of the group node with key 210 (records 711, 712, 713), centred on its projected point, the cursor icon scaled by the pulse; the hero marker is sprite node key 26's 32 x 32 art, drawn first, turned by the marker angle and squashed to 6/7 of its width (rendered into a 48 x 48 image by the inverse map), tinted by the pulse; the overlay group (key 214: the Triangle and Cross hints with Exit and View Quest, the L2 and R2 tabs and the Treasures group) is drawn every tick with offset (0, 0); the area name node (key 215) has its stored text replaced by the area's name in font 0, centred at x 256 on baseline 40; the cursor label is font 2, centred, at least x 16, 14 above the point. The Treasures line (predicate slot 73 while the total is nonzero) draws its two value-text nodes (variables 16 and 17) from the collected table (table 6, read now): the total counts every row naming the area, the collected count the set ones. Value-text nodes of other variables are still not drawn. Seen: the farm's map with the Herding Whinnies medal, the arrow marker, the hints, "Ellis Family Farm" and "Treasures: 0 of 4".
- **Book pages and HUD item icons (SPEC-0035 6.1 rules 4 to 14; SPEC-0024 Book records; SPEC-0023 sections 11 and 30; R-0071, R-0020, R-0018):** a book record now gives its style index, title and one body string (newlines end lines; two make a blank line), its glyph font from the layout chunk (type 2001; the shared front-end font parser `frontend::parse_font`) and its paper picture (128 x 128) and glyph sheet (256 x 256) from the second texture chunk. `neopets_sim::book::layout` lays the body out by rules 9 and 10: the style's template row (23 rows naming which block x, y and line-limit words each block uses; the words 0x2DB2B4 to 0x2DB2D4 are read from the disc), block A then block B, lines past both limits dropped, breaking at width 192 on the last space or underscore (the candidate consumed; with no candidate the overflowing byte is lost), and justification of a line that ended at a candidate with a candidate inside (e = min(L / n, 2.0) on every glyph, then min(rest / k, 2.5 x the space advance) on each candidate). The reader draws the flat page (rule 11: the paper over 448 x 448 and the lines in the book's glyph sheet in (100, 100, 100, 128); the shared glyph renderer `frontend::render_glyphs`) centred on the canvas, and the title in font 0 centred on x 256 at baseline 40 in (112, 112, 2, 128). Not built: the 3D page sheet and the decoration parts, the tilt by the page angle and the placement before the camera (rule 12, Low for the placement); so the page is shown flat and its first lines sit close under the title. The HUD's six item-icon elements (the quick-item cross, entries 0 to 3, and the selected item 0xA0 and 0xA1) now draw the art of the child of node key 0x9F at the item's id while its count is above 0 (none past the last child). The Square caption already matched (font 1, left edge 327, baseline 395, colour 160, 160, 90, 127). Tests: the wrap, break and justification rule, the template rows, the body string. Seen: book 7 (pack 777) with paper, the book's glyphs and justified lines on the right page; the quick-item cross with its item icons. NEOPETS_TRACE_QUICK=n presses Up on tick n.
- **World breakables (SPEC-0048 Breakable entry, states, draw, Break routes, Break effect, Placed drops on break; SPEC-0028 section 7 second list; SPEC-0036 Hit detection step 6; R-0040):** `neopets_data::world::breakables` reads the type-29 second list (count +0x08, offset table +0x0C, each entry at its offset from the table): the draw matrix, the state word, the mesh (the inline type-12 chunk at +0x44, or the near mesh of record +0x48 of the first type-20 chunk), the piece count, spawn key, drop placement, script index, break float, contact material, element class, start mode and the type-11 volume at +0x60 (kind byte, centre, radius, height, axis, rectangle axes and extents). Each world keeps its breakables with their live states; an entry keeps its pack state at each load (start modes 1 and 2 apply only with the broken bit set and no disc entry has them). Entries in states 1 and 3 are drawn with their own matrix after the placed objects. The hero melee route runs on every tick of the swing window: the swing triangle (P0 and the current and previous-tick tips) against each intact entry's volume (sphere: an edge within the radius; cylinder: an edge meeting it from its base along its axis; rectangles: an edge against the box), the element gate (class 0 or 7, or the slot-2 Mote's kind), the break from state 1 (state 2 and the drop action for the region placement, no luck gate) and the contact cue by material (143, 145, 146, else 144), which plays on a state-3 entry too. Not built: the script hook (RegisterScriptBreakable), Tor's recoil on an element mismatch, the spawn by key, the piece effects, the timed effect, the persistent broken bits, Roberta's shot and the attack-family and boss routes, and the level scripts' Break, MakeBreakable and SetBreakableActive calls. Test: an entry with a sphere volume parsed from synthetic bytes. Trace (NEOPETS_TRACE_BREAK, pack 732: 37 breakables, all with meshes and cylinder volumes): Tor standing 2.4 units from breakable 7 breaks it on his first swing (tick 62) and its drop placement 19 is paid out.
- **Hero attack-effect driver (SPEC-0034 3.17 and Where it lives; SPEC-0034 birth modes 2 and 3):** the effect objects gain birth modes 2 and 3 (a spawn point along the segment from the object's frame to its target; test on synthetic records) and a settable target. Play reads the six attack-family tables of 14 ids each from the boot executable at runtime (line 0x315280, aura 0x3153D0, charge-up 0x315398, burst 0x315328 and 0x315360, charged hold 0x3152B8 and 0x3152F0, tip light 0x315588), indexed by the slot-2 Mote id − 160. Once per pass for the active hero it computes the near-hand point N = position + 0.48 Z0 and the tip T = position + R Z0 (Z0 attach point 0's third axis, R the weapon reach), both moved 0.2 toward the camera eye. Tor keeps the weapon line (at N, target T) and the tip light (at T); Roberta keeps the aura and the charge-up effect at the hero, the charge-up only until the press passes 80. On the first pass of sub-state 1 past the full-press threshold (50 Tor, 80 Roberta) the burst is played once, detached (243 for Tor and 240 for Roberta with no Mote), and the charged-hold loop runs at N with target T until sub-state 1 ends (245 for Roberta with no Mote). The swing glows 244, 243 and 246 start 0.25, 0.2 and 0.25 s into Tor's sub-states 2, 3 and 4, and Roberta's spin glow 242 starts at once in sub-states 6 and 7. Each glow follows T while its sub-state lasts and is released to play out on a change. A role whose wanted id changes stops and releases its old effect. Not built: Roberta's shot visuals. Trace (NEOPETS_TRACE_FX with NEOPETS_TRACE_MOTES="-,-,164"): the tables read as ids 1500 to 1513, 531 to 537, 570 to 591 and 1300 to 1306. Mote 164 starts line 1506 and light 1302 on tick 1, and the glow starts in sub-states 2 and 3 of a combo and is released on the change. Unverified: the burst and hold (the trace's autojump ends sub-state 1 before tick 50 of a press), all of Roberta's roles, and how any of it looks on screen.
- **Movable blocks and the hero's block state (SPEC-0048 Movable blocks: Source, Placement, Push start, Second clearance test, Per tick, Solid for characters; SPEC-0033 Behaviors 4A.2 and 15A; SPEC-0022 Square chooser; SPEC-0028 section 6 n1 entries; R-0063 answered):** `neopets_data::trigger::movable_blocks` reads the type-11 n1 entries (count at header +0x1C, table at +0x44, 0x24 bytes; payload-relative pointers) whose kind-4 box has flag bit 1: size, height, model word, inline mesh and the box (corner, axes A, B and vertical, extents). The data inspector's `--blocks` census finds 90 blocks in 16 packs, all with inline meshes (the spec's count; pack 1344's entry has no box); pack 909's block is 2.759 by 2.741 and pack 1176's has the reversed axes, as the spec says. `neopets_sim::block` has the record and its placement (ground probe at the corner with ceiling + 3.0, position and home at corner + 0.5 S A + 0.5 S B, L = 0.5 S, active), move to locator and reacquire, the push start (not moving, a direction, the second clearance test's four relations and its table, n as a signed byte), the per-tick step (L / n for n ticks, the flag clearing on the tick after), the current-block scan, the character contact (the box at the placement corner plus the displacement, radius 0.75, nearest first within 10) and the face finder; and state 18: the approach to the grab spot (centre minus the face normal times S / 2 + 1.5, latch at 0.25, turn at 0.25 or walk with clip 2), then the grab (facing the face normal, the stick on the normal and the side axis: 42 push toward m, 41 pull toward m + 2, 144 and 145 side moves toward m + 1 and m + 3, 40 still, 41 and 42 back to 40 at 0.95), with the move once per clip cycle while the clip phase is strictly between 0.05 and 0.15 and n = keys − whole cursor − 2, doubled for the push. In play each world's blocks are built at load and step after the object animations; the hero's contact includes them (not in states 18, 19, 30, 41 and 58); the Square chooser shows Grab block (label 10) after the Miamouse catch when the face finder finds a face of the hero's current block, and Let go (label 20) in state 18 with the stick centred on clip 40; an accepted push plays cue 11 and the strain voice (Tor 33, Roberta 57). Blocks draw their inline mesh (local, about the base centre) at the block position with axes B, vertical and A. A block's box is no longer also a static push box (pack 1298's has bit 0; R-0077). Not from the spec: while he holds a block the hero moves with its horizontal motion, because the push clips carry no root motion here (R-0077); the approach turns on the spot in clip 0 (the walk-turn clip is not given). Not built: the footprint step and the pull-back clearance (no disc block has a footprint word), the predicted-position contact test, the block-slide cue and side-touch latch, the climb onto a block (state 58) and the block-edge hang (state 19), the trigger volumes a moving block fires, the attached effects and sound index, the level scripts' block calls (ActivateMovableBlock, MoveMovableBlockToLocator, ReacquireMovableBlockHeightPatch; the puzzles of 1324 and 1381), and region characters' contact with blocks. Tests: placement and a 38-tick push (the spec's 0.0717 a tick), the clearance relations, the face finder's spec vectors, the hold's push and pull lengths (38 and 23, the spec's vector) and the window, and the contact. Trace (NEOPETS_TRACE_BLOCK, pack 1394's cube): walking at its west face shows Grab block, the press enters state 18, the hero walks to the grab spot and latches, and the push moves the cube 2.726 along +X in 38 ticks, again every 43 ticks, with the hero following; from the east face it moves toward −X. A screenshot shows the crate drawn in its gap of the ledge. Unverified: how a push looks in the window, the side moves and the pull in play.
- **Block-fired triggers, the block script calls and the 1324 plate puzzle (SPEC-0048 Trigger volumes; SPEC-0030 Layout section 3 +0x28 and rows 18, 22 and 63; SPEC-0041 1324; SPEC-0042 1381):** a trigger record keeps its last fire argument (+0x28: the block's number, -1 for hero fires) and the trigger call passes it to the scripts. Each tick a block that moved (or stopped on that tick) clears its last-fired volume and fires every volume its upright cylinder touches (radius S, 0.6 S against a moving rectangle, height H) under the block gate (spheres and cylinders with bit 3, static kind-4 rectangles with bit 8, moving ones with bit 5; kinds 2 and 7 never), with object 0 and its number; an idle block fires its last volume again every tick. A record a block fired that decays (the block left) still runs its callback, so the scripts see state 3 (other modules' dispatch is unchanged). New level actions ActivateMovableBlock, MoveMovableBlockToLocator and ReacquireMovableBlockHeightPatch act on the current world's blocks. 1324: Init's unsolved side registers the stage-2 plates (1, 0) and stage-1 plates (2 to 4) and its thread moves blocks 0 to 2 to locators 6 to 8; the solved side freezes blocks 0 to 2 and puts blocks 3 and 4 on their plates; callback 1 (stage-2 plates, block fires only) moves the plate's marker block under a fade with its camera and the hero, sets its mark, and runs the solved scene with both marks and stage 1 done, and on the block's leaving clears the mark and sets the beam; callback 2 (stage-1 plates) swaps the plate object, freezes the block, plays the plate scene (camera 2, the pressed-set object, the unplaced blocks reacquired), sets the beam (13, 12 or 14 at 4, 2 or 1 s) and with all three pressed finishes stage 1 (camera 8, then the solved scene or the marked plate's handler again); the solved scene sets 239 with the fades and the ten chimes (movie 23 is not played). Unverified reading: on a block's leaving with one mark left the beam is 10 for mark A and 11 for mark B. 1381: pad 52 resets blocks 6 to 56 to locators 12 to 62; a solved station enables block k; the arrival restore activates blocks 1 to 5 of solved stations and moves solved pieces' blocks to locator k + 1. Not working in play: 1324's stage-1 plates are at y 31 to 41 and the bells stand at y 0, so no block reaches them (R-0078); 1381's goal and arm routines were registered later (see the 1381 block group). Tests: the 1324 plates (the spec's plate 0 and plate 2 vectors, a hero fire ignored, the third press re-running the marked plate, the solve and the leaving), the block gate, the block cylinder and the record's block number. Trace (NEOPETS_TRACE_BLOCK): in 1324 blocks 0 to 2 stand at locators 6 to 8 after Init; the survey lists 1324's plate volumes and 1381's; pushing in 1394 is unchanged.
- **Standing on, climbing onto and hanging from blocks, and blocks solid to characters (SPEC-0033 Floor-contact flag, Behaviors 15, 15A, 50 and 51 states 19 and 58; SPEC-0048 Solid for characters; SPEC-0040 Behavior 14A):** the motion record has the floor-contact flag: the record tick clears it and a block contact against a top face (|n.y| above 0.9) sets it, reads the gap as 0 and removes the downward velocity and force, so the hero stands and walks on a block top the ground probe does not report, and the air phases 2 to 5 count it as landed. The block contact also reports the side-touch latch (a side face of a block whose top is 1.0 to 5.0 above). The push-into-face counter (reset with no face, up to 15) and the climb-onto-block fit test (the loaded top 0.2 to 3.5 above the feet, the facing within the face, the stick pushed in) enter state 58: the hero is taken to the block position less the face direction times S / 2 + 0.75 at the top less 1.2 with clip 70, then clip 134 (whose root motion rises 1.22) to 0.95, then state 4 with the stick forward or state 1. A Cross press on the ground or air phases 0 to 5 with the side-touch latch and a face enter state 19: grab 72, the edge point (the face less S / 2 + 0.35 at the top less 3.7), 71 to 0.95, the hang idle 73 with Let go (label 20) and the shimmy 61 and 60 within 1.0 of the edge's ends, and with the stick forward the pull-up 59 (rising 3.71) to 0.95, then state 4 or state 1 moved 0.1 in. No gravity in states 19 and 58, and their root motion is not flattened onto the ground. The deadly-ground kill reads the probed gap, so a block top over lava is safe. Region characters of the current world are pushed out of the active blocks after they move. Not from the spec (R-0079): the approach to the edge point eases half the remaining way each tick; state 19's height is read as the block top less 3.7; the shimmy's edge direction; a top-face push leaves the point 0.01 inside the bound so the flag holds every tick; the characters' pass runs after all movement and moves only the position. Not built: the event-bit 0x40 test at the exits (the stick alone decides), the block-slide cue, the predicted-position contact test. Tests: the contact's floor and side touch, the fit test's spec vectors (909's block passes, pull-back and side push fail, the 5.452 cube is too tall), and the hang and climb sequences. Traces (NEOPETS_TRACE_BLOCK with NEOPETS_TRACE_CLIMB, NEOPETS_TRACE_PUSH and NEOPETS_TRACE_CROSS; NEOPETS_TRACE_ROOTS for the clips' root totals; NEOPETS_TRACE_NPCBLOCK) in pack 909: walking into the block's south face climbs it on tick 122 and ends on top at 48.2 (top 48.24), walks across and drops off the far side to the floor; Cross at the north face hangs (72, 71, 73, Let go shown), and the stick forward pulls up onto the top and lands there; the block moved onto a Flower Monster pushes it 1.25 out. Unverified: how these look in the window, a hang on a block taller than 3.7 above the floor, the shimmy.
- **Controller rumble and the pad stick scaling (SPEC-0033 Behavior 1C and Behavior 2; SPEC-0036 sub-state 13; SPEC-0034 positional mode 0):** `neopets_sim::rumble` is the rumble manager: the five presets read from the boot program at run time (0x2DD270: constant, ramp up, ramp down, up to the midpoint and down, up-hold-down), a request built into up to three segments in six entries with the merge rule (so preset 0 is one entry and the shipped preset-2 request one ramp that does not reach 0), and the per-pass step (count down, hand-off to the linked segment, the strongest entry's level trunc(clamp(i) x 255) sent with its countdown, a repeat of a steady level not re-sent). Entries run down with Vibration off and nothing is sent then; the menu pass (status 9) advances nothing. Sources built: Tor's leap landing (sub-state 13, the request from 0x2DD7AC to 0x2DD7B4: preset 2, 0.8 s, 0.6), and animation-event effect entries with positional mode 0 at their start (preset 0, the entry's duration, its intensity with the inner and outer radius falloff from the camera). The window sends each level to the first connected controller's strong (large) motor for its countdown, replacing the last one. The pad sticks now pass through the PS2 byte and the left-stick dead zone and scale ((r - 167.5) / 87.5 and (r - 87.5) / 87.5, 0 for the bytes 88 to 167), so a resting stick does not drift; the keyboard is unchanged. Not built: monster footstep rumble (the magnitude by class is not given), the grab-and-throw source, the pad-side gate quirk. Tests: the spec's rumble vectors (preset 0 1.0 s 0.7 sent once at 178 for 57; preset 2 0.8 s 0.6 from 149 down to 12 over 44 sends; 0.5 s 0.6923 at 176; the stronger request wins and then the other at 127; preset 5 and a frozen pass do nothing), the falloff, and the stick byte values (255, 200, 168, 167, 88, 87, 40, 0). Trace (NEOPETS_TRACE_RUMBLE): the presets read from the disc are the spec's shapes and the landing request is (2, 0.8, 0.6); in pack 985's fight effects rumble at level 76 for 117 ticks and 255 for 27. Unverified: the motor on a real controller.
- **Developer info panel (diagnostic tooling, no spec):** in play the panel with the disc, build, simulation and control text now starts hidden (`--show-info` starts it shown; `--hide-hud` still hides it in the browser shell). Its toggle moved from Tab to F6, because Tab is the keyboard's Start key and opened the game menu and the panel together. Checked by a screenshot of play started without `--hide-hud`.
- **Play fixes from the user's report (SPEC-0046 Sky domes; SPEC-0033 Behavior 7; SPEC-0029 Behavior 5 and SPEC-0056 Far centroid pick, R-0073 answered):** (1) Sky: play drew the whole dome resource, so the role-10 layer D (its purple cap, colour (110, 79, 122)) showed straight up; layer D draws only while the atmosphere blend factor is above 0, which play does not compute yet, so play now draws layer A (role 1) only, as world scenes already did. (2) Camera on slopes: the hero sinks into a slope until the ground constraint's 0.1 tolerance and then steps up 0.1 (every ~18 ticks walking uphill); the camera followed that step. It now follows the hero height follow of Behavior 7: the drawn copy's height catches up quadratically (q = 0.05 g^2, gain 0.04), in the jump states 7 to 11 and 43 to 46 only after 120 ticks, a drop over 0.5 or a gap over 3.5, and stops within 0.05; it resets at a teleport. The hero model is still drawn at the world height (the reader of the drawn copy's other use is not established). Test: a 0.1 rise closes smoothly; a jump's rise waits and a drop starts at once. (3) Whinny flee: sub-step A's path test is now the spec's (the 4-unit candidate on a triangle of the current mesh, edges inclusive), the latch clears when a flee sub-step ends, and sub-step B's safe point is the random far-centroid pick (up to T uniform draws) instead of the first centroid in triangle order, which sent every blocked Whinny to the same corner. Herd trace (`--trace-to herd`, 9000 ticks): all three penned in seeds 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 7 by 97, 76, 120, 112, 39 and 75 seconds; before, seeds 1 and 7 never finished. (4) The developer free camera takes a controller: both stick clicks together (L3 + R3) toggle it, the left stick moves, the right stick looks, R2 and L2 rise and sink, and the south button is fast. Unverified: the sky and the camera in the window (the camera change is checked by the test only).
- **Block answers R-0077 to R-0079 and placed-object ground (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 4A.2 and 51; SPEC-0048 Solid for characters, The block's moving-rectangle test; SPEC-0018 Patch-id table and Ground probe steps 2 and 4; SPEC-0028 section 7):** State 18's grab phase now places the hero on the grab spot (the block position less the face normal times S / 2 + 1.5, at the block's height) every tick, including the latching tick, with force and velocity cleared; this replaces the earlier stand-in that moved him by the block's motion, and the approach turn requests the idle picker's clip. States 19 and 58 approach their points by the spec's fixed steps (0.5 a tick along the 3D line, snapping within 0.25, for the hang; 0.2 snapping within 0.2 for the climb) instead of easing; the hang leaves to state 1 when its point is below a hero on the ground (so a block lower than 3.7 is never hung from), the shimmy's along position uses A = up x D (a right push plays 61 toward along 0 above 1.0, a left push 60 below S - 1.0) and a running shimmy cycle returns to 73 at 0.95; both states steer to the face at turn rate 0.5. The ground probe now reads placed objects' inline model sets (object +0x1D4, registered under the first word of the set's payload): an entry with a non-zero patch id is tried only while its object is drawn, at the point mapped from the object's current frame into its rest frame (d1, d2 along the current X and Z axes, p' = T + d1 A + d2 B) with the height raised by O.y - T.y. Each world refreshes the poses of its drawn patch-owning objects every tick and before a block placement. A moving block fires a moving rectangle (kind 4, switch above 0) by the five-face rule: its position within the box footprint widened by 0.6 S on every side, with u above -H. Tests: the owner mapping vector (p' = (97, 202), raised by 3), the plate 2 vector (no hit at z 3.608, a hit at 3.565, none with the bell at 0.0), and the hang and climb steps. Disc checks: in pack 1324 objects 15 to 29 register patch ids 16 to 30, the probe at (-19.082, 11.697) under 36.076 returns 31.1915 (the spec's value), the bells stand at 31.19, and a trace pushing bell 0 south fires plate 2 with block number 0 on tick 298, step 35 of the sixth push (the spec's worked example), running the plate scene (camera 2). Not built: the block contact's second (predicted-point) test (the 0.01 rest stands in; the flag is the same), region characters' block pass inside their motion tick and its velocity response, the mask and count gates of the probe, the touched flag's early climb snap. Unverified: the rest of the 1324 puzzle in play.
- **Ladders (SPEC-0033 Behaviors 13 and 22; SPEC-0006 Type 10; SPEC-0040 Behavior 19 gate):** `neopets_data::trigger::panels` reads the type-10 chunk's groups and their 0x60-byte records (origin, face plane, side axis U and up axis V with their offsets, lengths LU and LV, type -3 ladder or -2 climb panel); the chunk's offsets count from the chunk's first byte, 16 before the payload. The data inspector's `--panels` census finds 218 ladders and 235 climb panels in 63 packs, every record's origin inside its group's radius. `neopets_sim::ladder` has the grab test (facing and stick, or the facing with the stick centred, into the face below -0.8; -r to 1.25 r from the plane; 0 to LU along U; -1.0 to LV - (H + 1.25) along V, LV from the ledge hang; the stick above 0.1) and state 41: the hero faces the ladder at rate 1.0 with his motion cleared; the mount moves each axis at most 0.1 a tick to 0.9 out, 0.8 along U and the nearest rung (0.45 + 1.25 k), then rung A (93) with a gap of 1.5 or less, else 120 with cue 140 to its end; the stick forward climbs (91 or 90, then the other rung at 0.99 with cue 140), and at the top (LV - H - 1.3 or 1.27) climbs off with 59 and then 135 to 0.99; pulled back it climbs down (95 or 94) or, at a height of 0.5 or below or a gap below 0.5, steps off; a Cross edge jumps off (-0.5 times the facing, +2.0 up, the 30-tick grab cooldown). In play the world keeps its ladder records; the grab test runs from the ground states and while jumping; on the ladder gravity is off and the rung clips' root motion (about 1.25 a rung, 3.8 for 135) is not flattened onto the ground; Stop Climbing (label 18) lets go; the hero's frame-record wall contact is skipped in states 21, 23, 30, 35, 37, 38, 41 and 47 (SPEC-0040 Behavior 19). Tests: the grab gates, the mount to the first rung, a rung step with its cue, the climb off the top, stepping off and the jump off. Trace (pack 986, ladder 4, NEOPETS_TRACE_PUSH and NEOPETS_TRACE_CLIMB): the hero grabs on tick 18, climbs ten rungs from 102.0 to 114.6, climbs off the top with 59 and 135 and stands at 119.25 (the ladder top 119.31) on tick 410. Pack 909's ladder starts at 50.38 above a floor at 45.5 (out of reach from the ground or the box top: the box and a jump reach it), as its tutorial line says. Not built: the climb panels (state 21), the grab from the ledge hang (state 30 is not built), Roberta's step at the top, the record flag at the top.
- **Spec update, first pass (SPEC-0060 Plant colour in the draw, R-0072; SPEC-0007 When a hit on a hero takes effect; SPEC-0036 Hittable predicate; SPEC-0034 size factors; SPEC-0033 1A.1):** plants drawn by their near model now ignore the colour word (the default colour, opaque); a family with no near model (grass, drawn by its far sprite) takes the word as the MODULATE vertex colour (each byte / 128, so 64 grey is half brightness, and the alpha byte / 128 as its opacity). The earlier context-colour reading is withdrawn. Common enemies' attack choice now uses the hero's hittable query: no new attack and no cooldown countdown while the hero is in states 16, 17, 25, 33, 36, 48, 50 to 55, 57, 59 or 60, in Roberta's leap and landing sub-states, under the script hold or with a dialogue box up; there is no invulnerability window (a repeat hit deducts in full and the flinch clip, already requested, does not restart, as before). Checked and already matching: the effect size factors (x +0x6C, y +0x6E, z +0x6A) and the sneak controls (Square held, the stick back 39 and forward 40). REQUESTS.md: the 71 open rows marked answered by the specs whose Answers lines name them, and R-0080 asks for the ledge hang's inequalities and target points (the in-air ledge chooser reads as contradictory), so states 30, 35, 37 and 47 are not built. Not yet applied from this update: the Mote attack families of SPEC-0036, the boss fixes of SPEC-0011 to 0019 and 0029, SPEC-0055 2A's death flow, module 1089's crusher records, SPEC-0061's skip list, SPEC-0007's tick order, SPEC-0046's draw items (placed-object frames, the HUD coin, Tor's element glow, the world mask sites), SPEC-0026's menu controller map. Unverified: grass in the window (a farm screenshot shows the flowers and bushes at full colour).
- **Spec update, second pass (SPEC-0055 2A; SPEC-0011 Movement speed; SPEC-0056 Far centroid pick; SPEC-0012, SPEC-0014 checked):** a load or Continue now puts the selected hero in state 59 (clip 175, the arrival pose, no stick, back to state 1 at 0.99) at the saved pose, as SPEC-0055 2A step 6 and the resume table say. The far centroid pick is one navmesh routine shared by the Whinny's safe point and the Sisters. In the Sisters' alternate loops a hero within 5.0 starts the avoiding mode: the sister runs to the far centroid pick of her closest ground mesh (10 from the hero) at twice the movement speed and makes no attack decision, until she is within 2.0 of it or blocked. Test: the avoid run and its end. Checked against the update and already matching: module 735's death callback and post-fight word (kept inverted: 0 means set), the Black Knight's speed multiplier (written, never read), the Ixi Chief's push K x normalize(9 d + 12 up), the switch chord's ready states. Not applied: the Ixi Chief's height-check lock word and its touched-flag body contact (a 2.0 distance stands in), the Plague Serpent and Juppie notes (their set-back and slot rules), the remaining bosses.
- **World item models, lock chest objects and the farmhouse fire (SPEC-0004 and SPEC-0006 type 8 global model ids; SPEC-0021 Item model registry; SPEC-0048 Set-up poses by kind; SPEC-0028 Draw gate and section 9; user report):** a world item whose id is not a boot model (98 to 190) or a potion (50 to 59) is now drawn with the spawning world pack's type-8 child of that role id (world packs register their children as global model ids), so the farm's bucket (item 6, at the porch) draws as a wooden bucket instead of the green diagnostic marker; the model cache is keyed by (id, pack) for these. A lock chest's group objects other than object 0 are no longer drawn at rest: its set-up poses only object 0, so the others keep the disc state 3 and stay hidden until a script turns them on (the draw gate); pack 951's chest no longer shows its object 1 as a pale block over the lid. Other group kinds whose set-up is not built keep the at-rest stand-in. The farmhouse fireplace (pack 951 placed effects 0 to 2, effect 108) shows no flames: by the specs nothing sets their active flag (the disc flag is 0, module 951 starts no effect, its only group is the chest, and the switch path reaches only dig sites); R-0081 asks. Checked by screenshots of the porch bucket and the chest.
- **Spec update, boss pass (SPEC-0054 Activation and open question 22; SPEC-0011 to 0020, 0029 and 0038 checked):** module 1432's activate-world callback now also starts placed effects 18 to 25. Checked against the update and already matching: the Plague Serpent's phase and engagement gates (idle or belching only) and its slot pairs, the Juppie's start on animation 7 and its unreachable set-backs (left as code that never runs), the Black Knight's unread speed multiplier, the Werelupe King's per-attack damage and the boomerang's 65.0 gate (its hit cylinder, radius 2.5 and length 0.25 along the flight direction, is still approximated as an upright disc against the hero's body), Gnarfas's four locomotion modes, the common enemies' hittable gate (applied in the first pass), the projectile pool's six effect ids and flight effects. R-0082 asks where the enemy's 11-entry effect table (the missile trail, SPEC-0038 and SPEC-0029 Behavior 13) lives; no spec gives it, so the common enemies' missiles stay plain markers. Not applied: the Ixi Chief's height-check lock word, the Draik spit's flight and impact effects (527, 528, 991, 992; the spit setup's effect ids), the grab-and-throw, the Gelert Assassin.
- **Spec update, Mote attack families (SPEC-0036 Charm attack hits, Family A users, Tor Mote object placement):** Lava 161's orbit is now centred on the hero raised by 2.0 with the offset radius x (cos, 0, -sin) on world axes, its angle starting at 0 and continuing across spawns; Supernova 173's cylinder sits on the hero raised by 1.5 each tick; Wind 167's cylinder follows the ground probed from 8 above the hero (the previous height kept on a miss). Family A is new: Water 163 (radius 0.5 + 14.5 x age/0.7, 42 ticks) and Nova 172 (18 x age/1.2, 72 ticks) are vertical cylinders of height 4.0, and Rock 165 releases three stationary sub-shots started 16 ticks apart (radius 20 x age/0.6, 36 ticks, each with its own ten-slot list). Tests cover the spec vectors (7.75, 9.0, 10.0 and the orbit's quarter turn). Unverified: Water and Nova's attach points are taken as the spawn point (hero plus facing x reach). Not applied: Shadow 169 and Sun 171 (their radius is a live effect field with no number given), the effect objects' visuals.
- **Module 1089, the crusher sequence (SPEC-0042 1089; SPEC-0030 rows 3, 12, 14, 20, 124, 266 and the Note on animated locators; SPEC-0028 object +0x1D8; SPEC-0057 5A):** the placed objects' inline type-11 headers are now read (`neopets_data::trigger::object_header`: the trigger volumes and the inline locator list; `neopets-data --object-headers`, with NEOPETS_HEADERS_PACK for one pack). On disc pack 1089's object 0 has the 33 locators and the four button cylinders (radius 4.224, length 2.807) of the spec, and object 7 the 12 hazard locators. The host now keeps object links (MoveObjToAnimatedLocator with follow: the object's base is the host locator's current position by formula A), moves group effects to animated locators, sets speed factors, and tests registered animated triggers in the host object's current pose. The module: Init (T0 on trigger 0, the four animated triggers to routine K, the 24 moves), T0 (the four rounds backwards at -1 and -2 with sleeps 30, 10, 40 and 10, objects 0, 8, 11, 10, 9 and 7 at 0.6, then the latch and the hazard word), routine K (the Square prompt, the press plays, the counter, 1 s, the group's effects), thread A (1 point and a 0.5 s wait per hazard locator within 6.0 and less than 2.0 below the hero), and Update (the effect moves, the first-tick activation, the 18.4 mark, the 120-tick slow-down with 4 presses, and the timeout kill, reset and move). In a trace the arena floor (object 0's ground patch) carries the hero down at speed 0.6, the hazard thread takes 1 HP near locator 0 and button 0's prompt is pressed while riding. Interpretations: the reset's ActivateObj(false) puts objects 0, 8, 11, 10, 9 and 7 back at time 0 (so the timeout does not fire again), the timeout moves the hero to entry 0 of the world's list (SPEC-0030 row 156; the 1089 section names object 0's locator 0; R-0083), and T0's callback is not re-entered while it runs. Not built: the time-sync helper of Update step 2 (no rule given; R-0083). `--start` inside the arena falls, because the start probe runs before Init poses object 0 (diagnostic only).
- **The Undead Draik's spit effects (SPEC-0029 Spit appearance):** each spit record now has its effects: fire flies with effect 528 and bursts with 991, ice with 527 and 992. On the release tick the flight effect starts at the mouth; each tick it is moved to the spit; when the spit hits the hero or reaches the ground the flight effect stops (its particles finish) and the impact starts there as a one-shot. `draik::spit_step` now returns the ended spits (record, last position, damage). Not applied: the Ixi Chief's height-check lock word (SPEC-0014 Height check), because its only effect is to switch off the characters' edge guard and navmesh restore (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14), which this build does not have yet.
- **Hero items and the drawn flags (SPEC-0046 Held items, focus hero, steps 1 and 2; SPEC-0040 Behavior 29; SPEC-0021 Weapon slot and Shield slot):** the party now keeps the weapon-drawn and shield-raised flags. Writers: an attack start (Tor's and Roberta's) and the L1 guard with a shield set both; a hit that is not a poison tick, with the weapon sheathed, draws the weapon when Tor has one and raises the shield when the hero has one; Square with nothing to interact with sheathes while drawn (and still sneaks while sheathed); states 25 and 28, a talk and the hero swap clear both. The focus hero's shield (resource 560 + s) is drawn at W5 while raised, else on the back with axes (-X8, Z8, Y8) at T8 scaled by 1.6 / |bx - sx|, and only for Tor; the weapon (resource 544 + w) at W0 while drawn, else sheathed with axes (Y6, Z6, X6) at T6 scaled by min(2.6 / |bz - sz|, 0.6), and Roberta's only while drawn; nothing while the cloak is worn. Checked in screenshots with sword 100 and shield 106: both on Tor's back. Not built: the sheathe and draw sounds (their cue ids are not given), the held entry (item 36) (the Mote glow models were added later), and the box values are the model's vertex bounds (SPEC-0004 says they match the root-node box).
- **Character attachment models (SPEC-0046 Second walk and attachment slots; SPEC-0004 role ids 10 + k):** a region character's model set children with role 10 to 20 are no longer merged into the body (they were drawn unposed at the character's root); they are kept as the set's attachment models by slot. Each drawn, active, living character now shows its slot 0 model in the hand (axes Z0, Y0, -X0 at T0), its slot 6 model at W5, and its slot 1 model while the clip's event bit 0x1000 is on and the selected attack's projectile type is below 5, at W0 to W4 by the clip's event bits. Checked in pack 986: the Dark Knights hold their swords. Not built: the thrown-model word (the Black Knight's flying sword and the Werelupe King's boomerang drawn in place of the hand model while they fly), the light state of slot 0, the pause word and character flag 42 gates.
- **Climb panels (SPEC-0033 Behavior 51 state 21, Behavior 22A, Climb terms, Panel hand-offs, Straddles, Top-edge hang; SPEC-0006 Type 10 links):** the type-10 reader now resolves each record's four neighbour links (584 on disc). `neopets_sim::climb` holds the wall-panel grab (type -2: facing and stick into the face below -0.8, -r to 1.25 r from the plane, the a and b margins by the links, -1.0 to LV - 1.0 up, stick above 0.5; from the ground and while jumping, with the grab cooldown 0) and state 21: the approach (the panel hold and clip 121), the free climb (the hold, steering 0.5, requests 56, 45, 52 and 53 at clip rate max(|value|, 0.3), 49 idle), the up hand-offs (same plane: the stacked straddle 13; a top face: the top-edge hang 17 with cooldown 20), the down hand-offs (same plane: 14; a top face below: step off), the side hand-offs (side by side: 15 and 16; corners 8, 9, 11 and 12 by the normals' cross product), the straddles' hand back to 2, the top-edge hang (r - 0.7 from the wall, height E - 0.25 - H + 0.1, the shuffles 60 and 61 with the 0.05 step onto a side neighbour), the climb over (59 to 0.95, then state 1) and Stop Climbing (label 18, any sub-mode but 7: state 7 in air phase 5 with request 67). The travel step now keeps the root step with the stick behind the facing in states 21, 23 and 41 (Behavior 14C), so the ladder's climb down also moves. In a trace in pack 986 the hero grabs wall 8, climbs on the clips' root motion, hangs at the top edge and climbs over onto the top (y 127.4; the top face is at 127.69). Not built: the ceiling grab and sub-mode 18, sub-modes 3 to 6 (an up or down hand-off that would enter them requests 49), sub-mode 10, the sideways moves inside a stacked straddle and the vertical ones inside a side-by-side straddle. Interpretations: the approach holds the hero on the panel and plays 121 to its end; a corner ends with the hero's position along the new panel clamped into [r, LU - r] (the spec's 0.75 and -1.0 step is not clear); the top-edge hang reads the stick when either value is non-zero (the spec says both; R-0085).
- **Reviews against SPEC-0007 (Order of work inside one tick) and SPEC-0061 (what the rewrite does not need):** the play tick matches SPEC-0007 in the camera running before the hero (item 4, one tick of lag), the projectile pool before the hero (item 7), the hero's update, motion and animation in that order (item 8), the trigger callbacks and the level Update after the hero (item 10), and the sound apply at the end (item 15). Known deviations, not changed: the region characters and bosses (item 6) run after the hero and the script step instead of before the hero, so in this build enemies see the hero of the current tick and a hit they land reaches the hero's update one tick later; the Charm Mote objects (item 8(d)) step before the hero's update rather than after its animation pass; the world tick's placed-object animation (item 5) runs after the hero. Reordering the tick is left for a supervised session because every character system depends on it. SPEC-0061: none of its leave-out entries is built here (no sidle, statue turn, door hold, Draik stages 2 and 4, Cloud Beast, Spyder Nest or debug watch screen), and its keep list is covered (sphere contact, camera shake, the sneak entry, the 1089, 1090 and 1381 hazards, the road-fight code 7, the hero shot trigger pass, the 989 post-fight block).
- **The character edge guard and the lock word (SPEC-0040 Behavior 14E; SPEC-0056 Lock value; SPEC-0014 Height check):** every walking character's motion tick now saves P0, adds the root step D, and when its lock word is clear and its ground gap is above 2.0 in magnitude puts it at P0 - D and skips the ground constraint for that tick, so NPCs and enemies do not walk off edges higher than 2.0. The lock word is one tick long (cleared at the end of each motion tick) and also skips the off-mesh restore. `ixi::height_lock` gives the Ixi Chief's lock (heights more than 1.0 apart, strictly; the spec's vectors are tests); the Ixi Chief's controller is not wired into play yet, so nothing sets the lock in play. Checked: the herd trace still pens all three Whinnies, and pack 986's Dark Knights still walk. Not covered: the guard's AI-mode conditions (not in AI mode 2, nor AI mode 1 with its timer expired), because this build does not keep that AI mode word.
- **Module 1381's block group (SPEC-0042 1381 Block group, Block group points and routines, Activate-world restore; R-0078 answered):** Init now turns each station's objects (50 + k, 38 + k) on and makes movable blocks 0 to 5 inactive, and registers the five routines per station in the spec's order (pad, target, area, goal, arm; goal indices 61, 1, 21, 29, 35, 50 and arm indices 62, 2, 22, 30, 36, 51). The arm routine stores "touching now" (state 1 or 2) in block k's armed word only for block k's own arm index; the goal routine reads only the block number and, with block k not done and armed, sets its done word and flag 714 + k and plays the piece scene (control held, camera track 0 waited, sound 9, objects 26 + k and 20 + k removed, the camera returned); the sixth piece also plays object 18 and removes object 19, once. The pads close once 714 to 719 are all set. The restore now activates block 0 too (object 50 stays) and marks restored pieces done. The module words are 60 + k (done), 70 + k (armed) and 80 (the six-piece scene), clear of the hazard thread's 30 to 45. 1 test (the station 0 push vector, another block's arm index ignored, the block 2 vector, the arm word cleared on leaving, the sixth piece and the closed pads, the restore). Disc trace (NEOPETS_TRACE_PUSH toward z -60, --flag 708): block 0 fires goal 61 from the 6th push and arm 62 on the 7th (tick 354), and flag 714 is set with camera track 0 on tick 355; without 708 blocks 0 to 5 report inactive. Not given, so not built: the scene's two waits (one tick each here), block 0's four attached effects, the sparkle effect. Unverified: the rest of the six pieces in play.
- **ItemActivate and module 1096's item dispensers (SPEC-0030 row 198 and the note on ItemActivate; SPEC-0021 Drop routes 3, Pickup dispatch, Drop and money flow steps 4 and 5; SPEC-0041 1096 CB7, CB8 and Item dispensers):** a new level action ItemActivate (the world form: slot, placement ordinal, snap, placed) adds the placement with that ordinal among the item placements of the region loaded in that slot, as a scripted add: the luck gate, then for keys 0 or more the equipment and quest-style vetoes and the key itself, and for a negative key the group's item roll alone (`items::item_roll`, steps 4 and 5; no money roll). The point follows the region-load rule for byte +0x21 and then the snap bool puts it on the ground; placed items never expire. The host keeps each slot's item placements when its region arrives. 1096: the arrival (flag 45 clear) enables the two dispensers; once the boss slot has arrived, CB7 runs them first each tick. Item 163 is kept at one of ordinals 0 to 5 and item 161 at one of 6 to 12: a dispenser with nothing placed puts its item at a placement other than the last one used, either when the hero holds none of it or 30 s (1800 ticks) after its last pickup, and the pickup callback starts that 30 s. 1 test (nothing before the slot arrives; both placed in their ranges; still held: again at 30 s and not at the last placement, for six draws; used up: again at once). Disc trace: region 1097 (slot 0) has 13 item placements, and items 163 and 161 are activated on tick 3. The spec's random-draw routine is not given: the draw is the game generator's value modulo the other placements. My reading of the rule's "or" is that the 30 s applies only after a pickup. Not built: SetTargetActive; the row 202 (placement-id) form; ItemDeactivate.

The trainer's talk and remarks are unchanged. The test is extended (nothing before the slot arrives, once after, not again). Disc trace (--flag 22, 24 and 169): slot 0 holds region 1059 with one item placement, and item 160 (the fire Mote that the lesson's next step checks for) is activated from it on tick 3. Module 1251's ItemActivate calls stay unbuilt: their item slot is not established (SPEC-0030 note, Medium).

- **Module 1091's hazards and plates (SPEC-0044 1091; SPEC-0057 patterns A and B):** the animated-trigger table is now keyed by host object and record (each host's records keep their own trigger state). Before, two hosts using the same record index would have overwritten each other. Each of objects 3 to 10 is its own group's record 0: on disc each of those objects has exactly one inline volume, index 0. It also registers the plate triggers 41 to 48 and the button trigger 51. Pattern A runs one run at a time per object: objects 7 and 8 kill (KillCurrentHero, 1 s, the hero to locator 6) and the others damage 2 then wait 0.5 s (the mode-0 slot flags stay 0 in play, as the spec says). Pattern B (triggers 43 to 48), one run at a time per trigger: inside, the plate object is scrubbed 0 to 10/30 s a tick at a time; the sweep ids are played at 5.0 with 0.02 s between; 2 damage and poison at the default; wait until the hero leaves; the plate scrubbed 13/30 to 25/30 s. Not built: the trigger 41 and 42 sequencers (which swing the hazard objects), the Init object setups (not listed in the spec), the slots, the navmesh table, the target flags, the Update sequence (Medium-Low). - **Farm quest chain check and the pump scene (SPEC-0049 quest rows 0 to 8; SPEC-0045 909):** new trace options. NEOPETS_TRACE_QUESTS=1 prints the quest rows (index, kind, start and done flags, stage, map point, title and objective). The characters line now prints each world item's position. It now waits the spec's 1 s after the fade in, starts 18 and 17 without waiting, and waits only for the 0.33 s object 20 before stopping it; line 166 now comes about 3 s after the fade. The plays' waits are Medium in the spec (asked as R-0092). - **The package delivery traced and a playtest guide (SPEC-0041 951; SPEC-0045 1023 trigger 17 and the chase; SPEC-0049 quest rows 8 to 15):** traces from pack 909 to pack 1148. Giving Hubert the pliers sets 116 to 119, and his scene gives control back at tick 575. The house (951) gives the chest key on arrival with 119 (120). The chest upstairs (group 0, kind 1, key 43) opens with it and gives item 98. Hubert downstairs with item 98 sets 121 and 122 and gives the package. The hero then holds two packages: the routine's give and line 59's own dialogue give record both run, as the specs say (asked as R-0093). The house door loads the road (691).
